Gaps: (1) "&", "and" and commas are not joining words on purpose, since kits list their own tools that way (DCK2100P2 "Combo Kit with Hammer Drill & Impact Driver"), so "…Kit (DCK240C2) & Circular Saw" stays probable (pinned by a test); (2) a tool inside a longer word ("Flashlight", "Vacuum") is not seen; (3) when no tool is named before the first joining word, what follows it is read as the kit's own; (4) a genuine kit naming its own battery by model after "with" becomes review. *For v2:* the kit's contents (its Home Depot page, or the catalog's kinds) would let the rule reject what it can prove is not in the box and see "&"/"and" additions; and once matching uses barcodes, a bundle page that reuses the kit's barcode would grade exact.
- **The audit loop** (step 8c; owner: "this scraper should be perfect and should have a way to catch and safely and accurately fix failures"). An LLM checks; it never decides in the product. Settings → Your data → Export for audit writes what the scan chose (bundles, parts with `isKit`, the chosen listing and grade), every saved listing, the matches, answers and open questions; never the key or settings. `npm run audit -- prepare` makes a blind file (no grade, no choice), a checker labels it with `docs/audit-checker.md`, and `score` writes the wrong matches and missed listings and adds every labelled (part, listing) pair to `fixtures/audit/corpus.json`. `core/test/match-corpus.test.ts` grades every pair with the scan's own `grade(wantedFor(…), row)`: a pair labelled not the part must never grade probable or better, one labelled the part must at least reach review; a pair that fails is marked `known` with its reason until fixed, and the test fails when a known pair starts passing, so its mark is removed. Seeded with the validation's 279 pairs; its first run found the kit-plus-extras failures above. Listings with an empty brand field are never saved, so a checker sees them only through match questions. An LLM inside the extension (suggesting Review answers) is held for v2: a second paid key and listing titles sent to another service are the PM's call.
- **A part's sell prices are a labelled list, and an ROI is a range** (step 9; owner, Nov 18: "see all the options for the sell price with labels … max ROI and the min ROI and the tool condition and at what seller … and home depot price too"; PO, Nov 18: New only, no used or refurbished, Renewed prices are Amazon liquidations, value unlisted parts by eBay). Only New prices count: `amazon_new` (the matched listing's featured price) and `amazon_buybox` (the Buy Box Verify reads from the listing's page, `purchase_options.buy_new`, or a one-seller page's `single_offer`), and only while the listing is active and the Buy Box is no older than the featured price. Amazon's list price and Home Depot's price for the part sold alone are `reference: true` and never enter an ROI. Renewed, used and the "lowest of N used & new offers" figure are never parsed or counted. Each way to sell shows lowest (each part at its lowest counted price, $0 where none), Amazon New (the estimate, unchanged) and highest. On the validation set all three are 1 of 31 positive: the range only opens where a part has a Buy Box. `ebay_sold_new` is reserved for eBay prices (waiting on the owner's eBay developer keys; the Browse API is the planned source).
- **Home Depot part prices are read free in the owner's browser and kept apart from splitting** (step 9; fix round after an Opus review). After the kit pages, the scan asks Home Depot's own search for each part (`readPartPrices`, at most 40 a scan, the same cap in the reader), takes a product whose model is the part's or its RYOBI B twin and whose page names the part's brand, and reads its price. Each reading says found, not_found or not_read: not_read (refused, or past the cap) saves nothing; not_found keeps an earlier found price and records only the check time; nothing is stored over a price with a null. Readings live in their own store (`partPrices`, IndexedDB version 2, an upgrade that only adds missing stores) and never create or edit a catalog entry, so splitting and kit reading never see them; a test proves the validation replay's catalog and splits are identical with and without them. *For v2:* when the first search link ending in the model is a kit, the part reads not_found until freshness expires.
- **A New listing in bulk packaging may be chosen** (step 9; PO, Nov 18: "some tools have listings named (bulk packaging) or state in the description that it is repackaged, so just look for listings that say New"). One test, `isBulkPackaging`: "bulk packed/packaged/packaging", "no-/non-retail packaging", "repackaged", "(Bulk)"; "Bulk" followed by anything else ("Bulk Pack", "Bulk 25-Pack") is a quantity for the pack rules. Packaging is not a grade, and `pick` ranks grade first; among the best grade a retail-boxed listing is chosen, a bulk one only when no retail one shows a price (and then one that shows a price). Renewed, refurbished, used and open-box stay rejected; Verify no longer sends a page to review for packaging alone. Kept on purpose: a RYOBI battery pack of n in bulk packaging is still rejected (a seller's pairing of loose units, not RYOBI's pack). On the recordings P102 and P209DCN now pick bulk listings; the 216 pairs and the validation replay are unchanged. The step 8b entry's "never applies … to a bulk pack" now reads with this rule.
- **eBay data: prices and sold counts only, never eBay users' data** (step 9, Nov 19). The owner's production keyset is exempt from eBay's marketplace account-deletion notifications on the grounds that Split Finder stores no eBay user data, so nothing from eBay that names a person is kept: no seller usernames, feedback or buyer details, in the store, the cache, fixtures or exports. Recorded responses have `seller` removed. First test call (production Browse API, `item_summary/search`, New, fixed price, "RYOBI PCL206B"): 47 active listings; the search mixes the tool with accessories for it (cases, bit holders), so a title naming the model is not enough. `getItem` gives `estimatedAvailabilities[].estimatedSoldQuantity` (13 on the first listing): the units that listing has sold, not sold prices. Browse returns active listings only; sold prices need the Marketplace Insights API, which eBay grants on application.
- **The listing judge: the model proposes, code verifies** (step 10, Task 2; owner, Nov 19: "build it so its model agnostic. lest do claude first"). `core/src/judge/`: a provider-neutral `ListingJudge` returns the model's answer text or a typed `not judged` (auth, rate_limited, overloaded, server, bad_request, network, aborted, refusal, max_tokens, …), never throwing; `verifyJudgement` decides what the answer is worth; the Claude adapter (`claude.ts`) is one file, so a second provider is one more file. Rules: (1) every quote, after case, spacing and typographic-mark normalisation only, must sit inside one of the title, one item specific ("Name: value"), or the description; a quote spanning two places, an empty or invented quote makes the answer unsure, whatever its verdict; a match or not_match with no quote is unsure. (2) The judged thing is the product sold under the part's model number, not always a bare tool (fix round 1: the validation set holds batteries, chargers, combo kits, starter kits and bit sets). The part's name (Home Depot's title) says what the product includes: a kit's batteries, charger and bag, a 2-pack's two batteries, a bit set's pieces are the product, not extras. A match needs an identity quote containing the part's model or twin as a whole model number (PCL206 is not found in PCL206B or PCL206K1); at least one `contents` quote, and the contents quotes between them must name each battery, charger, bag or case the part's name includes (a name saying "Tool Only", "not included" or "sold separately" asks for none; "charger not included" does not name a charger); `contentsMatch` true, `quantity` 1, `condition` new, no `extras`, no `missing`, no quote offered as extra, missing or not the product; and eBay's condition id 1000 when known. Without a part name the model number alone defines the product, so a match still needs a contents quote: the listing must state what it includes, or the answer is unsure. (3) Packaging is not identity (PO, step 9 bulk entry): a New kit pull, a bulk-packed or repackaged item is the product when its contents are, so PCL660 "Bare Tool Pulled from a Kit" and DCS334B "taken out of a kit" with its blade can match. A tool-only product still includes what its maker ships with the tool, so PCL500B "TOOL ONLY No Blades" is missing the saw's blade. (4) Title checks, from the real searches, only lower a match: "for", "fits" or "compatible" before the model ("Hard Case for … PCL206B", "For DEWALT DCB205 … Drill"); a pack or count ("2 Pk", "2Pack", "lot of") unless the part's name states the same count ("65-Piece" for A986501, "(2-Pack)" for PBP2003, matched by number and unit, so "pack of 2" is "2-Pack" and "3-Pack" is not); and "no" or "without" followed by anything but box, retail, packaging, tag, battery or charger ("No Blades"), with battery and charger also caught when the product includes them. Every check only lowers a verdict. (5) **Kit ruling** (fix round 2, controller's ruling after an adversarial review; the owner may overrule): a listing that names the exact kit model (an identity quote with the model as a whole token, not "pulled from a … kit" phrasing), with no `missing` quote and no denial anywhere in its text ("no charger", "charger … not included / sold separately", "tool only", "battery only", "bare tool", "Battery Included: No"), sells the kit: the model number stands for the kit's contents, and the contents quotes need not name each battery, charger and bag. `contentsMatch` must still be true and `missing` empty. Why: 22 of the 138 labelled matches are kits whose listings name the model and little else (DCK225D2 860158654414, DCK240C2 800845491870), and the word check lost every one. A kit title stating a battery count other than the name's ("w/ 1 Li-Ion Battery" for PCL1600K2's two) is unsure; the name's count is "(n)" summed, or else the larger of its singular "Battery" words and its Ah figures. (6) More cheap checks from the review and the labels, each only lowering a match: a quote cut short before "(which are) not included / sold separately / seperately / not incl", or just after "no", does not count as identity or contents, and a negation after a list ("battery, charger and bag are not included") names none of them; a pack suffix after the model ("DCB205-2") unless the part states that count (DCB203-2's own suffix does); "compatible", "fits", "replacement … for", "for DeWalt/Ryobi", "motor assembly", "gearbox" anywhere in the title; "open box", "untested", "used" (not "never used" or "unused"), "refurbished", "reconditioned", "for parts", "parts only", "as is" in the title (eBay's condition id is 1000 on every recorded listing, so it never caught these); a brand that is a placeholder ("Unbranded", "Does not apply", "BRANDED") or does not contain the part's brand (TTI counts for RYOBI); an MPN or model specific naming the same letters with other digits (DCF911B on DCF921B; PCL206K1 on PCL206 and DCS391BR on DCS391B do not lower); a multi-variation listing (item id `v1|…|<variation>` not 0); a title naming a battery, charger, kit, combo, bundle or carry bag the part's name does not include ("Impact Driver & 1.5 Ah Battery", "Glue Gun with Battery and PCG002 Charger"; "Cordless Battery Jigsaw" and "Battery Powered" do not count); and a tool-only product whose item specifics say "Battery Included: Yes" or a set including a charger. Measured with a perfect synthetic answer on each labelled listing: all 138 labelled matches still verify; of the 79 labelled not_match or unsure, a dishonest clean match is now caught on 63 (was 31). Left to the judge: clone listings with copied text, a grinder sold with the battery, a kit with only "what is pictured", PCL1600K2X. Not used, because the labels disagree: "Set Includes: Battery" alone (on PCL235 147276605796, a labelled match, and 366569621360, labelled unsure, with identical specifics) and "ONE+ HP" (PCL515 920158666573 is labelled unsure for PCL515 and match for PCL515B). The checks are words, not understanding; the eval measures the rest. Malformed output is unsure; a failed call has no verdict. The prompt treats the listing as untrusted data and seller text cannot write the message's own tags; it asks for unsure when the listing contradicts itself about what is included (the first real listing says both "Battery Included: No" and "Set Includes: Battery, tool only"). The price is never shown to the model: it is not evidence of identity, and a judge that rejected cheap listings would bias the very value it feeds. Claude call: plain `fetch` to the fixed `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` (no base-URL option, so the key cannot be sent elsewhere), `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, the browser-access header, `output_config.format` JSON schema (shape confirmed in the structured-outputs doc), `max_tokens` 2000 (raised from 1000 in fix round 2, so low-effort thinking does not end a call early; a cut-off answer is not judged) and effort `low` (thinking cannot be turned off on these models and counts against `max_tokens`; the eval may raise both), no temperature, no thinking budget, no retries. Models offered: `claude-haiku-5-5` (Settings' starting choice, the cheapest) and `claude-opus-5-5`. `PROMPT_VERSION` keys the judgement cache (`listing-judge-2026-11-19.2` after fix round 1, `listing-judge-2026-11-20.3` after fix round 3). (7) **Fix round 3, after the first real run** (Haiku 5.5 on prompt .2, `fixtures/ebay/judgements-claude-haiku-5-5.json`: 2 false matches, 59 of 138 matches accepted, 41% unsure). The kit ruling lived only in the code, so the model left 72 labelled matches unsure, mostly kits naming their model whose listing "does not state the charger or bag", and many said "condition New is not stated" because eBay's condition field was never shown. The prompt now states the ruling (the model number stands for the product's contents; answer match unless the listing says a piece is missing, added or different, or it is a tool pulled from a different kit; never ask the seller to list every piece; maker-packed small pieces such as belt hooks are not extras), names the signs of a copy or clone (units the maker does not use such as N·m, "Origin: Mainland China" on a US-brand tool, "customized") as unsure, says the price is never evidence, and gives eBay's condition field in its own `<ebay_condition>` block outside the seller's text (seller text cannot write that tag). A model number with a run-on letter in the seller's own words, title or description ("Model: PCL1600K2X" on PCL1600K2 800870140728, one of the two false matches) now lowers a match in code too; the RYOBI twin and kit suffixes are exempt, and item specifics are not read because DCS391BR sits beside DCS391B among the MPNs of a labelled match. Labels after it: 138 of 138 matches verify with a perfect answer, 64 of 79 non-matches caught by code. The other false match (DCF921B 407209694810, a clone-looking listing) is left to the prompt's clone signs: its words give no cheap check that a labelled match would not also trip. `npm run judge:eval` gained `--sample <n>` (n labels evenly spaced over model then item id, the same every time, said in the dry run, the score and a replay) and `--max-cost <usd>` (stops before the next call once the run's cost reaches it, writes the file marked incomplete, scores only what was judged). *For v2:* the API also offers version `2026-10-21`; nothing this call sends changes under it.
- **eBay candidates: what the pre-filter drops, and the answer key** (step 10, Tasks 1, 3, 4). The search is "<BRAND> <MODEL>" in Power Tools (3247), New, fixed price, up to 200; the pre-filter only drops (not New, not USD, no price, another brand, the model or its RYOBI twin not named as a whole token, outside 3247) and keeps the first 8 in eBay's order. Both drop rules are loss-only: a real listing outside 3247 or with a brand written like "Ryobi Tools" is lost, never accepted. On the recording the cap of 8 hid 680 listings that name the model (DCB205 alone has 1,377 results), so a part's eBay value rests on eBay's best-match order, not on the best-selling listings; for v2, sort or page by sales. Listing descriptions are seller free text and can name a store or a person: the judge reads them, nothing but its verdict and reasons is stored, and fixtures keep descriptions with `seller` fields removed. The answer key (fixtures/ebay/labels.json, 217 listings: 138 match, 59 not_match, 20 unsure) was drafted by three independent Opus readings with verbatim quotes under the PO's packaging ruling; it stays `draft` until the owner checks a sample. Two labels contradict each other (PCL235 147276605796 vs 366569621360; 920158666573 unsure for PCL515, match for PCL515B) and wait for the owner.
- **Which model judges eBay listings: Opus 5.5 recommended, Haiku 5.5 the cheap option** (step 10, Nov 19–20; owner approved spending, then "$300 of credit"). Real runs on the 217 labelled listings, prompt listing-judge-2026-11-20.3: Opus 5.5 found 124 of 138 labelled matches (90%), sent 17% to Review, 2 false matches (DCF921B 407209694810, a clone-looking listing at $64.99; and 920158666573, labelled unsure for PCL515 but match for PCL515B, a label conflict), $3.91 a full pass (≈ $0.018 a listing, ≈ $1.08 a scan of 60). Haiku 5.5: 80 of 138 (58%), 35% to Review, 5 false matches, $0.117 a pass. With prompt .2 Haiku found 59 of 138: the kit ruling had lived only in the code, and stating it in the prompt (plus eBay's condition field) raised recall. Neither model meets the reliability bar (zero false matches, 30 owner-checked labels), so eBay stays a reference, not counted. The Settings default is still Haiku; the owner picks Opus there. Judgement files: fixtures/ebay/judgements-*.json (replayed by `npm run judge:eval -- --replay`).
- **The review assistant drafts, code checks, the owner decides** (step 11, A1; owner, Nov 20: "human reviews what the AI decided before approving"). `core/src/assist/` returns a `Suggestion`, never a `ReviewAnswer`; nothing in it writes to the store. (1) *Evidence is stored data only:* a split question gets the bundle's own record (title, Home Depot's model number, its page's specification lines and description cut at 4,000 characters) and the newest stored page of each kit its readings name; a match question gets each candidate's title and, when the listing is stored, Amazon's brand field. Prices are never shown (not evidence, as for the listing judge). The tool's own reasons go in as notes, not sources, so they cannot be quoted back as evidence. (2) *What "verified" means:* every quote is found word for word, normalised as the listing judge normalises, inside one line of the source it names; the choice is one the question offers; a split choice has quotes from the bundle's own Includes or specification lines or description naming every part of the reading (any written form by `modelForms`), because a kit's page says what the kit holds, not what the bundle holds, and the bundle's title and model number are what the splitter read to ask the question (controller ruling, fix round 1: quoting them back is circular; PCL1600K2-P553, whose page has no Includes lines, and PSK1212SB-PCL265B, whose Includes name the starter kit's batteries and charger, are unverified); a match has a quote from the chosen listing naming the part, and a title with "for/fits/compatible/replacement" before the model, or "renewed/refurbished/used/open box", is unverified. It means grounded, not right: the owner still approves. A "none" is never verified (fix round 2, controller ruling): it comes back as its own status, `none_suggested`, with the model's reason and only the quotes that were found, and the panel shows it as the AI's view with nothing preselected, as it does cannot_tell. One found quote shows what a page says, not that nothing fits: on the real runs Haiku 5.5 gave four verified "none" answers the key disagrees with (P553 and PCL406B, whose labels could not settle them; 321809549 and 341644756, where it did not know that the kit model PCL1600K2 stands for the six tools the page lists). The prompt now says a kit's model stands for its contents and that not knowing what a model holds is cannot_tell, not none. `SuggestedAnswer` keeps its `{ decision: 'none' }` member so code written against it compiles, but it is no longer produced. cannot_tell passes through with only the quotes that were found. (3) *Text written to an AI:* page lines matching `looksLikeInstruction` (instructions to ignore rules, "you are an assistant", role prefixes, the answer's JSON keys, the prompt's own tags) are shown as data but never count as quotes, and while any is present no choice or none is verified. The patterns fire on none of the recorded Home Depot pages, Amazon titles and 217 eBay listings (a test keeps it so). The prompt also defuses its own tags in page text. (4) *No call* is made for an answered question, a split with no readings (the owner types parts; the assistant never proposes parts that are not offered), or a match with no candidates. (5) *Transport:* the Claude call is shared with the judge (`callClaudeMessages`, factored out of `ClaudeJudge` with its behaviour and messages unchanged), so failures are the same typed reasons (auth, rate_limited, refusal, max_tokens, …) and come back as `not_suggested`. Prompt version `review-assist-2026-11-20.3`. *For v2:* measure agreement with the owner's approvals (A2 records `{source: 'ai', agreed}`) before trusting any suggestion more. (6) *The eval* (`npm run assist:eval -- --model <id> --dry-run | --run | --replay [--max-cost <usd>]`, fix round 1) asks about the validation replay's 26 questions: the 19 split questions the answer key answered, reopened (truth: the reading with the key's parts, else none), and the 7 open match questions (truth: the checker's label for the part; a label with no correct ASIN but uncertain ones is "uncertain", where any verified answer counts as wrong). It writes `fixtures/assist/suggestions-<model>.json` with each call's answer text and usage, and a replay checks those answers again with today's code. The bar is zero verified-but-wrong; it is necessary, not sufficient, since the owner approves every answer anyway. A none_suggested is never right or wrong there; the eval lists the ones the key disagrees with, to watch. Replayed with today's code (answers given to prompt .2, Nov 20): Opus 5.5 3 verified and correct, 0 wrong, 20 cannot tell, 1 none (the key agrees); Haiku 5.5 1 correct, 0 wrong, 17 cannot tell, 6 none (the key disagrees with 5). The 2 split questions with no readings are not asked.
- **The research agent proposes parts, code checks them against what it read, the owner decides** (step 12, R1; owner, Nov 20: "fully automatic with human gated approval"). `core/src/research/`: `researchQuestion` is a manual tool-use loop over the judge's transport (`sendClaudeMessages`, factored out of `callClaudeMessages` with the judge's behaviour unchanged). (1) *Tools:* three client tools the extension runs (`read_homedepot_page`, `search_homedepot`, `catalog_lookup`; contracts in `types.ts`, never a SerpApi search, never Amazon) and the server tool `web_search_20260209` with `allowed_domains` ["ryobitools.com", "dewalt.com"], `max_uses` 2 and `allowed_callers` ["direct"]: called directly, its results come back as plain `web_search_tool_result` blocks the ledger can read, and `max_uses` counts every search (with dynamic filtering the search runs inside code execution, its results are not in the response and the limit counts differently; web search tool and server tools pages). (2) *The answer is a strict tool's input* (`submit_answer`), not `output_config.format`: citations are always on with web search, and the structured-outputs page says citations with `output_config.format` is a 400. A plain-text JSON answer is read too, never trusted more. (3) *The ledger* holds every source the model was shown, line by line: the stored evidence first (as Ask AI gathers it), then every tool result and every brand-site result (by its address, the only name the model sees for it). A brand-site page's text reaches the model encrypted, so only its title and the passages the model cited can be quoted. Lines that read as written to an AI are suspect: shown, never evidence, and while one is in the ledger nothing is verified. (4) *Verified parts* (`verifyResearch`, only lowers): every quote found in one line of its source; every part model real (catalog, a Home Depot page's or search row's model field, or a quoted brand-site passage, of the bundle's brand; sameModel, so PCL500 and PCL500B are one tool); every part named by its own quote; the bundle's own words covered: a tool count from its title, Includes list or "Number of Tools" row equals the parts' tools; "Tools Only", "Battery and charger sold separately", "Number of Total Batteries Included: 0" or "Charger Not Included" allow no battery or charger part; every item of every Includes line of the bundle's own page is accounted for (an item with a model is that model's, a kit's items are those its own page lists, any other item only by a quote of it under a part, and a tool, battery or charger item only by a part whose quote naming its model says that kind: "18V Jig Saw" by "RYOBI PCL525B (item 318898528): ONE+ 18V Cordless Jig Saw (Tool Only)"), and every part accounts for one. Without an Includes list a stated tool count is required. The spec row "Included" is not read as a list: on the recorded pages it is a category ("Blade(s)", "Tool Only"). Kinds are KIND_WORDS; accessories (blades, bits, wrenches, a "65-Piece" set) are never a tool. A choose answer goes through Ask AI's `verifySuggestion`; none is `none_suggested`, never verified. Known false negatives, by design: "6-Tool Combo Kit … and Miter Saw" (the count is the kit's, the parts hold 7), a 2-pack battery for "(2) PBP003" (another model). (5) *Limits a question:* 8 tool calls (client calls and web searches together), 2 web searches, 60 s, max_tokens 4000, 12 requests; a limit, Stop, a failed call or a refusal is `not_suggested` with the cost so far; no retries. Cost is tokens at the model's rates plus $0.01 a web search; the button estimate assumes 16,000 tokens in, 1,500 out and one search. (6) `SuggestedAnswer` gains `{decision: 'parts', parts}` (each model repeated by its quantity, as a typed parts answer) and the unverified suggestion `researchClaim`, both additive. (7) *The eval* (`npm run research:eval -- --model <id> --dry-run | --run --max-cost <usd> | --replay`) asks the 19 split questions of the validation replay with tools that answer only from recorded files (`scripts/research-tools.ts`), and writes every model response for a replay. Prompt version `review-research-2026-11-20.1`. *For v2:* a cheap check that a kit part's page lists the accessories quoted under it; measure the owner's agreement before trusting more.
- **Research, fix round 1, after the first real run** (Opus 5.5, prompt .1, 19 questions, $3.09, `fixtures/research/runs-claude-opus-5-5.json`: 1 verified and correct, 0 wrong, 11 unverified, 7 cannot tell, 12 web searches). Two causes, each fixed. (1) *The eval's search only knew recorded query strings:* the model's own searches ("RYOBI ONE+ 18V Jig Saw Tool Only PCL525B") all answered "not recorded", so it turned to the brand sites. A search not recorded word for word now answers from the recorded index, every product a recorded search or page shows (the Nov 20 searches and pages, the validation pages and kit pages, and the validation set's part searches in hd-part-prices). A model number in the query that matches a product's model as a whole token, in any written form, ranks first; then come the products whose titles hold the most of the query's words, at least two, within the brand the query names; at most 10. These results are labelled "Home Depot search (recorded index)", the score lists each tool call with its source, and a run now keeps every tool result, so a replay shows the model the same results it answered. The index is not Home Depot's ranking: a real search may rank differently or find more. Pages and searches of research-*.json are read with the extension's own readers (`parsePageState`: descriptive bullets, not highlights). (2) *Brand-site quotes could never verify:* web search gives a page's text encrypted, so the model quoted text no ledger line held. The server tool `web_fetch_20260209` is now offered: `allowed_domains` ryobitools.com and dewalt.com (subdomains included, server tools page), `max_uses` 2, `max_content_tokens` 6000, `allowed_callers` ["direct"]. Citations are left off, the default, which does not conflict with the strict submit tool. A fetched page's plain text enters the ledger one line per line, joined to the search result with the same address, and a quote from a brand page verifies only against that text or the result's title. The API fetches only addresses already in the conversation (web fetch tool page). A PDF comes back as base64 and is not read. Web fetches count toward the 8 tool calls and are free beyond their tokens. A brand-site source id is matched as an address (host case aside). The prompt (`review-research-2026-11-20.2`) says to search Home Depot first, with the model number or the brand and the item as the page names it; to open the bundle's own page first; to use the brand sites only when Home Depot does not settle the question; and to quote only words from a tool result. Also fixed: parseIncludes leaves a joining "and" as its own piece before "(n)" ("Charger, and (2) Tool Bags"), and verification no longer counts it as a leftover item. The button estimate is now measured: 33,400 tokens in, 1,100 out and 0.6 searches a question on the run, rounded up for one fetched page to 40,000 in, 1,200 out and 1 search, about $0.20 on Opus and $0.02 on Haiku. (3) *API version:* the run sent `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` with `web_search_20260209` and got search results back (12 searches, every response read). Web search needs no newer version. Web fetch under 2023-06-01 is not yet confirmed by a run.
- **Research, fix round 2, after the second run** (Opus 5.5, prompt .2, 19 questions, $1.83, kept as `fixtures/research/runs-claude-opus-5-5-prompt2.json`: 4 verified and correct, 1 verified but wrong, 12 unverified, 2 cannot tell; no web search or fetch). (1) *A bundle is not a part* (must fix). On 344304336 the answer PSK1212SB + PCL265B verified; the key gives PBP004 + PBP003 + PCG002 + PCL265B, as it splits PSK1212SB itself (339206615). The rule is the splitter's, from its own sources. A manufacturer kit is one part when the catalog marks it a kit (`CatalogRecord.isKit`, which `decomposeRyobi` resolves as one part: PCL1600K2, PCL1800K3N, the DCK kits). Any other proposed part whose own page in the ledger has an Includes list naming other model numbers (the `kitContents` criterion) is itself a bundle: unverified, with those models named. A starter kit whose page names no model, like PSK0114 (a part in the key for 344285193), stays one part. No list of model prefixes is used. The wording "a battery-and-charger starter kit is not a part" would have split PSK0114 against the key, so it was not used. (2) *A kit part accounts for what its own title and page state.* A kit part accounts for a generic Includes item only with counts agreeing: "6 Tools" for a 6-Tool kit; "2 Batteries" when its page's battery items, or else `kitBatteryCount` of its title, make two; "Charger" and "Bag" when it holds one; "Accessories" when its page lists some; a named tool of a kind its page holds. Any other item does not count. So a kit can no longer take a separate set's bits, and that set stays a leftover. A quote of a whole Includes list claims each of its items the part can be, since the runs quote lists whole. A quote that spans two lists in one bullet ("... : Includes: ...") claims none. A quote from the part's own page names it. With no Includes list, the bundle's own title and lines, quoted or not, must show each part: its model, a kit's "6-Tool", a set's "65-Piece", or its kind of tool. A battery count in the title must equal the parts' count. In both cases, a piece-counted set or a kind of tool the title names that no part is or holds is a leftover. (3) *"6-Tool Combo Kit … and Miter Saw"* (320243741) stays unverified: the title's count is the kit's, and the parts hold 7. Rule 2 does not change that. (4) *Prompt* `review-research-2026-11-20.3` states both rules. Replaying run 2 with today's code: 9 verified and correct, 0 wrong, 8 unverified, 2 cannot tell. (5) *Prompt caching:* a breakpoint on the system prompt (the tools render before it) and top-level automatic caching of the growing conversation, per the prompt caching guide. Cost counts cache writes at 1.25× input and reads at the model's rate: Opus 5.5 $0.20 a million, Haiku 5.5 $0.01. Simulated on run 2's 53 requests, the 19 questions cost $1.07 instead of $1.83. The button estimate is now 8,000 tokens written, 14,000 read, 1,000 out and one search a question: about $0.08 on Opus, $0.02 on Haiku. Caching under `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` with raw fetch is not yet confirmed by a run; `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` will show it.
- **Research, fix round 3, from the whole-step code review** (run 3: Opus 5.5, prompt .3, $0.60, 10 correct, 0 wrong). Run 3 confirmed that caching works under 2023-06-01: 338,174 tokens were read from the cache and 36,790 written. (1) *A word-only Includes item needs its model tied to the bundle* (critical). On the real PCL500525SB ledger, any RYOBI jig saw verified for "18V Jig Saw" (PBLJS01B, PSBJS01B). Now a part that is not a kit accounts for a tool, battery or charger named in words only when it is tied to the bundle. Either the bundle's own page names the model, or a listing of the same bundle is quoted under the part and names the part in its model number. "The same bundle" means the same tool count, the same named tools and the same Tools Only: Home Depot's "PCL500B-PCL525B", item 324616618, a search row or its page. The tie is required even when the ledger shows only one model of the kind. The review's wording asked for it only when the ledger shows several. But run 3 searched each part by its own number ("RYOBI PCL525B"), so its ledger held one row each and could never show the others; the recorded "ryobi jig saw tool only" search holds five RYOBI jig saws. Without a tie the message says the item names no model; with other models seen it says "Several RYOBI jig saws fit '18V Jig Saw'; nothing ties PCL525B to this bundle." Of the review's option (c), only the lowering half is used: a part whose own title says brushless, where the bundle's spec says "Motor Type: Brushed", is unverified. Other attributes are not used to exclude competitors: a title that leaves out "Brushless" or "Compact" proves nothing, and excluding on it would raise a verdict. The prompt (`review-research-2026-11-20.4`) asks for the tie: search Home Depot for the bundle's title and quote the sibling listing under each part it names, and answer cannot_tell without one. Replaying run 3: 9 correct, 0 wrong. 328690510 (PCL500B + PCL525B, right) is now unverified, because the model found both by their own numbers and quoted nothing that ties them. (2) *A combo is one part only as a catalog kit* (risk 7). A part of two or more tools that the catalog does not mark a kit is unverified: Home Depot's combo SKUs PCL500525SB and PCL500B-PCL525B, whose pages name their tools in words or by model. PSBCK102K2, a part in the key, is not marked a kit by `catalogEntry` (its kit pattern covers DCK, PCL…K and P…K), so it stays unverified, a false negative of the splitter's catalog. (3) *Web search and fetch limits were already per question.* `max_uses` is counted per turn. A request whose last message holds only tool_result blocks, or that resumes a pause_turn, continues the turn (web search and web fetch tool pages), and the loop's continuations are always those, so the API counts earlier requests' uses. The loop also stops itself at 2 of each. The tools are not edited per request: removing or changing a server tool mid-turn can be refused (server tools page: keep the same tools) and would rewrite the cached prefix. A test holds both. (4) *The eval's index leaves out the validation part price lookups*, which were searched by the true parts' numbers. Replaying run 3 with the results it kept gives 9 correct. With today's tools and no part price lookups (`--replay --fresh-tools`) it gives 1, because most parts the model found by number were recorded only by those lookups. With them (`--with-part-prices`) it gives 9. The eval without them is pessimistic, since the owner's real Home Depot search would find those parts. With them it is optimistic. Recording Home Depot searches for the parts' kinds and the bundles' titles would make it fair.
- **Research reads Home Depot's highlights, and an Includes item joining two products is two items** (step 12, after R3b; the PO: Includes lists are the source of parts, with no leftovers). (1) `parsePageState` gives `details.highlights` first, then the descriptive bullets (a highlight that is also a bullet once), so a research page read holds lines like 328690510's "Includes: 18V ONE+ 5-1/2 in. Circular Saw and 18V ONE+ Jig Saw". `parseBundlePage` and the scan's splitter are unchanged. Fix round 1's "descriptive bullets, not highlights" is superseded. (2) An Includes item with no model number and a count of 1 that joins pieces with "and" or "&" is one item per piece, only when every piece has a kind word of its own (`kindsOf`). "1/4" and 3/8" Extended Reach Ratchets" (a size before the "and") and "(20) Black Oxide Drill and Drive Bits" (no product after it; and a count) stay one item. A model or count is never shared out between pieces: which piece it belongs to would be a guess. (3) A product named again on another Includes list of the same page (the same model number, or the same words and count) is one item on both lists: accounted for once, a quote of it does not span the two, and each list's quantity check counts it. (4) A kit's own page counts its batteries once: battery items that all carry a model, none on two lists, are summed as before; otherwise each list that names batteries counts them, and the lists must agree or the count is unknown. PCL1600K2's page (317987591) names "2 Batteries" in a highlight and PBP005 and PBP002 in a bullet: two, not four. So the true PCL500B + PCL525B answer must also quote each highlight half under its part (the sibling tie still required); the fake answers in the tests and e2e/ask-all.spec.ts do. The replay eval (`--replay --fresh-tools --with-part-prices`) scores the same before and after (6 correct, 0 wrong, 9 unverified, 4 cannot tell): no replayed question read a page with highlights. *Known false negative:* on PBLRC2501SB (328814689) the one ratchets item, quoted under both PBLRC01B and PBLRC25B, reads as one item given to two parts. *For v2:* split an item with a model number when its second piece starts with one.
- **Step 15, decision 4: fewer split questions, each rule measured before it was adopted** (the owner's second real scan left 15 "Which parts" questions, all "AI unsure", 0 estimates; fixtures/e2e-2026-11-23). `scripts/measure-splits.ts` (free, no network) replays (a) the validation set against its answer key, with Home Depot's Nov 18 part prices as the readings; (b) the owner's real store: which rule settles each of the 15, and every already-settled bundle run through each rule as if it were open (its answer must be the split that stands, or none); (c) the four recorded research runs through today's verifier. A rule is adopted only with zero wrong answers on all three and on the unit tests' real examples. The rules live in `core/src/scan/settle.ts`, run by `readSplit` after the model number and the page (so the scan, a page visit and a reopened question read the same split); each settled split keeps `evidence` (`{kind: 'bogo_pair' | 'hd_part_page' | 'page_quote', text, url}`), shown on the deal as `DealDetail.splitEvidence`.
- **Rule 1, adopted: a BOGO pair's split is its starter and its free item.** A bundle the pair rule matched (`findBogoPairs`: one starter with exactly one BOGO promotion, `<starter><joiner><free>`, the same price to the cent) is `[starter, free]`. The pair's facts now ride on its bundle record (`BundleRecord.bogo`, written by `pairBundleRecord`; at start the agent puts them on records written before). The free item's model is Home Depot's text after the joiner, or the one longer model the pair's own page names that ends or starts with it (a DEWALT short code: `921B` is the `DCF921B` its page names, quoted as `page_quote`); a DEWALT short code its page does not spell out, two such models, or a page/model-number disagreement in the notes leaves the split as it was. This refines "the free model ... never a model worked out from it" above: nothing is worked out, a page names it. *Measured:* 6 of the 15 questions settled (PCL1201K2 with PCL430B, P20015BTL, FVC51K, FVD50K, FVF51K, FVL51K); on the 38 settled bundles, every pair rule 1 resolves gives the split that stands (DEWALT 921B, B606, CH172B, 1404, 722B ... all to the model the page and the catalog already gave); 0 wrong. Validation set, with the pairs the Nov 23 BOGO recording lists for its item ids: PCL1201K2-PCL430B settled as the key; PCL1201K2-PCL515B, DCK200D1P1W921B and DCK200D1P1WB606, already settled, give the same split; 0 wrong.
- **Rule 2, adopted: a hyphen or kit+tool split is settled when Home Depot sells every part under exactly that model, and waits for the readings otherwise** (`homeDepotSplit`). Only the one reading the model number spells out: a RYOBI `A-B` (exactly the hyphen's pieces) or a DEWALT kit joined to a whole tool model; never a battery pack + code (`PACK_WITH_NOTE`), never a reading the page offered (Ruling 76), never a part ending in SB (a special buy is a bundle, not a part). Each part is confirmed by a found part-price reading whose page's model is exactly that part's (a reading of its twin form is "Home Depot sells PCL206B, not PCL206": a question), or by a catalog kit whose own page was read; a part whose Home Depot title counts two or more tools and is no catalog kit is refused. The bundle's page must not contradict: every model it names (RYOBI on its Includes bullets, DEWALT anywhere) is a part, the part's tool written without its kit or tool-only ending (`FVC51` for `FVC51K`, `P20015` for `P20015BTL`), or an item of a part kit's own read page; this is stricter than "another model of that tool type", since a page's kind of a named model is often not readable and any other model is a reason to ask. A tool count it states must fit (rule 3, or the parts' tools adding up to it), and Tools Only allows no battery or charger part. *Waiting:* a part with no reading yet, or a part kit whose page is unread while the page names models it may explain, makes the split wait (`Decomposition.waitingFor`; `BundleRow.waitingParts`, `DealRow.matchState`/`matchKind` `waiting_parts`, "Waiting for Home Depot's part pages", `needsAnswer: false`) in a run that waits for pages (the agent's `waitForPages`); the parts go into the part-price read and the kit into the kit-page read, and after the part prices are read the waiting splits are read again (`splitWaiting`). A question opens only when a reading came back not found ("Home Depot's search found no P553 sold by itself"), for another model, or the page contradicts. Elsewhere (the command line, the extension's panel) such a split is a question as before. At start the agent reads every open question again under rules 1 and 2 and withdraws the ones settled or now waiting (`withdrawUnreadSplitQuestions`); answered questions and Autopilot/Undo records are untouched. *Measured:* validation 16 right / 0 wrong / 15 open before, 24 / 0 / 7 after (PCL1600K2 with A986501, A963001, A971502, A989504; PCL1800K3N with A967002, A964001; PCL1201K2-PCL430B; PSK0114-PCL265B); its settled bundles probed give their split. Real store: of the 9 questions rule 1 leaves, 5 now wait (A986501, P553, A98401, PBP003; DCK240C2DCS380B for its kit page and DCS380B), 2 stay with a reason (PBP2003: the page names (2) PBP003; PSK005: the page names PBP006), 2 are untouched (DCKTS681D1P1W34's code `34`; PCL500525SB, no pattern); with Nov 18's readings 3 of the 5 would settle. 0 wrong. Known false negatives, by design: PCL1600K2-PBP2003 (the key's answer; the page's "(2) PBP003" is another model), PSBCK102K2-A967002 (its kit's title counts no tools).
- **Rule 3, adopted: "<N>-Tool Combo Kit … (and|with|w/|&) <extra>" on a kit + extra model counts the kit half** (`kitHalfCount`, `kitHalfOf` in research/verify.ts; also rule 2's count). The verifier's "The bundle states N tools; the parts hold M" does not fire when the model is a kit + extra (RYOBI `<kit>-<extra>`, DEWALT `<kit>W<code>` or `<kit><tool>`), the title has the count, the combo kit and a joined extra, and the kit part (the model's own kit, quantity 1) holds N by every one of its own titles. This reverses "6-Tool … and Miter Saw stays unverified" (research fix round 2, (3)). *Rejected first form:* "N always counts the kit half": PCL1201K2-PCL515B's "3 Tool Combo Kit With Drill/Driver, Circular Saw, Reciprocating Saw" counts the total (its kit is 2-Tool), so the total reading still stands beside the kit-half one. *Measured:* research runs replayed, prompt .1 1 right, .2 8 → 9, .3 9 → 10, latest 6; 0 wrong in all (320243741 PCL1600K2 + P553 now verifies); refused still: 343952159 (PSBCK102K2: no title of the kit counts 2), a model that is not kit + extra, a kit whose own count differs (PCL1800K3N for "6 Tools").
- **Rule 4, adopted: no Amazon listing found is the part's state** (`partState` → "No Amazon listing found", for the owner to confirm a price or mark it not sold separately). No settle rule and no split question reads an Amazon match; a test keeps PCL1600K2-P553 settled with its P553 looked up and matched to nothing. *Measured:* no part of the 15 had a "none" match (no lookups ran), so it settles none of them.
- **Rule 5, adopted: a refused AI answer says why in one plain sentence** (`plainVerification` in assist/plain.ts, `ReviewQuestion.suggestionPlain`): "Not verified: the page never names DCK240C2. Check the Home Depot page." At most two reasons, the count of the rest ("3 more in Activity"), and where to check; the technical reasons stay on the suggestion for Activity. Every reason the verifiers gave on the owner's 21 real suggestions has a plain form; one the file does not know reads "the AI's answer did not pass the checks", never a guess at it.
- **The catalog check: Amazon's structured fields settle a title match, never its title** (Nov 23, `core/src/keepa/verify.ts` `checkIdentity`; the owner should not be asked what code can prove). A Keepa product (or a SerpApi product page) is *confirmed* for a part only when its brand field is the part's brand (upper case, letters and digits only: "Brand: RYOBI", "Ryobi"), its part number field, else its model field, is the part's model by `sameModel` (DEWALT exact, RYOBI with or without one tool-only B), its manufacturer, when given, is the brand too, and nothing in the title or counts says otherwise: renewed, made for the tool ("for Ryobi", "Replaces", "compatible"), OEM or Replacement, a pack or package or item count that is not the part's, a kit with a battery, charger or amp-hours for a part that is one tool (a bare "kit" passes only for a RYOBI model without its tool-only B: A98401 is a 40-piece kit), a kit sold with more (`addsToKit`), a DEWALT kit whose title names amp-hours none of its battery letters gives (D 2.0, C 1.3 or 1.5, E 1.7, H 5, L 3, M 4, P 5, T 6, X 9, Y 12; any other letter reads nothing), or a title naming another model of the brand and not the part's. *Contradicted*: another brand while the manufacturer is not the part's brand either, or a part number that is another model of the brand's pattern and not a longer or shorter form of the part's. Everything else is *unknown*. *Measured* (`npx tsx scripts/measure-identity.ts`, a test holds the numbers), with keys written from the titles only (`fixtures/keepa/identity-labels.json`) or the validation labels: the 20 recorded Keepa search products checked against PCL235B, 16 right, 0 wrong, 4 unknown; the same products against the model each names, 10 right, 0 wrong, 6 unknown, 6 uncertain keys; the three recorded pulls against after-scan3's parts (PCL235, DCB606, DCF921B), 3 right; the two SerpApi product pages, 2 right; 279 validation search rows, 34 right (all contradictions of another brand), 0 wrong, 227 unknown: a search row has no part number, so it never confirms. Adopted at 0 wrong in 326. *Rejected variants, measured:* contradicting another brand even when the manufacturer is the part's (1 wrong: B0BL8BC8MF, brand FIRE ROSE, manufacturer Ryobi, the validation's right listing for PCL265B); confirming on brand and part number alone, as `grade` makes a part number "strong" (1 wrong: B0DNNZ1W52, part number PCL540, "Cut-Out Tool Kit with Battery and Charger"). Ignoring a manufacturer that is not the brand (B08L38QFFW, RYOBI made by Partyreal) and contradicting a variant part number (PCL001K for PCL001B) each gained one right at 0 wrong; both kept out, as one example cannot show either is safe. The amp-hour rule keeps one listing the validation calls right unknown: B0GQ6QKPWF for DCK540D1. *For v2:* `grade`'s own part-number rule lifts the kit rule (`!partOk`), so a Verify or a product page owed can still make B0DNNZ1W52 strong for PCL540; it was left as it is here (it changes the validation's results) and wants the same title and count checks.
- **The catalog check runs by itself, 1 Keepa token a listing** (`core/src/scan/catalogCheck.ts`). The listings it reads: every automatic title match (probable), and every listing an open automatic match question offers because the brand was not confirmed (`BRAND_NOT_CONFIRMED`, `BRAND_IN_TITLE_ONLY`) or a RYOBI model does not say whether the part is a kit (`KIT_NOT_KNOWN`); each once per part (`MatchRecord.catalogChecks`: a brand or part number does not go stale), most valuable first. In a scan with Keepa in play they are planned after the lookups and the product pages owed, before the refreshes, as `identify` steps with `check: 'catalog'`: a check replaces its listing's refresh (the pull carries the price), else takes tokens the plan left, and none while a lookup waits for tokens (a part with no listing comes first); one that does not fit is not planned, never deferred, so it never stops a scan. A lookup that leaves a title match queues its check behind the queued lookups when one more token is above the reserve, and every Keepa product a scan pulls for any reason is checked free. Never a SerpApi search. The drip (`agent/verifyMatches.ts`) does the same once no lookup waits. *Confirmed*: grade strong, `source: 'page'` (no search row judges it again), `verifiedBy: 'keepa_catalog'`, `verifyEvidence` "Amazon's catalog lists brand DEWALT, part number DCF921B: https://www.amazon.com/dp/B09M3TL9BB", a question offering it withdrawn. *Contradicted*: the listing joins `rejectedAsins`; a title match moves to its next alternative (still a title match, checked next), else the part has no listing with the reason in `verifyEvidence`; a question stops offering it and, with nothing left, is withdrawn the same way: never counted in an estimate, and not a question for the owner. *Unknown*: nothing changes. Activity's drip run says "Verified 6 listings from Amazon's catalog; 1 did not match and was removed.", a scan's summary the same, `LastScanSummary.matchesVerified` counts them; `PartRow.verifiedBy`/`verifyEvidence` carry it to the screens, and `DealEstimate.listings` says what the estimate rests on ("1 part on a verified listing, 1 on a title match."), beside the unchanged ROI range. *Replayed* on after-scan3 (`npx tsx scripts/replay-catalog.ts`): 29 title-match listings and the 3 questions' 4 listings (DCF860B, DCH172B, A98401), 33 tokens in all; the recorded pulls verify PCL235, DCB606 and DCF921B; the other 26 and every question listing need a live pull. DCK540D1 on B0GQ6QKPWF needs one too, and its saved title ("… 5Ah Battery and Charger (DCK540D1)") already keeps it from being verified (a D1 kit holds a 2.0 Ah battery): it stays a title match, and the six estimates on it say so.
- **Step 16: more split answers settled by code, each lever measured at zero wrong before it was adopted** (owner, Nov 23: "come up with more AI solutions to automate even further while still maintaining highly accurate results with grounding"). `npx tsx scripts/measure-reverify.ts` (free, no network) replays (a) the four recorded research runs against the 19-question key and (b) the owner's 21 scan-2 suggestions (fixtures/e2e-2026-11-23). Their ledgers had been kept slimmed (names and links only), so (b) rebuilds each: scan 2's stored evidence, the bundle's own page from its recording or else the page scan 3 stored, plus each line the AI quoted from it that the first check found (its highlights), every search from the rows the AI quoted that the first check found (a row it did not quote is lost), other pages from recordings, the catalog. What cannot be rebuilt stays empty, so its quotes fail: the rebuild can only refuse more. (b) has no key; every newly verified answer was checked by hand against its page. Before step 16, verified right on the runs: prompt .1 1, .2 9, .3 10, latest 6; real rebuilt 3 of 21. After: 1, 11, 12, 8; real rebuilt 8 of 21. Verified wrong: 0 everywhere, before and after.
- **Lever 3, adopted: three narrower verifier rules** (`core/src/research/verify.ts`; tests `core/test/research-narrower.test.ts`). Each one was a technical refusal of an answer the key or the page shows right. (1) *A quote inside one Includes list points only at that list's items.* The real 321809549 and 321811428 pages have highlight lists ("Includes: 6 Tools, 2 Batteries, Charger, Bag, and Accessories") whose one-word items are words of the bullets' items too; a quote of the bullet "Includes: 18T Carbide Blade, … (1) 18V Charger, Tool Bag, …" read "Charger" and "Bag" as the highlight's and "spanned two lists". A quote no single list's text holds keeps the old rule. Measured: removes every "spans two Includes lists" refusal of the real data; releases no question on its own (what stays on those two is below). (2) *A title whose "(2)" lost its parentheses counts two batteries* (`titleBatteryCount`): SerpApi's "2  1.5 Ah Batteries" for "(2) 1.5 Ah Batteries" (324904496, 324903619), only when every Ah rating of the title is in such a phrase, the noun is plural and no "(n)" or singular "battery" is there; the listing judge's `kitBatteryCount` is unchanged. Releases 324904496 PCL1201K2 + PCL430B on runs .2 and .3 (key agrees) and on the real data. (3) *The extra half of a kit + extra model number may account for nothing on the Includes lists* (`extraHalfOf`): when the lists are all the kit's (nothing left over), the kit half is a catalog kit with quantity 1, there are exactly two parts, the extra is the model number's own second piece (RYOBI `<kit>-<extra>`, DEWALT `<kit><whole model>`, never a short code), a single product whose kinds the title names, and the title has the "<N>-Tool Combo Kit … with" phrase. Releases 341644756 PCL1600K2 + PBP003 on runs .2, .3 and latest (key agrees); PCL1600K2 + PBP004 on the same page is still refused. *Rejected:* letting bags, chargers or "Accessories" go unaccounted. The owner's rule is that everything but manuals counts as a leftover (Nov 11), and manuals are already left out by `parseIncludes`. A kit whose own page names a bag accounts for "Bag" already. Letting "Charger" be given to two parts, when two lists each name one, was also rejected: the code cannot tell two chargers from one named twice (real 321811428, PSK005). Kept as known false negatives: 321809549's "(2) PBP003" for the 2-pack PBP2003; PSBCK102K2 (no catalog kit).
- **Lever 1, adopted: an unverified answer is checked again, with no new AI call, when the scan stores new evidence** (`core/src/research/reverify.ts`, `agent/reverify.ts`; tests `core/test/research-reverify.test.ts`, `agent/test/reverify.test.ts`). The model's own answer goes through the same verifier with its ledger plus three things the scan stored by itself, free. (a) A found Home Depot part-price reading of a part's model (the owner's Chrome searched brand + model and read the page found): a ledger product with that page's model and title, sameModel deciding as for any Home Depot page in the ledger. It is kind `reading`: no lines, never quotable, since the model never saw it. Its title also says what the part is (as the part's own page quoted would); the tie to the bundle is still required. (b) An Amazon match graded exact: existence only, no title (a seller's words). (c) A kit page read since the ask, never replacing a source the research read. A reading of the bundle itself, of another model or not found is no evidence. The record keeps its key and cost and gains `verifiedAfter: 'new_evidence'`, `firstCheck`, `recheckedAt`. Not yet verified: the models no source confirms that the part-price reader never read are `waitingFor`, read first in the scan's and the drip's part-price step (`partPricesExtra` / `extra`), and Review says "Waiting for Home Depot's page for DCS334B to confirm the model." (`ReviewQuestion.waitingFor`). To be checkable, an unverified or cannot-tell research suggestion now keeps its ledger whole (the pages' text, the owner's own data in his store); a verified one is slimmed as before. Records written before this change are slim and cannot be checked again: ask again. A suggestion verified this way is a verified suggestion: Autopilot's own rules apply to it as to any. Hooks in `agent/work.ts`: before every AI pass, and after the drip's part prices. *Measured:* runs latest 7 → 8 (328690510 PCL500B + PCL525B, refused for "PCL525B is not a model"); others unchanged; the safety probe gives every unverified answer a found reading for every product the recordings show, and none of the wrong ones verifies. Real data (rebuilt) 4 → 8: DCKTS681D1P1W34 (DCKTS681D1P1 + DCS334B, DCS334B read by the part-price reader), PCL500525SB (PCL500B + PCL525B), PCL1600K2-A986501 and PCL1600K2-A98401 (the kit page PCL1600K2 read since: its drill), each plainly right on its page. *The 7 open split questions after scan 3:* DCKTS681D1P1W34, PCL500525SB and PCL1600K2-A986501 verify with what scan 3 stored. PCL1600K2-PBP2003 stays (the 2-pack false negative). PCL1600K2-PSK005 stays (two "Charger"s). PCL406424SB-A21701-A25RS51 and PCL1600K2-A10FB1G were asked in scan 3, whose suggestions the fixture does not hold.
- **Lever 2, built and off: ask again when what the ask lacked exists now** (`ai.reaskOnNewEvidence`, absent or false: off). Each research ask is stamped with its evidence key (the stored sources with their line counts, and what was missing) and what it lacked (`lacks`). A client tool that failed (`ResearchTrace.failedTools`: a page Home Depot refused, a search a cold tab could not run) is `tool:<name>`; a stored source the evidence said was missing is `missing:`; a kit among the readings with no stored page is `kit:`. A model no source confirmed is not here: lever 1 settles that with no AI call. A question is due again only when its live suggestion is unverified or cannot tell, one of its lacks is resolved (the tool worked in a later ask, the page is stored), no ask was made on that key before, and fewer than two re-asks were made for the question as it reads now. It is asked after the questions with no suggestion, under the same monthly cap, and the cost is the ask run's in Activity (`reaskOf` on the record). Not measurable offline: whether the second answer verifies needs paid calls. Off until the main session measures it; the real "Home Depot search failed" question of scan 3 is the first case.
- **Lever 4, harness only, off: a second model's independent split** (`npm run crosscheck:eval`, `scripts/crosscheck-eval-cli.ts`). For every recorded unverified answer, a different model (default `claude-sonnet-5-5`, $2/$10 a million, through `ModelProvider`) gets only the answer's code-verified quotes, flattened and sorted so the grouping by part is gone, and the bundle's own Includes lines, never the first answer, and must give the parts itself; "agree" is the same models and quantities by kitKey. It prints against the key how many unverified-but-right answers agreement would release and how many wrong ones it would let through. Dry run today: 24 unverified answers on the four runs (19 right, 5 wrong), 22 distinct prompts, about $0.16. Its only proposed use, if measured at zero wrong: "two models agree, check and approve", still the owner's approval, never Autopilot. *Caveat:* the quotes are the first model's choice of evidence, and a hyphen model number spells its own split, so agreement is not fully independent.

## Extension

- **Manifest V3, built with WXT 0.21, React, plain CSS.** One npm package, WXT at the repo root with `srcDir: 'extension'`. Permissions: storage, alarms, sidePanel, notifications, scripting; hosts: serpapi.com and homedepot.com.
- **The service worker is the only context with the key.** Pages send typed messages. The key is in `chrome.storage.local`, restricted to trusted contexts, never in IndexedDB, logs or the page markup (the key field is uncontrolled so React does not mirror it into an attribute).
- **The worker answers only the extension's own pages.** A message from a content script or another extension is refused. *For v2 and step 6:* the chip on Home Depot pages will need its own short allow-list of message types.
- **A scan that cannot be saved is continued at most twice without progress**, then stopped with a plain sentence, so a full disk cannot become a spending loop.
- **The key is stripped from any error text before a scan record is stored.**
- **An account check that never reaches SerpApi throws the search path's network error** (`Network error reaching SerpApi: the account check got no answer.`), never the fetch error's own text, which can carry the account URL and so the key. The panel's Notice says any network error as "SerpApi could not be reached"; the settings page shows the sentence as it is.
- **Bundle pages are read from a background Home Depot tab.** Home Depot refuses page requests from the service worker, so the reader (`extension/lib/hdReader.ts`) opens one inactive background tab in the current window (no permission needed; a minimized window was tried and Chrome opened it full-screen and focused on the owner's Mac), waits until that tab is complete on Home Depot itself, not its blank start page, checking the tab every 250 ms (Chrome does not always deliver the tab's "complete" update event; relying on it timed out 7 of 20 end-to-end runs), runs `fetch('/p/<id>')` inside it with `scripting.executeScript` in the isolated world, and closes it. The tab id is kept in `storage.session` so a tab left by a stopped worker is closed on the next read. `scripting` is the one permission this adds. The function run in the tab is serialised from the built bundle; the end-to-end build proves the bundler leaves it self-contained. `globalThis.splitFinderReadPages` exists only when built with `SPLIT_FINDER_DEBUG=1`; a test asserts the production build has neither it nor any localhost text.
- **Known gaps of the doorway:** if the extension is reloaded or Chrome quits while it reads, `storage.session` is cleared and the doorway tab is not closed (session restore may reopen it); Stop is checked only before reading, so it can wait for the reading to finish (at most about 30 s); with no Chrome window open, opening the tab fails and the next scan tries again.
- **The doorway's blank tab is sent to Home Depot only once its blank page has loaded** (`openDoorway`, Nov 18). Since step 7 the tab is opened on about:blank and named before it navigates (the chip ignores the reader's tab). Navigating it at once raced Chrome's own load of the blank page: in about one doorway in seven under load, and 3 of 120 in a bare loop, Chrome dropped the navigation and left the tab `unloaded` with no URL, so the reader waited its full 30 s and failed with "The Home Depot tab did not load." (a bundle-page read that fails this way also skips the part prices). Waiting for the blank tab's status `complete` (checked every 25 ms, at most 5 s, then sent anyway) lost none of 120. The server had already received the request, so this was never Home Depot refusing; it would have hit the owner's real scans too. The 30 s stalls in the step 9 end-to-end runs (scan.spec, panel-prices), put down to machine load, were this.
- **End-to-end tests cannot reach homedepot.com:** Playwright's Chromium is refused even for a plain visit to a product page. The replay server serves the recorded pages at `/p/<id>`, the recorded refused page (without its own scripts, one of which loads from Home Depot's CDN) for ids a test names, and an empty-data page for ids with no recording, so reading goes on past them. The production-build check builds into `.output-prod-check`, so `npm test` never overwrites `.output/chrome-mv3`.
- **Real-browser checks run in the owner's Chrome** with a debug build and a snippet pasted into the service-worker console. Claude in Chrome cannot open `chrome://` pages or another extension's pages.
- **The side panel reads everything through the worker** (step 5): `getReport` serves `buildReport` plus the owner's packaging answers; pages never open the store. Each screen is its own component with fixed props (`report`, `state`, `api`, …), so screens were built in parallel without touching the app frame.
- **Packaging answers live in `chrome.storage.local`** as one map from bundle model to `retail_boxed` or `loose`, validated on read; no IndexedDB store, so no database upgrade (owner's decision, Nov 10).
- **End-to-end runs from several worktrees use separate replay ports** (`SPLIT_FINDER_E2E_PORT`); the shared fixed port caused spurious failures when two runs overlapped.
- **End-to-end tests use a separate build** (`SPLIT_FINDER_E2E=1`) that may talk to a local replay server on localhost; the production build has no localhost permission and a fixed SerpApi address, and a test asserts both.
- **A running scan's job is broadcast each time it is saved, at most once a second.** A save within the second goes out when the second is up (a timer), carrying whatever the job holds by then; the broadcast at the end of the scan replaces any that is still due. Before this, pages heard only of a scan's start and end, so the progress row never moved.
- **A one-minute alarm is the heartbeat.** Each tick continues an interrupted scan or starts a due one; nothing relies on the worker staying alive or on an alarm surviving a restart.
- **IndexedDB sits behind the same store interface as the in-memory store**, proven by one shared conformance suite.
- **Stored settings are untrusted:** validated on read, with a default per failing field. The shape check lives in `extension/lib/worker.ts`; *for v2* it belongs in `core/` next to `validateSettings`.
- **Known gap: a network error's text is stored with the scan record** in IndexedDB. Replies to pages strip the key from such text; the stored copy does not. *For v2:* strip it where the runner records the message.
- **The settings page saves a field on blur or Enter**, radios and checkboxes at once; the forecast follows every keystroke.
- **A Verify or Look up quote counts only for what it was asked under** (`extension/entrypoints/sidepanel/useQuote.ts`): the parts or the listing, the screen's own count of paid actions, the scan period (a scan's start and its end each begin a new one), and the report on screen. The state does not say when a paid action ends, but the worker broadcasts then and the panel reads the report again after every broadcast, so a new report object stands for "something may have changed": a refusal from a Verify on another screen does not stay. Otherwise the button is disabled, keeping the last count on its label, until the new quote comes. A quote from before a scan or for other parts used to enable the button at once.
- **A Verify or Look up replies when it has ended**, unlike Scan now, which replies once the scan is under way: it is short, and its answer is the button's. As with a scan, Forget key, or saving a different key, stops it before its next search, never during one: it replies `Stopped: the key was removed or changed.` and the parts not searched for wait. **Known gap:** Stop is a scan's control and does not stop a Look up; it spends at most its quote.
- **Accept all for split questions with one reading** (step 6b, Task 3). On his first real run the owner had ten questions whose one reading was the bundle's own model-number split (kit + bit set, kit + battery), each one his to answer. Review shows, once two or more are open, a block listing each bundle and its parts (three rows, then Show all), `Accept all <N>`, and "Each bundle gets the parts shown. Questions with more than one answer stay below." It sends the ordinary `answerReview` `{ choice: 0, seen }` per question, one at a time, so each answer passes the same checks as a single one; questions with zero or several readings, match questions, and questions whose one reading came from the page's Includes list (Ruling 76; they stay below, one at a time, with their "The Home Depot page names …" sentence) are never included, so nothing is chosen that the owner did not see listed and that the model number did not name. The worker reports refusals as sentences only, so the panel treats three (changed, already answered, not found) as skipped and counted and stops on any other; a test ties those sentences to the worker's. Result: `Accepted k of N.`, then ` c had changed and is/are still open.` and ` g was/were no longer open.`; a failure is appended after the count. Its focus is taken once per result, and the run stops if the tab is left. Each row's bundle model links to that bundle's Home Depot page (the link the question's Open on Home Depot uses), in a new tab, named `<MODEL> on Home Depot`, so every bundle accepted in bulk can still be checked at its source (QA, Major). A page reading is recognised by `pageReadingOf` (core/src/pageSplit.ts), which matches Ruling 76's note exactly; a note it missed would be bulk-accepted, a stray match only stays below. The result sentence is cleared when a scan starts, because it describes questions as they were. *For v2:* the worker should send refusal codes, not sentences.
- **A page refusal is said as one sentence** (step 6b, QA). The Deals notice says `Home Depot refused a page (HTTP 403); the rest are read next scan.` as it is, without "Set aside for the next scan." before it, because it already says what happens next; a failed lookup after it keeps that prefix, since the lookup was set aside. The panel matches the runner's sentence by its shape. *For v2:* export the sentence (or a matcher) from core, as `NETWORK_ERROR_PREFIX` is, so the two cannot drift.
- **Download CSV saves every bundle for the owner's PM** (step 6b, Task 4). On the Deals summary row, left of Sort: every bundle in every section, in the order the list shows them, through the same `bundlesCsv` and `api.download` as Detail's single export, named `split-finder-deals-<local date of the click>.csv`. Bundles not yet estimated are included with blank figures, so the file is the whole scan. Not shown with no bundles.
- **The chip on Home Depot product pages** (step 7, Tasks 2 and 3). A content script on `https://www.homedepot.com/p/*` reads the page's inline state (never fetches), sends only the parsed page (`pageSeen`), and draws one 40px `<button>` directly after `[data-component^="price:Price"]` (checked Nov 17) in a closed shadow root; no anchor, not a bundle, or a brand not scanned: no chip. Copy: "Splits into N parts", "Est. profit", ROI and a chevron; "Splits into N parts · Look up prices · N searches" (`lookupParts`, before the per-scan limit; the panel's Look up still says "X of N"); "Splits into N parts · needs your answer" or "Which parts? · needs your answer". A click asks the worker to open the side panel on This page; if Chrome refuses (a relayed click is undocumented as a gesture), the chip's content becomes "Open Split Finder from the toolbar to see this bundle." without growing. Content scripts may send only `pageSeen` and `openThisPage`; every reply is `{ ok, data }` (the merge found the chip reading bare replies, which would never have shown). The scan's reader tab is opened on about:blank, remembered, then navigated, and its visits are ignored. The setting "Show the chip on Home Depot pages" (Alerts, on by default) lives in `chrome.storage.local`; the worker answers `off` and tells open tabs when it changes, so the content script never reads storage. Fonts: `@font-face` does not load in a shadow root, so the faces are declared in the page under names only the extension uses, from web-accessible font files with `use_dynamic_url`, for Home Depot only. Accepted: the host element and the font style reveal to a page that the extension is installed.
- **The This page tab** (step 7, Task 3). Tabs are Deals, This page, Review. For the active tab: "<MODEL> read from this page · no search used", the estimate, "Costs $X · parts net $Y after fees", the parts (Detail's own blocks), Copy ASINs, Open full detail, or the bundle's Review question; while the visit is not stored yet, Save and full detail wait with "Saved when the scan ends." (or "…the search ends."). No bundle page: "Open a Home Depot bundle page to see it here."; chip off: "The chip is off. Turn it on in settings to read this page." The panel opens on This page only when the chip opened it for that tab. Accepted for v1: without a key the panel shows first-run, so This page needs a key although visits do not.
- **The package for the PM** (step 8). `npm run package` builds the production extension into its own folder (`.output-package`) with `NODE_ENV=production` (under the test runner React would otherwise ship dev JSX with absolute paths) and writes `output/bundle-split-finder-<version>-chrome.zip`; a test reads the real zip and refuses dotfiles, maps, localhost, personal paths and any key. `docs/install.md` is the PM's one-page guide.

## Design

- **Open question for the owner: the forecast meter's order.** It draws scheduled searches, then the reserve, then what is unused, so the unused part after the reserve can read as if the reserve were spent. The mockup never shows an unused part.
- **Detail's head says when Home Depot's price was seen** (QA M6): `BundleRow.seenAt`, the bundle record's `seenAt`, the time of the listing response that showed it. Until step 5's QA fixes the head showed the newest Amazon part price time, which read as Home Depot's.
- **A bundle's open split question is asked in its Detail too** (QA M1), with the Review tab's question component and no Skip for now: a "Needs your answer" row otherwise led to a Detail that could say only "not known yet". After a save, focus goes to Detail's title.
- **In Detail the question is its own section, compact**, between the estimate and Parts (QA N5): it leaves out the title, brand and model that Detail's head already shows, and keeps them in the legend as visually hidden text for screen readers.
- **Detail says when a split is the owner's answer and lets the owner change it** (Ruling 42; QA N2, N3). The split is the owner's answer when `BundleRow.answer` is set. Detail then reads "You chose these parts.", "You entered these parts." or "You said none of the readings is right.", with Change, which reopens the question in place. When the data now settles the bundle on its own, the reopen resolves it as a scan would (Ruling 45), and Detail says so instead of showing a question.
- **"Enter the parts" is offered on every split question** (Ruling 34), after the readings and before None of these: a reading can be wrong too, and a typed entry is the owner's own answer. The panel sends the entry split on commas and trimmed; the worker upper-cases and validates it.
- **Detail names a looked-up part with no listing in its estimate:** "Counts PCL1201K2 at $0: no Amazon listing was found." (QA M4). The `$0` is literal because the extension never sets `liquidationRate`, so core's default of 0 values every such part (Ruling 38). *For v2:* if a liquidation setting is added, this sentence must state its value. *Superseded Nov 23 (third real scan): see "An estimate exists only when every part the owner must sell has a counted price".* Detail now says "Not counted: Tool Bag …, with no model number", and only for such items.
- **Each Deals row leads with its section's reason** (QA M4, M5): Confirmed `N parts matched`, or `N title matches` when any part is a title match (owner's decision, Nov 11: title matches stay in Confirmed, and Verify is on Detail); Waiting `N parts to look up`; Missing listing `N parts with no listing`, then `· N to verify`; Inactive and Contents not known as before. A bundle with more than one way to sell adds the tag below.
- **A scan the owner stopped has its own notice:** "You stopped the last scan / Scan now picks up where it left off." (QA M7, Ruling 36), with no Change the limit link. It is true because set-aside lookups go first, listing pages come from the cache and unread pages are read. Stop reads `Stopping…` and is disabled until the scan ends (QA m1).
- **The progress row names its phase and its total is the live plan** (QA m2, Ruling 37): `Scanning · page X of Y`, `Reading bundle pages · X read`, `Looking up parts · X of Y`. Y is done plus queued, worked out from the job each time, so it may fall by one when a brand has fewer pages than planned; it is never a remembered high, which would name pages that will never be read. Brand pages count as done pages (`counts.catalogPages`); leaving them out made the total fall in QA.
- **The limit sentence states the searches used** (QA m7): "It used 2 of the 3 searches allowed per scan." A `run_cap` stop names the limit that made work wait, so it can come with 0 searches used when the planned steps failed, or came from the cache with nothing of their phase left to move up (Ruling 48).
- **Review's count is `N of M`, where M is the open questions, the tab badge's number** (QA m3, N6; Ruling 40). N is the question's place in the current round: the open questions put off this round, other than the one shown, plus 1. Answering lowers M, Skip for now moves N, and the round starts again once every open question has been put off. A count that included the questions answered in this session disagreed with the badge, and it shifted when the tab was switched.
- **Focus follows the owner** (QA m6): Detail's title on open; back to the opened row on Back or Escape, or to the Deals tab when that row is gone; Enter on a Review radio does not save.
- **The settings page opens in a full tab** (QA B1, Ruling 30), through the options entrypoint's `manifest.open_in_tab` meta tag: Chrome's embedded options dialog cut off the key field. The panel and the settings page put SerpApi failures in the same words (`extension/lib/sentences.ts`).
