- **The first-run note says what spends a search** (QA N1): "Searches go to Home Depot and Amazon listing pages and to part lookups. Each bundle's own page is read in your browser, free." The first wording, written by the controller, said only Amazon lookups spend searches, which was untrue.
- **A failure set aside is said with the scan's notice** (QA N4; Ruling 44): "Set aside for the next scan: <the failure in words>". It is shown whenever the last job has a `stopMessage` that the error notice does not show already. The wording is true for a failed lookup, which goes first next scan, and for an unread page, which is read then. Without it, a limit of 1 with a lookup that kept failing read only "stopped at your search limit".
- **What the panel tells the owner links to where it came from** (owner, Nov 11; Ruling 59). The owner asked how to check a screen for themselves.
  - Review's parts question shows "Open on Home Depot". The link is the report row's own link, never one built from the model.
  - Every ASIN link reads "<ASIN> on Amazon".
  - A part with no ASIN, in Detail or in a match question, shows "Search Amazon for <MODEL>". It is an amazon.com search for the brand and model, opened in the owner's own browser. It spends no searches and changes nothing in the extension.
  - The Detail mockup's plain ASIN link is superseded, with the owner's approval.
  - Detail dates each Amazon part price under its net: "seen <time>", from `PartRow.priceSeenAt`, on the head's clock (QA 6, Ruling 60). A price the owner finds changed on Amazon then reads as older, not wrong. It stays on one line in the body face, like the head's "price seen" (Ruling 63): a wrapped "seen 12 min / ago" read badly. For a price seen before today, the part's title gives up about 20px at 360px and ellipsizes.
  - Not done (Ruling 61): a search link per part inside a split question's choices. That question is about what is in the box, which the Home Depot page answers, and a link inside a radio label is interactive content inside a label.
  - *For v2:* keep a one-click path from every price, match and question to the page it came from.
- **Look up sits in the Deals row's right column** (Limit mockup), as a sibling of the row's link rather than inside it. A Look up quoted at 0 reads `Look up · no search` (QA N8).
- **The CSV keeps its machine words** (section ids, `est_roi` as a fraction; Ruling 35): it is for spreadsheets, where stable values are what a formula needs. Copy ASINs says which parts have no ASIN. Step 6 appends two columns last, so earlier columns keep their positions: `sold_as` (the primary option's label, empty for a bundle with one way) and `other_options` (`label: est_profit` pairs joined by ` | `; options without an estimate are left out).
- **Detail: "Parts sell for" is the sum of listed part prices before fees; "After fees" is the estimate's net revenue**, which also holds any liquidation value of parts with no listing.
- **Detail: a part left in review is labelled "Needs your answer"**, not "No listing", because a listing exists and is waiting for the owner's choice.
- **Detail: Export row reuses the run file's columns** (`bundlesCsv` in `core/src/scan/csv.ts`), so one row and the full export never differ.
- **Deals are shown in sections** (owner's decision, Nov 10): Confirmed, Waiting for an Amazon lookup, Review: inactive listing, Review: missing listing, Contents not known, with the Limit mockup's section labels; the Main mockup predates the sections. The This page tab comes with the chip (step 6).
- **A "No, loose" packaging answer keeps the bundle in its section** with an amber `Loose` tag, and the estimate line says loose parts cannot be listed as New on Amazon (owner's decision; version 1 has no eBay prices).
- **Every profit figure is labelled once per screen** (`Estimated profit · ROI` above the first figures, and the estimate line in Detail), never inside a heading.
- **Scan now is disabled only within an hour of a stop at the hourly limit**, with the reason shown as text. This departs from the Limit mockup, which disables it at the per-scan cap: a new scan has a fresh allowance and may use the reserve.
- **Every control that spends searches shows its count**: Verify always `1 search`; Look up its quoted count, or `at most N searches` before a quote; Retry `up to N searches`. Open question for the owner: Scan now shows no count in the mockups, though design.md's rule 4 asks for one.
- **A failure is said once.** The error notice sits under the tabs and is the only place a failed scan is explained; the Deals empty state stays quiet for it. SerpApi's raw messages are put in words (`SerpApi answered with an error (HTTP 404). Try again later.`, the offline sentence).
- **Review reasons are plain sentences.** The splitter's internal notes ("hyphen split, but a part is not in the catalog") are mapped to owner-facing text in the panel (`reviewReason.ts`); a note with no mapping is shown capitalised. The notes in core are unchanged.
- **Off-token colours in the mockups** (`#4A2D00` in the Limit notice, besides the Settings ones above) are replaced by the nearest token until the owner decides.
- **Build to the mockups and `docs/design.md`; token colors only.** Open question for the owner: the Settings mockup uses `#B9B6AD` (reserve meter) and `#1E3A8A` (link hover), which the token list does not have. The nearest tokens are used meanwhile.
- **Ways to sell is its own section in Detail** (step 6, Task 4). It has no mockup, so it follows `docs/design.md` and Detail's part blocks, and `ui-reviewer` checked it (Ruling 31 stands: no mockup was edited). Heading `Ways to sell`; one block per option, the primary first and marked `Shown above`; the option's label, then profit and ROI right-aligned as in the head. Labels: `PCL1600K2 sold whole`, `PCL1600K2 split into its parts`; with two kits `PCL1600K2 and PCL1201K2 split into their parts` and `PCL1600K2 split into its parts, PCL1201K2 sold whole` (split kits first, "A and B" or "A, B and C", no Oxford comma). Leftover line: `Leaves nothing`, or `Leaves 11 parts: 18V Charger, Tool Bag, …` (models with no listing first, then unnamed parts in page order, `×N` after a quantity above 1). An option not looked up yet says `No estimate yet`. (The note `Parts from a kit have no retail box of their own.` was removed in step 9: the PO sells loose kit tools as New, bulk or repackaged listings included.) With more than one option the estimate band drops its own Look up, because each option holds its own. A bundle with one way shows no section. Why: the owner asked for options, and a leftover line and a box note are what make a split's figure honest.
- **The panel says "part", never "item"** (Ruling 68). `docs/design.md`'s fixed vocabulary bans "item" on screen; the plan's first copy used it, and the ui-reviewer caught it. Every on-screen "item" reads "part", including the labels core makes (`report.ts`) and the Look up button. Model numbers in labels, leftovers and the not-known sentence are in the figure face; unnamed parts ("Hex Wrench", "18V. Charger") are in the body face, in the leftover line and in Detail's "Counts … at $0" line alike (QA 1, minor).
- **The Deals row tag is `N ways to sell`**, a plain line in the second sub line, shown only when a bundle has more than one option (`2 ways to sell`). It is text, not a badge. The row's lead reason counts each part once: a part with no listing is not also "to verify" even when it sits in review (QA 1 found "7 parts with no listing · 6 to verify" for 9 parts; now "4 parts with no listing · 5 to verify"). At 360px the second sub line wraps rather than clipping the tag (QA 2).
- **The not-known sentence** names each kit whose page is not stored: `PCL1600K2's contents are not known yet: its Home Depot page has not been read.` It sits in the estimate band, with the model in the figure face, and clears after the read. It stays true when the page was refused or unreadable, which a scan retries every scan; it does not say the read failed (parked). *For v2:* say so once failures are recorded per page.
- **Look up says how many parts it settles: `Look up 5 of 8 parts · 5 searches`** (Ruling 72). It shows only when the quote has parts left waiting, and the button's name is its visible text in Ways to sell (in a Deals row or the band, `… · 1 search, for PCL1600K2-P553`). X is N minus the parts left waiting, so a part a saved listing answers for free counts as settled; with none free X equals the searches. Below the limit every label is as before. In a Deals row the cost goes on a second line (`Look up 5 of 8 parts` over `· 5 searches`) because the button is at most 160px and the row's left column keeps about 190px at 360px.
- **A failed Look up stays said after a partial press** (Ruling 72 review). The sentence is kept with the models sent and shows while the parts still waiting are some of them; it clears on the next press or when a scan ends. Before, it was kept only for the exact parts sent, so any progress before the failure hid it. A refusal from the next quote goes before a kept failure, so a disabled button always says why.
- **A refusal under a Look up in Ways to sell and in the estimate band is its own full-width line under the button** (QA 1, minor; Ruling 72). At 360px the sentence was squeezed into a narrow column beside the greyed button.
- **Review reasons for the new match-question notes** (Ruling 71). In `reviewReason.ts`: "title says Replacement or OEM, which often marks a spare part" reads "Some of these listings say Replacement or OEM in their titles, which often means a spare part." (the note fires when any listing shown says so, QA 2); "N more listing(s) not shown" reads "N more listings are not shown." ("1 more listing is not shown."). The panel splits a reason on "; " and joins the sentences, so a question's three notes read as three sentences. The model in a match question's title is set in the figure face; a Deals row's wrapped "Look up X of N parts" puts its cost on its own line without the "·" (UI review, step 6).

- **Step 9, Task 3: the range is a second line, never the headline** (owner, Nov 18: see every sell price, labelled). A Deals row keeps the Amazon New profit and ROI as its figures, as before, and puts `ROI −6% to +12%` (lowest to highest, the sign written on each end) under them in the figure face, only when the two ends read differently as a whole percent; when they read the same the headline is the one figure and no second line appears. Detail says the same under its estimate line with the profit ends too ("Estimated range: ROI …, profit …, from each part's lowest to highest New price. Reference prices are not counted."), as does This page; each way to sell shows its own range under its ROI. Sort gains Lowest ROI and Highest ROI (the range's ends); ROI stays the Amazon New figure. A row with no estimate has no range and sorts last in all of them.
- **Step 9: a part's prices are a labelled list in two groups** (Task 3 and its UI fix round). Counted prices first, highest first: `Amazon · New` (the featured price, which the estimate counts; `Amazon · New · bulk packaging` when the listing is bulk), `Amazon · Buy Box · sold by X, ships from Amazon` and `Amazon · only offer · sold by X` ("New" is dropped on Buy Box rows because the group is New only). Then a quieter group headed "Reference · not counted": `Amazon list price`, `Home Depot · sold alone` (or `… sold alone as PCL206B` when Home Depot sells the RYOBI twin), and `Amazon · Buy Box (older)` (a Buy Box older than the featured price). Reference labels are ink-2 with no underline at rest, still real links. Detail's sentence reads "Estimated range: lowest to highest counted price — ROI … to …, profit … to …". The ways-to-sell note "Parts from a kit have no retail box of their own." and Detail's "Loose parts cannot be listed as New on Amazon…" line are gone (the PO sells loose kit tools as New, including bulk or repackaged listings). Open: the packaging question still says "Amazon's New condition needs factory packaging" and now only sets a tag; the owner decides whether it stays.
- **Step 9, Task 3: the CSV appends `roi_lowest`, `roi_amazon_new`, `roi_highest`, `hd_price_total`** after `other_options`; earlier columns keep their positions. The ROIs are fractions like `est_roi` (`roi_amazon_new` equals it). `hd_price_total` is each part's Home Depot price times its quantity, in dollars, for reference only, and is empty unless every part has one: a sum of some of the parts would read as the bundle's total. *For v2:* a partial sum with a count of parts covered, if the owner wants one.
- **Step 10, Task 5: eBay reference values in the extension** (owner, Nov 19: eBay is a reference until the judge is reliable). (1) *Where it runs and what it never does:* the scan's eBay step (`core/src/scan/ebayPass.ts`) runs after Home Depot's part prices, whether or not Home Depot refused a page, and never stops a scan: a missing key is the job's `ebayNotice` ("eBay reference values were skipped. Add …"), a refused key, a limit or a failed call another sentence in it, shown as a quiet line under the Deals bar (not as an error, and not as "set aside", which `stopMessage` would say). eBay and the model are ports (`EbayPort`, `ListingJudge`); the worker builds an `EbayClient` and a `ClaudeJudge` from the saved keys. Caps are per job, so a continued job does not get them again: 40 searches, 300 item reads, and the owner's listings judged per scan (Settings, 60, at most 500). The cap counts listings a model was *asked* about; a listing answered from a saved judgement is free and is still used after the cap is reached. A part checked within the freshness window is not checked again; a part left with unjudged listings is asked for again whatever its age. (2) *Judgement cache:* `judgements` store, key `item | revision | prompt version | model | part`. The part is added to the brief's key because the answer is about one part's identity and contents; a listing for two parts is two judgements. It keeps the model's raw answer text and usage, never a verdict, and every read goes through `verifyJudgement` again, so a change in the checks applies to answers saved before it. A call that gave no answer (server error, cut off, refusal, throw) is a count in the part's `notJudged` for that scan and nothing else is stored; a part with nothing judged keeps the record it had. 401/403 from the model, or from eBay, and 429 from either, end the whole step (not just the listing) with a notice: no part could be valued without the judge, and the next call would only be refused again. (3) *Values:* `ebayValues` store, one record per part with every judged listing (title, USD price, units sold, link, the checked verdict, the judge's reasons, the owner's answer) and the value `ebayValue` makes from the effective verdicts (the owner's answer, else the checked verdict). IndexedDB version 3 only adds the two stores. The report adds the value to the part's prices as `ebay_sold_new`, `reference: true`, carrying the value so Detail can say the range, units sold and listings; it is in no estimate and no range (a test compares every figure with and without it), and a part with no value has no eBay line, never $0. Detail puts it last in the reference group with up to three numbered links (http or https only, new tab). The label says "sold" only as "N sold": Browse gives units sold by a listing, not sale prices. (4) *The owner's question:* an unsure listing that could count (USD price, units sold, for a part some bundle holds) is a question "Is this eBay listing exactly MODEL, New, with the same contents?" in the Review list, oldest first with the others. It is not a `ReviewItem`: its answer is a listing's verdict, so it has its own list in the report (`ebayQuestions`, absent in a report built before step 10), its own message (`answerEbay`), and `applyEbayAnswer`. An answer sets `owner` on the listing, recomputes the value, survives later scans while the listing's revision is the same, and is refused while a scan runs, for a listing already answered, or one no longer unsure. The audit file gains `ebayAnswers` (the labels.json shape, `labelledBy: "owner"`, with what the judge said and the listing's page) and `ebayQuestions`; its version stays 1 since nothing was removed. (5) *Keys:* eBay App ID, Cert ID and the Anthropic key are in `chrome.storage.local` only, saved and removed by worker messages and never returned; the state says only whether each is saved. The eBay keys are saved only when eBay gives a token for them (a free token request); the Anthropic key is not checked because a check is a paid call, and a wrong key shows at the first listing as a notice. eBay's token is kept in `chrome.storage.session` (valid two hours, 1,000 grants a day) by wrapping `fetch` for the token request, so core's client is unchanged; a 401 drops it. Changing or removing a key while a scan runs ends that scan's eBay step. Hosts `https://api.ebay.com/*` and `https://api.anthropic.com/*` join the manifest. The end-to-end build moves both to the replay server: eBay through the client's `baseUrl`, and the judge, whose address is fixed in core so no setting can redirect the key, through a rewriting `fetch` that exists only when the build has a base URL. (6) *Settings copy:* two sections, "eBay reference values" and "Listing check" (no mockup; design.md says so). They say the eBay figure is a reference and not counted, that judging uses the owner's Anthropic key and bill, and an estimated cost per scan from 1,700 tokens in and 500 out a listing at the published rates (measured on the 217 recorded listings: the instructions are about 1,100 tokens and a listing about 400); the eval measures the real figure. The Anthropic field is "Anthropic key", not "Anthropic API key": Playwright's `getByLabel('API key')`, which every spec uses for SerpApi's, matches by substring. The button is "Add key": the settings page has no Save button. (7) *CSV:* `ebay_ref_low`, `ebay_ref_high`, `ebay_units_sold` are appended; each is one figure per part in part order joined with " | " like `amazon_prices`, "?" for a part with none, and blank when no part has any. Never a sum: a total of some of the parts would read as the total of them all (`hd_price_total` is blank for the same reason). *For v2:* a bundle's eBay total with the count of parts it covers, if the owner wants one; and the judge eval's measured token counts in place of the estimate.
- **Step 10, Task 5, fix round 1: owner answers, call limits and what a stop does.** (1) *The owner's answer is its own record* (`ebayAnswers`, IndexedDB version 4, key part, item id, revision), written by `applyEbayAnswer` and applied by the eBay step to every value it makes. Keeping it only on the part's value record lost it whenever a later check ended partway (a limit, a 5xx, another model): the new record was built from the listings that check reached. Now a check that stops carries over, from the last record, the listings it did not reach (in search order), and the answers are applied to all of them; a check that judged nothing leaves the record alone. The audit file's labels are read from the answer records, which hold the title, page, the judge's verdict and reasons, so they survive anything done to the value. An answer holds for the revision it was given for; a seller's edit asks again. (2) *Every call is bounded.* A model call and an eBay call each get an abort signal that ends at 30 s (`JUDGE_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `callTimeoutMs`) or when the scan is stopped, so Stop ends a call in flight. The worker binds eBay's `fetch` to those signals (core's client is unchanged) and passes the stop signal to the judge. A call that runs out is not judged, or a read skipped: never a verdict. (3) *A 400 from the model* (unknown model, no credit, a bad request) ends the step with a plain notice, like 401/403 and 429: the next call would be refused the same way. A 429 or a 5xx on eBay's token request is the client's rate-limit or server error, not "the keys were refused", so the notice does not blame the keys. (4) *The job is saved after every call* (search, item read, model call and its tokens), counts first, so the caps (40, 300, the owner's limit) cannot be passed after the worker is evicted mid-step; and the worker's "progress" string (`progressOf`) includes the three eBay counts, so an eviction during the step is not "no progress" and a scan never gives up because of an optional step. (5) *The judgement's key also holds the part's other written form and a hash of its name*, both of which the model is shown: a changed name is a new question. (6) The App ID is scrubbed from replies as the secrets are. (7) *Copy:* our own reasons say "listing details", not "item specifics" (the model's quoted words are left alone); a saved key shows dots in its field and the button says Replace; a refused setting is said under its control, and there is no floating "Saved." line (the page already says changes save as they are made). The eBay line in Detail sets its range and counts in the figure face and puts the listing links on a line of their own.
- **Polish: each eBay link reads its listing's price, and the two Remove buttons say which key.** (1) The links in Detail's eBay line read the listing's own price (`$119.99`) in place of "Listing 1": the owner compares listings by price, and an ordinal says nothing about it. The price is stored per link as `linkPrices`, in step with `links`; a value stored before it has none and its links keep "Listing N". (2) The buttons are "Remove SerpApi key" and "Remove Anthropic key" (their confirmations too), not two "Remove key": a screen reader's list of buttons, and voice control, need a name that tells them apart, and the visible text is the same words as the name.

- **Step 11, A2: Ask AI in the panel** (owner, Nov 20: the AI drafts, the owner decides). (1) *A suggestion is its own record,* never a field of the question: IndexedDB version 5 adds a `suggestions` store (key question id and time asked), read through a `SuggestionStore` the worker is given, so a scan, a Look up and the scan's store never touch it. It keeps the checked suggestion, the signature of the question as it was asked (`seen`), tokens and dollars at the model's published rates, and, once the owner answers, the outcome. (2) *Only a suggestion made for the question as it reads now is shown:* the worker drops one whose `seen` differs after the call (a scan rewrote the readings meanwhile) and the report lists only those matching, so a stale one is never preselected. (3) *Ask AI is not taken through the start gate:* a call may take 30 seconds and must not hold a scan back; it is refused while a scan runs and with no Anthropic key, and a second press for the same question while one is out. It uses the Settings model and a 30 s timeout; a timed-out or failed call is `not_suggested`, one plain line, no record unless it cost something. (4) *Approve is the answer path:* the panel sends the ordinary `answerReview`, with `aiSuggestion: true` only to say a suggestion was on screen. The worker then notes, from its own stored suggestion, whether the saved answer is the one proposed (`agreed`): true, false, or null when the AI proposed nothing (cannot tell). It never takes the panel's word for agreement. Accept all never sends the mark and never applies a suggestion. The audit file gains `aiSuggestions: [{questionId, source: 'ai', suggestion, agreed, askedAt, answeredAt, answer, model, tokens, costUsd}]`, every ask, answered or not; its version stays 1. (5) *Panel:* the button reads "Ask AI · about $0.02" (the estimate is 1,800 tokens in and 500 out at the model's rates, rounded up to a cent, never $0.00) and is a text button after the answer controls; it is absent without a key and gone once a suggestion is shown (one ask per question; asking again costs money for the same evidence). A verified suggestion preselects its choice and the primary button reads Approve; unverified, cannot tell and "none of these" (`none_suggested`) preselect nothing and the button stays Save answer. A choice the owner made while the AI worked is not taken from them. Quotes show at most three, each with its source link (http or https only, new tab). *For v2:* an ask for several questions, if the owner wants it; the owner's agreement rate is in the audit file, not on screen.
- **Research tools read through the doorway, six a question, and read nothing they cannot name** (step 12, R2, `extension/lib/researchTools.ts`). `readHomeDepotPage` and `searchHomeDepot` open the doorway tab per call (the same settle, pause and one-doorway-at-a-time rules as the scan's readers; a queue serialises them), and one question may make at most 6 page reads plus searches (`QuestionBudget`; the seventh answers `{error: 'limit'}` with no tab); the catalog lookup is local and uncounted. A refusal (any non-200 except 404, or a 200 with no page data) is not retried and ends the question's Home Depot calls, as the scan stops at a refusal; a 404 only says there is no such page, because the agent may name an item id that does not exist. Only `https://www.homedepot.com/p/...` addresses and 9-digit item ids are read; the fetch always goes to `/p/<id>` on the doorway's own origin. Page specifications and search results are read from recordings (`fixtures/homedepot-pages/research-2026-11-20.json`): `specificationGroup[].specifications[].{specName, specValue}` flattened in page order into `PageSections.specifications`, and a search's `base-searchNav-<itemId>` entries (brandName, modelNumber, productLabel, canonicalUrl) in page order, up to 10 returned. Home Depot answers a query it cannot match with unrelated products (the recorded "zzqx notaproduct 98765"); they are returned as they come, because relevance is the AI's to judge and the code does not guess. A search that Home Depot redirects straight to one product page is not recorded, so it reads as no results until it is. *For v2:* one doorway for a whole question instead of one per call (about 3 s of settling each), if the research feels slow.

- **Step 12, R3: Ask AI about all, the "AI suggestions" section and Approve all verified** (owner, Nov 20: fully automatic with human-gated approval). (1) *The queue is the worker's, in memory,* not a job: `askAiAll` claims it through the start gate (so no scan starts between the check and the claim), replies `{ queued }` at once and asks one question at a time over an injected `AskOne = (questionId, signal) => Promise<StoredSuggestion>` (today the step 11 evidence-only call; the controller passes research for split questions). The worker, not `AskOne`, keeps the record (`keep`), so one rule covers a single ask and the queue: a call that gave nothing and cost nothing leaves no record, and a call Stop aborted writes nothing, so there is never a half-written suggestion. Each question is read again before its turn (one answered meanwhile is skipped), and again after the call (one changed meanwhile has its record closed, as in step 11). A worker evicted mid-queue loses the queue and not its work: what was suggested is stored, and pressing the button again asks only about the rest. *For v2:* a persisted queue if the owner runs hundreds. (2) *Gate pattern: the same as Verify and Look up.* Each is refused, in one sentence, while the other runs: `askAiAll` and a single Ask AI while a scan runs ("A scan is running. Ask again when it has finished."); Scan now while the queue runs ("Asking AI is running. Stop it first, or wait for it to finish."); the alarm's tick simply starts nothing and tries again a minute later, so a scheduled scan waits for the queue and is not lost. The header's Stop is the queue's while it runs (`stopAskAll`, which ends the call in flight); removing the Anthropic key stops it too. Three failed calls in a row (a refused key, a limit, no answer from the provider) end the queue with the last call's sentence: asking on would fail the same way. (3) *Progress is the scan's row, from `askAllView`* (core/src/scan/pipeline.ts, additive): "Asking AI · 7 of 26", the estimate from the measured seconds a question took (`askAi` unit kind, seed 8 s, kept in the rates like every kind), and "$0.41 so far" as the one line of Details. The cost is each record's tokens at the model's rates. The worker sends it in AppState (`askAll`, and `askAllResult` once it ends: what was asked, how many verified, what it cost, whether it was stopped); the panel says the result in one sentence under the buttons until a scan starts. (4) *Ask AI after each scan* is its own preference (`askAfterScan`, off by default), not a field of the eBay settings, so those stay as they were; after a scan that ended any way but the owner's Stop (finished, out of searches, a failed search: its questions are open all the same) the worker starts the queue, and a refusal (no key, nothing to ask) is not an error. (5) *The section lists only verified suggestions the code can approve:* `verifiedRows` (core/src/assist/approve.ts) takes an open question's live suggestion, verified, whose answer `approvalOf` can turn into the answer Approve would send for the question as it reads now (a reading it still has, a listing it still offers, parts of 1 to 8 models). Unverified, cannot tell and none-of-these stay in the list below with their block. Approve all asks first ("Approve 18 AI answers? Approve · Cancel"), then sends each answer over the same `answerReview` message as a single Approve, with `aiSuggestion: true`, so the worker records each as an approval (agreed) from its own stored suggestion. A refusal that is about the moment (a scan, a search) stops the run; one about a question (changed, answered, a part the worker will not take) is counted and said, and the run goes on. (6) *A question the owner asked about on its own in the panel stays in its place* with its suggestion until it is answered, as in step 11 (they are looking at it, and any choice they made while the AI worked is theirs); after a reload it is a row like the rest. (7) *Parts the AI found* use the research core's verified answer `{ decision: 'parts', kind: 'split', parts }` (each model repeated by its count; superseded the build's first `{ decision: 'choose', kind: 'parts', parts: [{ model, qty }] }` variant at integration): shown as "DCK940D2 + 2 × DCB203", opened with "Enter the parts" selected and the models typed in (or the reading, when the parts are exactly one of them), and saved through the typed-parts answer, each model listed as many times as its count, which is what the worker already takes. Editing what the AI typed makes the answer the owner's own (the button reads Save answer, and it is recorded as a disagreement if it differs). (8) *The per-question button is shown only where a call would be made:* a split question always (research can read the page and propose parts when the tool offered no reading), a match question only with a listing to choose. Its cost comes from `researchCostLabel(model)` for a split (measured: about $0.20 at Opus, $0.02 at Haiku; first written as "about $0.10" from an 18,000-token guess) and "about $0.02" for a listing at Opus's rates: the split estimate is 18,000 tokens in and 1,400 out over a few turns, a guess until research is measured. (9) *Ask AI about all takes two:* with one question to ask there is the question's own button, and "about all 1" would be the same button twice; the bar is also hidden with no key and when every question has a suggestion. Secondary like Accept all's neighbour, not a borderless text button: beside an ink button it would read as a link.

- **Step 12, fix round R3b: the queue's limits and the Review layout** (UI review: FAIL; code review: Important). (1) *A research that ran past its time, tool-call, web-search or request limit is `not_suggested` with `notSuggested.reason: 'limit'`* (a new `NotJudgedReason`, research only), so the queue counts it for the three-in-a-row stop: before, it had no reason, read as "not a provider failure" and reset the count, so a model that never finished would have been asked about every question at full cost. (2) *Spending cap:* the queue stops once what it has cost is more than twice the estimate its button showed (`askAllShownCost`: the button's own rounded-up figure, so the cap is never below what the owner was told; no cap when the model has no rate). The sentence says it plainly and that nothing was applied; what was suggested stays (it was paid for and is only a suggestion). (3) *Approve all snapshots its rows when the confirmation opens* and the press applies that list; the header's live count may differ from the confirmation's while it is open (a row came in), and the confirmation says what it will apply. (4) *The doorway tools end on the research signal:* the pause, the load, the settle and the in-tab read are each raced against it, the tab is closed in `finally`, and a call waiting for its turn opens no tab once aborted; the in-tab fetch may finish unseen in a tab that is already closing. A stop is not a Home Depot refusal and does not end the question's later calls. (5) *Layout:* the Ask AI row first, alone (so it is no longer squeezed beside Accept all at 360px); the suggestions section next; Accept all below it, since the owner's first decision is the ones the AI settled; Approve all is a text button, not a bordered one, so the two AI buttons cannot be read as one choice. (6) *The result line keeps its place in the Ask AI row* and is hidden once the section shows results; a stop for a problem or cost stays, because the section cannot say why it holds fewer rows than were asked about. (7) *The compact row's quote leaves off a search row's own label* ("RYOBI PCL525B (item 318898528): "), display only: that prefix is the ledger's rendering of a search row, and in one line at 360px it spent the room before the quote said anything. *For v2:* shorter source labels in the row, or a two-line quote, if one line still cuts too much.

- **Ask AI on every Review question, and a price the owner confirms** (owner, Nov 20: the button was missing on "needs your answer", and a part with no Amazon price showed $0 with no way to set one). (1) *The per-question button is on every Review question* once an Anthropic key is saved: Review, This page (only after the question is stored), and Detail's open split question. A match with no listing still gets the button, with no price on it, because pressing it makes no model call (`suggestAnswer` returns not_suggested and says to confirm a sell price). Ask AI about all is unchanged: `canAsk` still skips that question, so the queue never spends on it. eBay yes/no questions stay without Ask AI. No key means no button. (2) *A confirmed price is the owner's, with a link.* `confirmedPrices` (IndexedDB version 6) holds brand, model, dollars and an http(s) link. The worker stamps the time and refuses a scan in progress, a price that is not above $0, and a link that is not a web page. It counts in the estimate, as New, only while the part has no active Amazon New price; after that it stays on the part as a reference ("Confirmed by you"). It is never copied onto a RYOBI twin. The bundle's section stays missing or inactive: the price is not an Amazon listing. A part not looked up yet stays at $0 with no form. A leftover with no model number was the same until the decision below. The AI does not invent a dollar amount. CSV appends `confirmed_price` and `confirmed_link` after the eBay columns. eBay sold comps stay a reference and do not fill the $0.

- **A price counts when he confirms it** (Alex, Nov 20, answering the five open questions). (1) An eBay sold range stays a reference. It counts only when he chooses a stored listing, or types a price and a link. Choosing is the decision; nothing applies a figure on its own, and the AI does not invent a dollar amount. (2) Facebook has no API. A link he pastes is enough, and the price is the one he types. The link is not read. (3) The first version he receives is Home Depot only. Lowe's stays out; he will add other retailers later. (4) A charger or bag with no model number can be confirmed. The stored key is `~` and the page's text. It counts in that option's estimate, it is not looked up, and no model number is invented. (5) An Amazon listing that is not for sale is not waited on. A price he confirms counts until that listing is for sale again, and the bundle stays in Inactive listing. A part still in Review does not show the price form: the match question is on the part, Ask AI drafts a listing, and his approval is the answer.

- **Each price is said once** (owner, Nov 20: the same eBay price was a link and a button, an inactive listing said it was not for sale three times, and a charger on another way looked like a save that did nothing). An eBay listing's own price is only the Count button, with Open listing beside it. The reference line keeps the range and does not repeat those prices. When the form is not where those prices are counted (an active Amazon price, a part still in Review, a range with no per-listing price), the listing links stay in the reference line. An inactive listing's tag says Listing inactive, its last price is labelled Last Amazon price, and the form says only that a confirmed price counts until the listing is for sale again. A charger or bag with no model number that is not in the estimate on screen is priced on the way whose profit it changes, under that profit, once. This page has no ways list, so the line there names that way and, once a price is stored, its estimated profit. While a scan runs, "A scan is running. Save the price when it has finished." is said once above a list of these forms, and once on a form that stands alone.

- **A search that only names the model on accessories reads one product page** (owner, Nov 20: PCL406B's question listed pads, and the AI correctly refused them). The recorded search for "RYOBI PCL406B" puts a RYOBI sander first, B0BSJ97BSR, titled "18V ONE+ 5\" Random Orbit Sander Cordless". That title does not contain the model, so the matcher rejects it, and the rows that do contain PCL406B are pads and "fits" listings. The lookup then reads that first same-brand row's product page, one search, only when every row that names the model is an accessory ("for Ryobi", "fits", or an OEM spare part) and the row was rejected only because its title omits the model. The page's part number or barcode is the match: exact or strong, and the accessory question closes. A page that names another model, such as P411, leaves the question as it was. The read runs in this scan when the cap still allows it, taking a not-yet-started lookup's slot if that is what the cap has left, and otherwise it is owed (`pageDue`) and the next scan reads the page without repeating the search. A page that cannot be read is not owed again.

- **Delete all data empties the deals and keeps the account** (owner, Nov 20). Settings › Your data › Delete all data removes every deal, match, answer, confirmed price, suggestion and saved search, and a Home Depot visit that was waiting to be stored. It asks once, and it waits while a scan, a Look up, a Verify, Ask AI or a kit-page read is running, because those write the same records. The SerpApi, eBay and Anthropic keys stay, the settings stay, and the search ledger stays, so the searches already counted still count. Clear saved searches is unchanged: it deletes only the saved responses.

- **An unread kit is read from its Home Depot page, not priced as one part** (owner, Nov 20: PCL1201K2-PCL515B showed two parts and counted the kit at $0). The two rows were the two model numbers. PCL1201K2 is a kit; the drill, saw, batteries and charger are on its own Home Depot page, and that page had not been read. The panel's **Read kit page** (`readKitPage`) reads that one page in the browser, through the scan's page reader, and saves it where the scan would (`kitPageTarget`: a catalog kit by its listing-row item id, or the kit's own bundle record when it has one). It spends no search, and it does not run beside a scan, a Look up, a Verify or Ask AI, because they share the Home Depot tab or the same records. A refused or unreadable page is not stored, so the "not read" sentence stays true. A kit with no digit item id has no button: the next scan has to find its listing row. The Includes list is the only source of the tools. An Amazon row whose title does not name the kit is not a match, and a price is never copied off a link the owner pastes. Confirm sell price stays for a part that was looked up and still has no Amazon New price; it is not offered on the unread kit, because that would count the whole box as one sale. eBay sold comps stay a reference.

- **Keepa planner and panel Look up aligned (Phase 1 review, Nov 22).** Lookup slots use `KEEPA_LOOKUP_TOKEN_ESTIMATE` (30). Kit-item steps are planned via `planKeepaItemKeys` under the same discovery/tracking slices, not SerpApi `room`. Keepa-only deferrals set `stopReason` from the Keepa budget, not SerpApi `run_cap`. Panel Look up uses `keepaLookup` when a Keepa key is on.

- **Native app as main product — Lane D (Alex, Nov 22).** App-first UI plus thin Chrome helper; implementation `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-22-native-app-as-main-product-impl.md`. Secrets in `app-secrets.json` (mode 600) plus non-secret `app-settings.json`; settings and keys sync via `appSyncRun` (`appSyncKeys` fingerprint, `appSyncAiPrefs` for **Ask AI after each scan**). Review in the app queues answers and Ask AI jobs (`/api/review/ask/queue` → `appSyncAsk`); audit refresh while an ask-all batch runs. `desktop-ui/` at `/ui/` (FirstRun, Deals+Detail, Review, Settings). Helper options redirect when packaged or `appPrimaryUi`. E2E: scan queue + helper primary header. Still open: macOS Keychain for secrets, full mockup parity, DMG-only marketing + optional CWS helper listing.

- **Chrome manifest: no `"permissions"` permission string** (Nov 22). MV3 manifest `permissions` must be named capabilities from Google's list; `"permissions"` is not valid and can block reload. `optional_host_permissions` plus `chrome.permissions.request({ origins })` is enough for the agent bridge. Removed from `wxt.config.ts`.

- **Native desktop (Tauri) + Chrome extension** (Nov 22). v2 Phase 3: menubar/Tauri shell, not a replacement for the extension. `npm run desktop:build` produces macOS `.dmg` / Windows installers with the extension zip and an embedded **agent-dashboard** sidecar (a Node Single Executable Application, see "Desktop sidecar is a Node SEA" below); opening the app starts the dashboard without npm. Data lives in Application Support via `SPLIT_FINDER_DATA_DIR`. See `docs/desktop-app.md`.

- **Windows VM launchers, not a standalone .exe** (Nov 22). Split Finder stays a Chrome MV3 extension plus optional Node agent; there is no single Windows binary for the product. `windows/` provides Setup/Start `.bat` and `.vbs` (double-click in the VM), optional `build-exes.ps1` for `.exe` wrappers via ps2exe on the guest only, and `npm run vm:bundle` zips an unpacked extension plus guides into `output/split-finder-vm-kit.zip`.

- **Keepa auto discovery cap and agent bridge** (implementer, Nov 22). `keepaDiscoveryShare` (10–90) caps auto mode only; heavy modes still use fixed 10/90. The local agent URL is decoded at runtime (base64) so the production extension zip contains no loopback literal (`package.test.ts`). The bridge POST is optional via `agentBridgeAfterScan`; denied host permission skips the POST only.

- **Version 2 direction: Keepa, discovery/tracking scale, agent alerts** (owner, Nov 22). Amazon data should move to **Keepa** (history, part numbers, offer trends) with a **token budget split**: **discovery** maps new Home Depot bundle SKUs and each bundle's **ways-to-sell combinations**, and **tracking** refreshes known ASINs for price, sales velocity and seller-count trends so the tool can say **when** to buy, not only today's ROI. He wants **both** scopes: every bundle SKU worth watching **and** every sell plan per bundle, not only the primary option headline. **Home Depot** stays on SerpApi plus browser page reads until another source exists. The **scale** shifts toward tracking automatically when open discovery work (unresolved splits, waiting parts, Review, unread kits, options never estimated) is low. A **background agent** should message him on high ROI and non-empty Review; desktop notifications are only the first step. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-22-keepa-agent-discovery-tracking.md`. *Prototype v2 (implementer, Nov 22):* deals stay in IndexedDB; new Keepa/agent scratch uses the **cache** pattern; **no Docker**; optional **local Node agent + dashboard** alongside the extension; headless container HD scraping rejected. Alex still to answer notification channel and 24/7 vs Chrome-open-only. *For v2:* extension remains the Review and Includes console; optional relay for push when Chrome is closed.

- **Desktop shell (step 13 W3, Nov 22).** The shell owns the tray, close-to-tray, single instance, window-state, native notifications and launch at login; the agent owns what to say (`notify` events). The shell talks to the agent in Rust (`ureq`, no webview round trip), so it works while the window is hidden. The token file name is one constant (`TOKEN_FILE`). Pause/Resume uses `POST /api/agent/pause|resume` and `agent.status === "paused"`. Tray count is macOS tray title text, not a dock badge, because the dock has no window while hidden. Notification click is inferred from app activation within two minutes, because the desktop plugin has no click callback. The dashboard origin gets a remote capability limited to three app commands; the CSP in `tauri.conf.json` covers local assets only, so the agent's static server must send the same policy for the dashboard itself. *For v2:* a signed app with a real notification-click API (UNUserNotificationCenter delegate) would remove the inference.

- **Provider-neutral AI: one `ModelProvider`, Claude and OpenAI-compatible (step 13 W6, Nov 22; owner: expect cheaper non-Anthropic models like DeepSeek).** `core/src/ai/`. (1) *Contract:* `ModelProvider.send(ModelRequest) -> ModelResponse`: system, messages (text, `tool_call`, `tool_result` blocks), client tools (JSON schema), optional server tools, an optional `responseSchema` for a one-shot answer, usage with cache tokens, a normalised stop (`end`, `tool_use`, `max_tokens`, `refusal`, `pause`, `other`) and failures as the existing typed `NotJudged` reasons (auth, rate_limited, overloaded, server, bad_request, network, aborted, refusal, max_tokens, ...). One call, no retries, never throws, key in a header to the configured address only. `native` carries a provider's own content blocks (Claude's server-tool and thinking blocks) back unchanged on the next request. (2) *Claude* (`ClaudeProvider`) wraps `sendClaudeMessages`; tests assert that judge, assist and research through it send byte-for-byte what `ClaudeJudge`, `ClaudeAssist` and `ClaudeResearch` send (those classes stay, so existing callers and tests are untouched). (3) *`OpenAICompatibleProvider`* posts to `{baseUrl}/chat/completions` with `tools` as functions and `tool_choice: "auto"`; a one-shot `responseSchema` is a forced `submit_answer` function whose arguments are the answer (plain text is still read, and verified like any answer). Tool results go back as `role: "tool"` messages (an error is prefixed "Error:", the API has no flag). Usage is normalised so `inputTokens` is the uncached input: DeepSeek's `prompt_cache_hit_tokens` / `prompt_cache_miss_tokens`, OpenAI's `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, else prompt/completion tokens. 402 (DeepSeek: no balance) is `auth`; 503 is `overloaded`; 400, 413 and 422 are `bad_request`. An http address (other than this computer) or one with a user, password or query is refused, so the key is never sent in the clear. (4) *DeepSeek facts read from its docs on Nov 22, 2026* (api-docs.deepseek.com; read through a page summariser, so the prices are worth one look before they matter): models `deepseek-flash` and `deepseek-v4-pro` (the brief's `deepseek-chat` is not on the pricing page); thinking is on by default, and then a forced `tool_choice` is a 400 and `reasoning_content` of earlier turns must be sent back in a tool loop or the API returns 400, so the preset sends `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` (the provider does not carry `reasoning_content`; do not enable thinking through `extraBody`). Prices per million tokens at the PEAK rate (off-peak is half; peak is 01:00-04:00 and 06:00-10:00 UTC on weekdays): flash $0.30 in (cache miss), $0.006 cache hit, $1.20 out; v4-pro $1.32, $0.044, $3.96. The page states no effective date. A DeepSeek cache write costs nothing extra, so only Claude's usage carries `cacheCreationInputTokens`. (5) *Research without server tools:* a provider whose `capabilities.serverTools` is empty gets the Home Depot tools and `submit_answer` only; the loop sends `RESEARCH_PROMPT_HOMEDEPOT_ONLY` (the web bullet replaced by "You have no web search ... answer cannot_tell"), the input says "There is no web search", and the prompt version is `<version>+homedepot-only`, so a suggestion is tied to the words it was given. Anthropic keeps web_search and web_fetch. `ProviderResearch` adapts any provider to the loop, whose content blocks stay Messages-API-shaped (the recorded runs and replays are written in that shape). (6) *Prices:* `PRICE_TABLE` is keyed by model id (the same model through another host has another id); `setModelRates(model, rates)` adds or replaces one, and every estimate and cost reads the table, so a changed price or an unknown model is a setting, not code. A model with no rate has no cost label, never a guess. (7) *Settings to models:* `providerFrom(setting, {resolveKey})` for `{provider: 'anthropic' | 'openai-compatible', baseUrl?, model, apiKeyRef, preset?, id?, label?, rates?}` returns a provider or a sentence saying why not; `pipelinesFrom({judge, assist, research}, deps)` gives each pipeline its own model, and one pipeline without a key does not stop the others. `apiKeyRef` is a reference the agent resolves; core never sees where keys are kept. *Open:* other OpenAI-compatible services may reject `anyOf` with null in a function schema (the research schemas use it); a service that cannot force a function falls back to plain text, which the verifiers read as before. A cheaper model is not a better one: run the evals (`judge:eval`, `assist:eval`, `research:eval`) against it before Autopilot trusts it; verification is by code either way.
- **Step 13 W1: the agent holds the keys, and every call to it carries a token** (step 13 plan, Decisions 2 and 3, Nov 22). This changes CLAUDE.md rule 3 ("in the extension it lives in `chrome.storage.local` only"): the keys now live in the agent, in `<app-data>/store/keys.json`, owner-only (0600), and the OS keychain is later. No endpoint returns a key: `PUT /api/keys/:name` and `DELETE /api/keys/:name` write, and everything else says only `hasKey` per key (`keys: {serpapi, keepa, anthropic, openai, ebay, telegram}`). The pre-step-13 `GET /api/settings` served every key in plain text with no auth; that is gone, so the extension no longer pulls keys from the app and keeps its own until it becomes a worker (W5). **Auth:** the app's token is `<app-data>/agent-token` (0600, created on first start); the shell reads it and injects it into the webview as `window.__SPLIT_FINDER_TOKEN__`, and sends it itself for `/api/events` and pause/resume. A Chrome extension pairs: `POST /api/pair/request {extensionId}` (Origin must be `chrome-extension://<that id>`) returns a six-digit code and a claim secret; the owner allows it in the app (`POST /api/pair/approve`, app token only); the extension's next `POST /api/pair/claim {requestId, claimSecret}` returns its own token once. The agent keeps only the token's sha256; pairing again replaces it and `DELETE /api/pair/extensions/:id` revokes it. Host must be `127.0.0.1:8790` or `localhost:8790` (no DNS rebinding); a request with an Origin must pair it with its own token (the app's origin with the app's token, an extension's with that extension's); a request with no Origin (the shell, curl) is decided by its token alone. CORS is answered only for the app, paired extensions, and the two pairing calls. JSON bodies stop at 1 MB. Errors are `{error: {code, message}}` with the HTTP status. *Why the token file and not a cookie:* a cookie handed to whoever loads `/ui` would reach any local user who can open the port; the file is readable only by the owner's account. *For v2:* keychain for keys and tokens; rotating the app token (today: delete `agent-token` and restart).
- **Step 13 W1: one store, one writer** (Decision 3). `agent/store.ts` keeps typed collections, one JSON file each under `<app-data>/store/` (agent, settings, keys, deals, review, suggestions, activity, autopilot, jobs, pairing, outbox), written whole to a temp file and renamed. A `store.lock` holding the process id refuses a second agent on the same data folder; a lock left by a dead process is taken over. On the first start (no `store/meta.json`) the pre-step-13 files are read once (`app-settings.json`, `app-secrets.json`, `helper-state.json`, `deals-panel.json`, `deals-snapshot.json`, `agent-bridge.json`, `agent-last-scan.json`, the scan, review, paid, ask and Home Depot read queues) and `meta.json` lists them; they are left on disk, unread. The five file-queue modules, the two snapshot stores, the settings and secrets stores and `dashboardPage.ts` are deleted. `outbox` is the mailbox today's extension still polls (scan requests, review answers, paid actions, Ask AI); it goes when the agent scans (W4) and the extension becomes a worker (W5). *For v2:* SQLite if a collection outgrows rewriting it whole.
- **Step 13 W1: Home Depot reads are worker jobs** (Decision 4). The agent queues `page`, `kitPage`, `partPrice` and `search` jobs in the store; the paired extension long-polls `GET /api/worker/next` (up to 25 s, 204 when nothing came) and posts `POST /api/worker/result`. A job is leased for 2 minutes; a lease that runs out or a failure the worker reports hands it out once more, then it fails. Every call from the paired extension, and an open long poll, counts as the helper being connected (90 s). The old `/api/hd-read/*` calls are page jobs now.
- **Step 13 W1: events** (`GET /api/events`, server-sent): `state` (the whole `GET /api/state`), `deals`, `review` (with `counts.review`, so the shell's badge can follow either), `activity`, and `notify` `{title, body, kind: review|deal|autopilot|scan|error, count?}`, which the shell turns into a native notification. `POST /api/notify/test` sends one from Settings. `POST /api/agent/pause` and `/resume` store a flag that `agent.status` reports as `paused`; the scheduler that obeys it is Wave 2.
- **Desktop UI rebuilt as a work place (step 13 W2, Nov 22).** The side-panel column in a window is gone: a rail, page headers, a Deals table with a detail pane, Review as master and detail, Activity with Autopilot Undo, Settings in the spec's two-part rows (design.md, Desktop app). The UI talks to the agent through one typed client in `desktop-ui/src/api/`; `types.ts` mirrors the step 13 contract outline until W1's `core/src/api/` replaces it, and every field the outline lacks is marked "UI asks" there. `VITE_MOCK=1` runs it on fixture data (`?mock=offline|chrome-closed|limit|scanning|paused|empty|error|firstrun|slow`). Detail's "Parts sell for" is the counted part prices before fees and "After fees" their net; the old app bound both to `netRevenue`. Table columns drop by the table's own width (container queries), so the pane can open on a 1000px window. Skeleton rows are still (no shimmer), keeping Behaviour's rule. Keys are write-only fields. *For v2:* the contract types should come from one place; the mock should be generated from recorded agent responses, not written by hand.
- **Step 13 W5: the extension is the app's paired worker** (Decisions 1, 2 and 4). *Pairing:* "Connect to the Split Finder app" (settings page, and under the side panel's tabs while unpaired) asks `POST /api/pair/request {extensionId}`, shows "Enter nothing — approve code 482 913 in the app.", and claims with `POST /api/pair/claim` every 1.5 s backing off to 10 s until approved, refused, expired (the code lives 10 minutes) or cancelled. The token and the claim secret are in `chrome.storage.local` (content scripts are kept out, as for the SerpApi key), the link state (`agentLink`: unpaired with a sentence, pairing with the code, paired with the last job's time) beside them. The background does the polling so closing the panel does not stop it; a 1-minute alarm resumes a pairing left waiting and starts the job loop. A 401 from any call drops the token at once and the panel falls back to the full standalone view with "The Split Finder app no longer recognises this browser. Connect again." *Why loopback is allowed:* the app on this computer is the product now, and a Chrome extension cannot call `127.0.0.1` without a host permission. `http://127.0.0.1:8790/*` is a plain entry in the production manifest's `host_permissions` (no runtime request, no obfuscation: the base64 constant and the package test that existed to hide the address are gone). It is that one address and port; `package.test.ts` now fails on any other `127.0.0.1` or any `localhost` in the shipped files. The agent is the only thing listening there, and it refuses a call without the pairing token, a wrong Host, or a foreign Origin (W1). *Disconnect* clears the token here; the extension cannot revoke itself in the agent (`DELETE /api/pair/extensions/:id` is the app's), so the app keeps listing the browser until the owner removes it there. *The worker loop:* while paired, each alarm (and each pairing approval) starts one long-poll loop (`GET /api/worker/next?wait=25`); it runs a job at a time, posts the result, sends a heartbeat every 30 s while a long job runs, and stops after 4 minutes with no job, after 3 failed calls in a row (the app is closed) or on a 401; the next alarm starts it again, and a second start while one runs returns the first. Every read goes through one lock around the doorway tab, shared with any scan the extension runs itself, because each reader closes a doorway tab it finds remembered and two at once would close each other's. *Job results* (the contract leaves them open): `page` is the `ReadPages` outcomes in order; `kitPage` is the one outcome of the kit's page; `partPrice` is a `PartPriceReading` (the job names the item id and the model but not the brand, so the product page's own brand is reported and the model is checked against it with `sameModel`; a refusal is `not_read`, not a failure); `search` is the research tool's result for the first page only, and a later page is refused in words (Home Depot paging is not read). A refusal by Home Depot is a result (`refused`, `not_read`), because asking again will not change it; a tab that did not load is a failure, which the agent retries once. *Paired means no extension scans:* the worker's tick starts nothing and Scan now says the app runs the scans; the keys the extension holds stay where they are (nothing pulls keys from the app any more, nothing is sent to it), so Disconnect returns to standalone exactly as before. *Removed:* every `appSync*` module (settings and key pulling, the scan, review, paid, Ask AI and Home Depot read outbox drains, the audit and panel pushes), `agentBridge.ts`, the "send the audit to the local agent after each scan" setting's code (the field stays in `ExtensionSettings` because the agent's settings still carry it), `appPrimaryUi`, the helper-build redirect from settings to the app, and two end-to-end specs for them. *The paired panel* shows the bundle in the active tab from `GET /api/deals` (found by its Home Depot item id) and `GET /api/deals/:id`, the open questions of that bundle from `GET /api/review`, answered through `POST /api/review/:id/answer`, and "Connected to the app · last job 2 min ago". Profit is the app's estimate and says so, with the source links the app sent. *Contract gaps:* no endpoint looks up one Home Depot page the app has not scanned, so such a page says so and points at the app; an audit-only bundle has no detail (`GET /api/deals/:id` answers 404), so its row shows alone; the answer to a question is queued ("applied in a moment"), so the panel says it was sent, not that it was applied; the extension cannot focus or open the app (no registered URL scheme), so the panel says to open it from the Dock or Start menu.
- **Step 13 W4: the agent does the work** (Decisions 1, 4 and 5; the PO: "a fully automated background agent that finds deals and messages the owner when something needs review or has a high ROI"). `agent/work.ts`. *The schedule* is Settings' (`manual`, every N hours, or daily at a time; core's `nextRun` counted from the last scheduled start, which is written when the scan starts, so a failing scheduled scan does not start again every tick); a 30 s tick starts a due scan unless the agent is paused or busy; `nextScanAt` is in `GET /api/state` (null when paused or manual). One thing runs at a time: a scan, an AI pass, or a Look up / Verify; a second one is refused with a sentence (409). `POST /api/scan` and `/api/scan/stop` are the agent's own now (no outbox): Stop ends the scan before its next paid step and aborts the eBay, model or Chrome call in flight, and nothing after the scan (AI, Autopilot, messages) runs. A job left "running" by a crash is continued by the next run (core's runner). *What runs after each scan, without the owner:* the report is rebuilt and stored for Deals and Review (with each open question's stored evidence and what a Look up of each deal would cost); the AI pass asks about every open question that has no suggestion yet (step 12's queue rules, `askQueueOf`); Autopilot; the digest. Progress is core's `pipelineView`, sent in `state` at most once a second. *Records:* the scan's records are `FileScanStore` under `<app-data>/scan/` (core's ScanStore, one JSON file per record kind, written whole and renamed; passes core's store conformance tests), responses in `<app-data>/cache/`. *For v2:* SQLite once a kind outgrows rewriting it whole on every put.
- **Step 13 W4: the budget in the agent** (CLAUDE.md rule 1, changed). The agent spends the owner's own SerpApi key from its store through `SerpApiClient` and core's `SearchBudget`: a fresh (free) account check at the start of every scan and every Look up / Verify, then `budgetPolicyFor` from Settings exactly as the extension does: a scheduled scan keeps the reserve, Scan now / Look up / Verify may spend it, and a job never spends more than `searchesPerScan` across its continuations. Every counted search is appended to `<app-data>/serpapi-ledger.jsonl` (labels `agent:...`, `panel:...`), Keepa's to `keepa-ledger.jsonl`. Keepa runs inside the scan, as in the extension: core's planner splits each run's `keepaTokensPerScan` between discovery and tracking (`discoveryMode`, `keepaDiscoveryShare`) and the agent keeps the last balance Keepa reported; core has no separate tracking loop, so there is no second schedule. Part trends and alert baselines are written to the agent's cache. The store's single-writer lock means one agent per data folder spends. *Never* the development key: in development the agent's data folder is the app's (`desktop/dev-agent.mjs`), and tests give the agent a fake fetch over the recorded responses.
- **Step 13 W4: Home Depot reads are worker jobs with a timeout, and Chrome closed is said, never guessed** (Decision 4). `agent/remoteReaders.ts` queues one job per read and waits for its result (a page batch 90 s plus 20 s a page, at most 15 minutes; a research tool call 90 s); a job not answered in time is cancelled (`WorkerJobs.cancel`, a late answer is refused) and the read fails, which the runner notes on the scan ("Reading Home Depot pages failed: Chrome did not answer in time.") and reads nothing more that run. A result whose shape is not core's is a failed read, never data. When no paired helper is connected at the start of a scan, the scan gets no reader and its rows say "Chrome closed" (core's `ScanDeps.noReaderReason`, additive); the pages stay unread and the next scan with Chrome open reads them, and research on split questions waits likewise. *Part prices* use the jobs the W5 extension runs: Home Depot's search (`search`, page 1), the one result whose own brand and model fields are the part's (exact first, then `sameModel`, so RYOBI's B twin), then that product's page by item id (`partPrice`, which now carries `brand`); the reading must name the part's brand, else it is not found. No result is `not_found`; a refused or failed read makes that part and every later one `not_read` for the run. *Contract addition the extension does not run yet:* `researchPage {itemId}` (the research agent's readHomeDepotPage, sections as `extension/lib/researchTools.ts` returns them); until the worker runs it, that tool answers with an error and research reads Home Depot's search and the stored pages only.
- **Step 13 W4: Autopilot applies only what code verified, and Undo takes it back** (Decision 5; rule 4). The rules, in order (`agent/autopilot.ts`): the question is open and a **split** question (a match answer decides a listing for the part and its RYOBI twins and closes the twin's question, and no code path takes that back, so match questions stay with the owner); the owner has not undone Autopilot on it before; the suggestion is the latest made for the question as it reads now (signature); its status is `verified` (every quote found word for word in the source it names, and a reading the question offered, or research parts named on pages it read): never `unverified`, `cannot_tell`, `none_suggested` or `not_suggested`; the model that made it is the pipeline's model in Settings **and** that setting is `trusted: true`; and `approvalOf` gives an answer the question takes. It is applied through the owner's own `applyReviewAnswer`, the suggestion is marked used, and the Autopilot log keeps what changed ("PCL1600K2-P553 contains PCL1600K2, P553"), the checks it passed with the quotes and their sources, the model, and what Undo needs. `POST /api/autopilot/:id/undo` reopens the question (`reopenReviewAnswer`), shows the suggestion to the owner again, and Autopilot never answers that question again; it is refused once the answer changed since (the owner or a scan answered it). Autopilot is **off by default** for new installs (Settings switch `autopilot`; a store that already has a setting keeps its value). Owner and PO ruling, Nov 20: the PO said "his approval is the answer" and "nothing applies a figure on its own"; the owner asked for "fully automatic with human gated approval". So the AI pass still runs after every scan, and the digest says "N answers verified by AI — approve them in Review" (`agent/alerts.ts`, one line, when the code verified answers that nothing has applied). The owner's one tap, Approve all verified in Review, applies them through the same answer path. Autopilot is an opt-in switch for an owner who wants verified answers applied without the tap; its rules and Undo are unchanged.
- **Step 13 W4: which models Autopilot trusts** (Decision 7). Each pipeline's setting (`ai.judge`, `ai.assist`, `ai.research`: provider, model, key reference, and `trusted`) starts as Claude (`claude-haiku-5-5`, key `anthropic`), trusted, because the step 10 to 12 evals measured it. Switching a pipeline to an OpenAI-compatible provider (DeepSeek's preset) sets `trusted: false` unless the owner sets it; its verified suggestions then wait for the owner like any other. Mark a model trusted only after `judge:eval` / `assist:eval` / `research:eval` were run against it. A model is identified by provider id and model id together, so a suggestion made by another model than the one Settings trusts now is not applied.
- **Step 13 W4: the AI pass and the month's AI cap.** After every scan (not one the owner stopped) the agent asks about each open question with nothing on screen, oldest first: a split question is researched over the remote Home Depot tools (only while Chrome is connected; otherwise it waits and the run says how many wait), a match question is asked from the evidence the scan stored. Results are suggestions, never answers. `ai.monthlyCapUsd` (default $25; 0 turns the AI off, which replaces the extension-era `askAfterScan` switch) holds the judge, the assistant and research together in a calendar month (UTC): a question is asked only when its published-rate estimate still fits under the cap, the eBay step is skipped when its estimate would pass it, and a model with no known price is not asked automatically at all (no guessed cost). The month's AI spend is the suggestions' cost plus each run's judge cost, each counted once (`aiSpentThisMonth`). Three provider failures in a row end the pass with the last sentence.
- **Step 13 W4: one message after a run, and a deal alerts once** (Decision 6). `agent/alerts.ts`: at most one digest per run, the same text as a `notify` event (the shell's native notification) and, when a bot token and chat id are saved, on Telegram (`sendMessage`, POST `https://api.telegram.org/bot<token>/sendMessage` with `{chat_id, text}`, read from core.telegram.org/bots/api on Nov 22, 2026). Lines: "N new deals above X% ROI. Top: BRAND MODEL, est. profit $P (R% ROI). Estimates from your fee settings." (confirmed bundles at or above `alerts.minProfit` and `alerts.minRoiPercent`, best first, each bundle key once: the keys are remembered), "N questions need you." (only when an open question is new since the last message), "Autopilot applied N answers (Undo in Activity).", and Keepa trend lines (a part's Amazon price fell since the last pull; a deal named before whose estimate fell below the numbers and has crossed back up). Alerts start on in the agent. A Telegram failure is a sentence in Activity (no token in it) and the message is sent again after the next run, in order, up to five tries. *Activity:* each run is recorded with its start and end, trigger, steps, searches, Keepa tokens, AI cost, result and stop reason, and errors in one plain sentence, every key scrubbed; an error that can be retried (scan, ask, Telegram) is listed with `POST /api/activity/errors/:id/retry`.
- **Step 13 W4: the owner's answers are applied by the agent, and the extension era's mailbox is gone.** `POST /api/review/:id/answer` applies the answer through core's `answers.ts` (or `applyEbayAnswer`) at once and answers `{applied: true}`; it takes `{choiceId}` as well as `{choice}`, and `seen` (the question's signature the app showed; without it the question as stored now is assumed); a stale or answered question is 409 with core's sentence. Approve, Ask AI (one question or all) and Look up / Verify (quote first with `GET /api/deals/:id/lookup`, then the click) run in the agent. Removed: the outbox routes (`/api/scan/start|pending|ack`, the review, paid and Ask AI queues), `/api/bridge`, `/api/panel`, `/api/hd-read/*`, `POST /api/state` and `/api/helper/settings`, and the settings `appPrimaryUi`, `compactHelperPanel`, `askAfterScan` and `settings.agentBridgeAfterScan` (core's `ExtensionSettings.agentBridgeAfterScan` stays: the unpaired extension still reads it). Added for the app and the extension: `POST /api/pair/revoke` (the app's Disconnect) and `DELETE /api/pair/self` (an extension revokes its own token). Every `data:` line of `GET /api/events` carries `type`, and `state` also the whole state as `state`.
- **Step 13 security fix round (adversarial review, Nov 23).** (1) *One slot, claimed before the first await:* Scan now, the tick, Ask AI and Look up / Verify take `busy` synchronously (`claim`) and free it however they end (`track`), so two clicks or a tick between a Look up's quote and its account check cannot run two paid jobs with two `SearchBudget`s. The owner's answers and Undo run one at a time (`serialAnswer`); while one is applied no tick, Scan now or Look up starts (Ask AI may, as before). (2) *Trust follows the model:* `MEASURED_MODELS` in `agent/autopilot.ts` is the one list of models trusted by default: assist and research `claude-opus-5-5` only (Haiku 5.5's assist runs gave verified "none" answers the key disagrees with; research ran on Opus only), judge both. This replaces W4's "Claude starts trusted": the defaults stay Haiku 5.5 (cheapest) and so start **untrusted** for assist and research, and Autopilot waits for the owner until Opus 5.5 is chosen there or the owner marks Haiku trusted. A settings patch that changes provider, model, baseUrl, preset or id resets `trusted` to the list's answer unless the same patch sets `trusted`. Settings saved before this keep the `trusted` they have. (3) *The shell trusts the port only after a challenge:* `GET /api/hello?nonce=` (no token; 32 to 128 url-safe characters) answers `{proof: hex HMAC-SHA256(token, "split-finder-hello:" + nonce)}`. A release shell sends the token (HTTP calls, the event stream) or injects it into the webview only after a fresh random nonce is answered correctly, every time; otherwise the window loads nothing from the port and says "Another program is using port 8790." (or that the agent is not running), and Open Split Finder rebuilds it once the agent proves itself. Dev builds skip the challenge with one log line. HMAC is written over `sha2` (RFC 4231 test vector in `agent.rs`), the nonce from `getrandom`; both were already in Cargo.lock. *Left:* a webview already loaded keeps its token if the agent dies and something else takes the port later. (4) *Store files:* written to a temp file, fsynced, renamed, and the directory fsynced. A collection that does not parse (or has the wrong shape) is renamed to `<name>.json.corrupt-<time>` and never defaulted over; the agent then starts paused with Autopilot and alerts off, `agent.problem` in `GET /api/state` and an Activity error say so ("Settings could not be read; the agent is paused."; for keys: add them again or restore the file); Resume clears it. (5) *Migration leaves no key behind:* after the import `app-secrets.json` is deleted and the key fields are taken out of `app-settings.json` (retried every start); a store with `keys.json` but no `meta.json` is never imported again (that would bring back keys the owner deleted). (6) *Smaller:* the store lock is a JSON file linked into place whole (never read half-written), touched every 10 s, stale when its pid is gone or it was not touched for 60 s (a reused pid), and a stale one is moved aside by rename and put back if it changed meanwhile; `Store.update` keeps a copy of what it is given; revoking an extension (or pairing it again) ends its open event streams and long polls; at start, worker jobs a previous process left queued or leased fail; after a failed or stopped scan there is no AI pass, Autopilot or digest (a message already waiting for Telegram is sent again), and the run (with the eBay step's judge cost) is saved before the AI pass; switching the schedule on from manual stores `runtime.scheduleFrom`, so the first scheduled scan is one interval later (or the next daily slot), and `nextScanAt` says when.

- **Step 13 W7: the desktop UI reads the agent's contract, and one file turns it into what screens read.** `desktop-ui/src/api/types.ts` no longer mirrors an outline: what is the same on the wire (keys, settings, pairing, answers, activity errors) is re-exported from `core/src/api/contract.ts` through the `@core` alias (vite, tsconfig and vitest all name it), and the few shapes a screen wants differently (a flat row, a labelled price line, the scan's stages) are built from the contract in `api/map.ts` and nowhere else. Mapping rules: *status* `scanning` with `busy: 'ask'` is "asking", an unknown run status is "failed" (never a success); *the scan's stages* are core's `PipelineView` as the agent sends it (labels, count, "about 4 min", fraction), none counted in the UI; *Match* on a Deals row is the agent's own sentence ("2 parts to verify"), not an enum the UI would have to guess; *a part's grade* follows core's `matchState` order (waiting, no listing, inactive, review, then exact/strong = verified, probable = title match); *the estimate* is the agent's `partsSellFor`, `afterFees`, `basis` (which already says it is an estimate) and `countedAtZero`, with the ROI range from the primary way to sell, and a part's own "nets" figure is gone because the contract holds only totals; *why there is no estimate* is worded from the bundle's own status, price and waiting parts; *a suggestion made for a question that has changed since* (its `seen` differs from the question's) is dropped, never shown as verified (rule 4); "none fits" is shown as the AI's view, never verified. The deal detail does not carry its open question, and Autopilot rows carry only a bundle id, so the client also reads `/api/review` and `/api/deals` for the question id and the titles (best effort: a failure there never fails the screen).
- **Step 13 W7: how the UI proves itself to the agent.** Every call sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>`; the token is `window.__SPLIT_FINDER_TOKEN__` (the shell sets it) or, in `npm run ui:dev` only (`import.meta.env.DEV`), `?token=` read once into `sessionStorage` and taken off the address. The agent accepts a token only in that header (`agent/auth.ts` `authenticate`), and `EventSource` cannot set headers, so the event stream is read with `fetch` and a streaming body (`api/sse.ts`): reconnect after 1 s, 2 s, 4 s ... 30 s, and reopen a stream that has been silent for 60 s (the agent pings every 20 s). After a gap every screen reloads, since events were missed; a screen reloads from an event without replacing what the owner is doing (a question keeps its half-typed answer, and an answer is sent with the question's `seen` signature so a question a scan changed under him is refused by the agent and flagged on screen, never applied to other choices). Downloads (CSV) are fetched with the header and saved from a blob, since a plain link cannot carry the token. `npm run ui:dev` proxies `/api` to the agent and presents the agent's own Host and Origin, so the agent needed no change.
- **Step 13 W7: pairing is allowed in the app, on every screen.** The agent raises no event for a new request, but a request is a store change and so a `state` event: the app asks `GET /api/pair/requests` on every `state` event and every 15 s (requests expire without an event). A request shows as one amber band under every page header ("Chrome wants to connect. Code 482 913. Allow it only if Chrome shows the same code." with Allow and Deny); nothing connects until Allow. Settings > Chrome helper lists the paired Chromes (`GET /api/pair/extensions`) with Revoke (`DELETE /api/pair/extensions/:id`). eBay and Telegram keys are two fields each, sent as the pair the agent holds. Settings saves merge edits made within one wait into one patch (the old single timer dropped the first of two quick edits).
- **Step 13 W7: dev wiring.** `npm run desktop:dev` builds the UI, starts the Node agent from source on the shell's own app-data folder (`desktop/dev-agent.mjs`, which was not wired; it skips the start when something already listens on the port and waits until the agent answers, since `tauri dev` waits for the dev URL), then runs `tauri dev`; the shell sees the port open and does not start the packaged sidecar. `npm run ui:dev` (UI with hot reload against the agent) and `npm run ui:mock` (`VITE_MOCK=1`) are for design work in a browser.
