- **Step 13: Ask AI and Research start on Opus 5.5, trusted (owner ruling, Nov 23).** The PO wants a fully automated agent, and Autopilot applies only models marked trusted (Decision 7). Opus 5.5 was measured on the validation runs with 0 wrong verified answers, at about 3 to 8 cents a question with prompt caching, so `ai.assist` and `ai.research` now default to `claude-opus-5-5` and are trusted by default. The judge stays on `claude-haiku-5-5`. The one place is `DEFAULT_AI_MODELS` in `agent/store.ts`; it fills only new settings, so a store that already has `ai` keeps the owner's models and trust, and any model Settings changes to still resets trust unless marked trusted.

- **"Recommend when to buy": signals against a stored baseline, and velocity from Keepa's monthlySold** (PO, Keepa plan §3b; Nov 23). `core/src/tracking/signals.ts`: after every finished agent scan each bundle gets a stored point (Home Depot price, counted Amazon New total, each way to sell's estimate, open Review, slowest part's Keepa figures; 30 days, at most 200), and each *watched* bundle's ways to sell are compared with the last point and the 7-day average of stored points. Signals: `marginUp` (crossed up through both of the owner's numbers), `marginDown` (fell below `alerts.floorProfit`, default the profit threshold), `reviewCleared`, `discoveryDone` (first time confirmed, only when earlier points exist), and `buyNow` = confirmed, no leftovers, at or above both numbers, velocity passed, and improved (marginUp, up at least $1 on the last scan or on the 7-day average) or newly confirmed (Review cleared, discovery done, or first seen with an estimate). Each has a sentence and its numbers. *Velocity:* the slowest part's Keepa `monthlySold` against `alerts.minMonthlySold` (default 50, the lowest figure Keepa shows; a preference call for the owner). A part with no stored figure gives "no sales data" and `buyNow` is false (rule 4): Keepa leaves `monthlySold` out for slow sellers, so "no data" may mean "few sales", which is not guessed either way. *Sales rank is not a gate:* it is carried (from `stats.current[3]`, see the next entry) and charted, but no recording has it yet. Field names are from `fixtures/keepa/product-*.json` (`monthlySold`, `fbaFees.pickAndPackFee`, `referralFeePercentage`, `buyBoxEligibleOfferCounts`, whose eight indexes are not interpreted: only their sum is shown). An identify pull now starts a part's trend too.
- **Every Keepa product pull asks for `stats`; the price is Amazon's offer, else the lowest New offer** (controller, Nov 23: "tracking is blind"). At `price_only` the client sent `history=0` and no `stats`, so a refresh carried no price at all: the Nov 22 recording has no `stats`, `csv` or other current-price field (`competitivePriceThreshold` is not a price on sale). keepa.com/api-docs/product.html (read Nov 23) says `stats` and `history` have "no extra token cost", `offers` costs "6 for every found offer page (contains up to 10 offers) per product, with a minimum of 5", `buybox` 2 per product. So `stats=180` goes at every depth. Tokens per part: `price_only` 1, `price_and_rank` 1 (the same request now: the rank is `stats.current[3]`), `full` up to 13 (offers=20, two pages); `productTokenEstimate` and the extension's depth labels say so. The price is `stats.current[0]` (AMAZON) when Amazon sells it, else `stats.current[1]` (NEW, the lowest marketplace New offer; statistics-object.html), recorded as `amazonPriceSource`. The lowest New offer is at or below what the Buy Box usually shows, so the estimate can only come out lower. The Buy Box itself (`buybox=1`, 2 tokens) is not asked for. Cache keys changed with `stats`, so old cached pulls without a price are not reused. *Recording needed:* `npm run keepa:record` (3 tokens) re-records the product fixture with `stats`; the test uses the recorded product with Keepa's documented `stats.current` shape until then.
- **The watchlist is automatic, the owner's choice holds** (Nov 23). A bundle that is confirmed and discounted at a scan is watched (`by: auto`) and stays watched; `POST /api/deals/:id/watch` sets `by: owner`, which scans never change. Only watched bundles get signals and messages. The digest's deal line is now `buyNow` ("N deals worth buying now. Top: ... <reason>"), not bare ROI, and each bundle, signal and local calendar day is sent once (`tracking.notified`); `marginDown` joins it only with `alerts.marginDownDigest`. The extension's own Chrome-era alerts are unchanged.
- **BOGO is the owner's entry until a recording shows how Home Depot marks it** (PO: "for a BOGO deal, go through each item"; Nov 23). *Superseded the same day by "BOGO is automatic, from Home Depot's own pair listings" below; the owner's entry stays as its fallback.* No recorded listing row (badges are `top rated`, `new`, `exclusive`; `price_badge` is `Special-Buy` or `New-Lower-Price`) or product page marks a product as BOGO; the page's `pricing.conditionalPromotions` is null on both recorded pages, and its shape when set is unknown, so nothing parses it. The one recorded shape is a search's "Featured BOGO" value under "The Home Depot Events" filter (count, `hd_filter_tokens` value `1z24hvk`, link): `bogoFacetOf` reads it, and it says the search has BOGO items, not which. A promotion (`POST /api/deals/:id/bogo`) is the bundle plus `freeCount` items the owner chooses, from the eligible list when he gave one; its estimate is the primary way to sell plus each chosen item's counted parts (a free item is sold whole as its own model unless he lists its parts), cost unchanged. Until he chooses: "BOGO: choose your free tool", "+ free tool not counted yet"; a free part with no counting price says why. Nothing picks a free tool. To read promotions from Home Depot, record (main session): one SerpApi search per brand with `hd_filter_tokens` plus `1z24hvk` (2 searches), and, free in Chrome, one BOGO item's page (`fetch('/p/<itemId>')`) with `conditionalPromotions` set and the eligible free items' list. Scans do not look up free items' parts yet.
- **FBA fee from Keepa is shown beside the estimate, never instead** (Nov 23). `fbaEstimateOf`: the primary way to sell with Keepa's `fbaFees.pickAndPackFee` per counted part in place of the flat fulfillment setting (a part without one keeps the setting), labelled "Amazon's FBA fee estimate (Keepa)". It is still an estimate: pick-and-pack only (no storage or inbound placement), and Keepa's per-product referral percentage is not used. Signals and alerts use the main estimate.
- **Step 14 D2: Settings is a window; the look is written twice** (Nov 23, owner: "native, cooler, where everything lives"). `prefs.rs` opens it; the dashboard's Settings route only opens it. The look (appearance, accent, density) stays in `localStorage` so it applies before first paint and reaches the dashboard window by the `storage` event, and is also PATCHed to `settings.appearance` so the agent holds it; local storage wins on load. The "A deal is worth buying" switch writes both `notifications.kinds.deal` and `alerts.enabled`, and shows on only when both are, because the agent treats either one off as no message. A pipeline's `trusted` and `apiKeyRef` are taken from the agent's answer after a save (it resets trust when the model's identity changes, rule 5 of Autopilot); the model and base URL text are not put back, so typing is not undone. A field that fails its check saves nothing and says why under itself. Missing endpoints, so no control: Telegram test, Reset scan data, Export audit, Restart agent, agent and Node versions; the agent keeps no log file.

- **Desktop UX critique on the owner's real data, Nov 23** (owner: "is the UI user friendly? ... don't let the info clutter the page"; the daily user, Alex, is a reseller). Method: each screen read as a first-time reader (what the eye lands on, words he would not know, anything said twice, actions more than two interactions away, each `docs/ux-checklist.md` item), on `?mock=real` / `real-ai`, light and dark, Mac and Windows. Decisions:
  - *Match state has one vocabulary and one function* (`lib/matchWords.ts`). The agent still sends core's sentence (`matchState`; it is read, never reworded by guess: an unknown sentence is shown as the agent wrote it, rule 4). "1 title match" means a listing whose title matched but was not confirmed, so it reads "1 listing not confirmed yet" in a row and "1 of 2 listings not confirmed yet" in the inspector, where the part count is known (a row does not carry it; `DealRow` has no part count, and adding one is core's change, not this one's). "inactive" is "not for sale right now". The test runs every `matchState` in the owner's third scan. `menus.ts` still matches the agent's raw sentence for Verify, which is now "Confirm Amazon listings…".
  - *Today has no figure tiles.* Each of the four said again what a list, the sidebar badge or the Agent card says. The first screenful is the best deals with a verdict, then what needs him, with the agent beside them. The verdict line is the agent's `signalNote` when a row carries one (read through a local type: the agent adds the field), else the confirmation state. Never a figure derived here.
  - *Inspector: decision first, then evidence, long things folded* (`components/Fold.tsx`). The verdict's "Why" repeated the estimate box's profit and the scan's confirmation, so it folds (open only when a reason is an attention one); reference prices ("not counted") fold under each part's counted price; BOGO's 5 to 16 choices, Ways to sell and History fold. Fold state is kept per name for the session, as Review's Details is. One primary button per state, chosen in `Detail` (`next`).
  - *Activity:* runs are sentences grouped by day because a table of "Started / Run / Searches / Keepa / AI / Result" asked him to read columns to learn whether a scan went well. Autopilot's raw "checked by the code" lines (which quoted whole Home Depot addresses) fold, and the addresses become "source" links. Rows sort by time in the screen, not by the order the agent sends (the mock's runs arrived 8:51 then 8:52 under "newest first").
  - *Contrast:* measured, not assumed. Apple's secondary label and system blue on white are 3.98 and 4.02:1; the app uses its own `--label-2`, `--accent-text` and `--accent-fill`. This gives up the system's Increase Contrast response for those three; the alternative (the system colours) fails the checklist. `Welcome.css` still fills its button with `--accent` (another agent's file in this round).
  - *Left alone, with the reason:* Scanning, Notifications and Welcome (other agents); the table's selected-row colours (white on the Mac selection blue measures above 4.5:1; the quiet selection is the system's); inline links are under 24px, which WCAG 2.5.8 exempts inside a sentence; the Deals table keeps the 44px row, so the longer "Amazon listings" words cost the title column some width at 1440 (a 196px column).

## How it was built

- **One controller session plans, dispatches and reviews; subagents implement**, routed by model: mechanical work to the cheapest, state, budget and matching to the strongest. Each task is reviewed by an agent that did not write it. *For v2:* the plans in `docs/superpowers/plans/` and their "Where this stands" sections are the trail.
- **Parallel implementers each work in their own git worktree** and are merged by cherry-pick after the controller verifies them. A worktree is cut from the controller's current directory: in step 4 that directory drifted into a task's worktree and the next worktree started from the wrong commit, so the controller stays in the main checkout.
- **Each step ends with an end-to-end QA walkthrough by an agent playing the owner** (owner's request, Nov 10), driving the built extension against the replay server through every flow. The first one, after step 5's reviews had passed, found a blocker (the settings key field could not be reached) and seven major problems that code reviews and per-screen checks had missed.
- **The mockups are the owner's reference and are never edited to fit the build** (Ruling 31). Where the build departs from a mockup, the departure is written here.

- **Desktop sidecar is a Node SEA, not `pkg`** (Nov 23). `pkg` bundled Node 18.5 and is unmaintained, but the agent uses Node 20+ APIs (`AbortSignal.any` in `agent/work.ts`, `agent/aiPass.ts`, `core/src/scan/ebayPass.ts`), so scans and Ask AI would have crashed in the packaged app. `desktop/bundle-agent.ts` now bundles `desktop/agent-entry.ts` with esbuild (cjs, target node24), makes a blob with `node --experimental-sea-config`, copies the Node that runs the script (`process.execPath`) to `src-tauri/binaries/agent-dashboard-<triple>`, removes its signature, injects the blob with `npx postject` (sentinel fuse `NODE_SEA_FUSE_fce680ab2cc467b6e072b8b5df1996b2`, Mach-O segment `NODE_SEA`) and ad-hoc signs it. The sidecar therefore runs the Node you build with; build with Node 22 or later. The agent reads no file relative to itself: the shell passes `SPLIT_FINDER_DATA_DIR`, `SPLIT_FINDER_UI_DIR`, `SPLIT_FINDER_EXTENSION_ZIP` and `SPLIT_FINDER_EXTENSION_DIR`, so the layout inside the SEA binary does not matter. Startup logs the Node version. *For v2:* the Tauri app is not notarized; a Developer ID signature on the sidecar is needed to distribute it.

- **Step 14 D1: the desktop app leaves the extension's design system for a native one** (Nov 23, owner: "make this app more native … where everything lives … I want options"). docs/design.md > Desktop app is now the macOS spec; the extension keeps paper/ink/Barlow. Choices the code does not explain: (1) **Vibrancy needs `macOSPrivateApi`.** The sidebar material is the window's `Effect::Sidebar` behind a transparent webview, and a transparent WKWebView needs the private `drawsBackground` API, so `tauri.conf.json` sets `macOSPrivateApi: true` and the crate has `macos-private-api`. This rules out the Mac App Store, which this app was never headed for; drop it if the sidebar is made opaque. (2) **System colours come from WebKit, not from Rust.** `-apple-system-label`, `-control-accent`, `-selected-content-background` and the rest track the Mac's appearance, accent and Increase Contrast with no bridge; `light-dark()` literals stand in elsewhere, so the mock UI still renders in Chromium. (3) **Profit green and loss red are darkened in light mode** (`color-mix` toward black) because system green on white is about 2:1 for 13px text; dark mode uses the system colours as they are. (4) **The look (appearance, accent, density) is browser storage, not agent settings**: it is a per-Mac display preference and needs no agent round trip; D2's Preferences window should write the same `splitFinder.look` key through `lib/appearance.ts`. (5) **Traffic lights at (20, 26)** centre them in the 52px toolbar by Tao's arithmetic (container = 14 + y, buttons 7pt above its bottom); the first real-window check should confirm it. (6) **The first run is a sheet over the live app**, opened once by itself and again from the sidebar or Help; skipping writes the existing `splitFinder.firstRunSkipped` flag, and "Finish setup · N of 3" stays until Chrome is paired, the key saved and a scan run. (7) **Deals views are client-side filters** (`lib/views.ts`) on the rows the agent already sends; "Losing money" counts only an estimate below zero, never a bundle still waiting for an answer. (8) **Loading is a spinner now**, reversing step 13's "no spinner" rule for the desktop only, because a Mac app shows NSProgressIndicator where a web page would show skeleton rows.
- **Step 14 D4: the menu bar icon opens a popover; the count has one home.** A left click on the tray icon toggles a borderless window (Popover material, floating, on every Space) under the icon, placed from the click's tray rect; a right click keeps the tray menu. It hides on losing focus; a tray click within 300 ms of that blur-hide is the click that closed it, so it does not reopen. The popover is the same dashboard page at `#/popover` with the same token, and may call one shell command, `popover_do` (hide, open the main window at a screen, Preferences, quit); everything else goes through the agent's API. The window-state plugin denies its label, and the popover taking focus no longer counts as the owner clicking a notification. It follows the event stream only while on screen (the shell says when it shows or hides it), so a hidden one costs the agent nothing. Dock badge: the open-question count through `set_badge_label`, from the same state events as the tray menu, none at 0 or with the Dock icon hidden. Tray title text: the count shows as text beside the icon only when the Dock icon is hidden (then it has no other glanceable home); with the Dock icon shown the badge carries it and the menu bar icon stays a plain icon, the macOS norm. "Best deal" is the best estimate of what the last scan holds because deal rows have no first-seen date; "new" would be a guess. Preferences is reached through the shell's `settings` menu item, so the Preferences window needs no change here. Unverified until the DMG runs: placement on a second screen with another scale factor, and whether `document.hasFocus()` is true at once when the popover is first shown.

- **Step 14 D3: the app confirms a price through the agent, with the same core rules as the extension** (Nov 23). `POST /api/deals/:id/parts/:part/price` takes `{kind: 'ebay-listing', url}` (a stored eBay listing of that part, counted at its own price; a price sent beside it must match) or `{kind: 'typed', price, url}`, plus an optional note; `DELETE` removes it. The planner is `planConfirmedPrice` in `core/src/scan/confirmed.ts`, so the app and the extension refuse the same cases: a part in Review (answer its question first), a part not looked up, a part an Amazon price already counts (unless he already confirmed one, to change or remove it), an eBay link that is not stored. `ConfirmedPrice` and the `confirmed` price option gain an optional `note`; where a price came from ("eBay", "Facebook") is read from the link's host and the link itself is never read. A kit item with no model number is addressed by its `~name`. No Watch or Unwatch menu items: the agent has no such endpoint. The row menu's Verify opens the deal, since a row has no part list and Verify is per part with its own price on the button.
- **D5: the desktop UI for the buy signals** (PO; Nov 23). (1) *Views come from the rows, not from `GET /api/watchlist`:* Worth buying now is `DealRow.buyNow`, Watchlist is `DealRow.watched`, both counted in the sidebar from the one deals list, so a watch or a scan moves them with the same `deals` event; the watchlist endpoint (thresholds, per-row signals) is not read by the app yet. (2) *The UI never works a verdict out* (`lib/signals.ts`): the agent's `buyNow`, `notBuyNow` and signal sentences are shown as sent (with a real minus), and only the lines built from its numbers are worded here (margin against the last scan and the 7-day average, velocity). No sales figure is "no sales data", never slow or fast. (3) *The watch star is a toggle in the first column,* so a glyph in a row is a control; the sidebar uses the same star for the Watchlist. (4) *BOGO is created by the owner:* the deal menu and the inspector offer Mark as BOGO, since nothing detects it; the sheet picks from the eligible list when there is one, else takes a model (and a title and link if he has them). It does not enter an eligible list, a free item's parts or the free count (the contract takes them; no screen asks yet). The BOGO estimate is shown in its own section beside the main estimate, which stays the bundle alone. (5) *History is stored points only,* drawn with gaps where a scan had no figure; a chart appears only when two scans have a figure for it, and Sales rank only when a scan stored one (no recording has one). (6) *Best deals no longer lists a loss;* Worth buying now replaces it on Today whenever any bundle has the verdict. (7) *Mock values are invented* (`desktop-ui/src/api/mockTracking.ts`: Keepa figures, history, FBA fees, the eligible list and its prices); the mock lets DCK240C2 and DCK225D2 leave nothing unsold so the verdict can show. *For v2:* a tracking screen could read `/api/watchlist` and its thresholds to say what a number was judged against.
- **Windows look for the desktop app** (Nov 23; Alex uses Windows). One stylesheet, `desktop-ui/src/windows.css`, scoped to `<html data-platform="windows">`, re-tokens the macOS build instead of forking components; `lib/platform.ts` decides the platform once from the user agent (then client hints, then `navigator.platform`; anything else is `other` and keeps the Mac look with Ctrl labels). The wording differences (Settings, taskbar, notification area, File Explorer, Exit, PC) live in one `Words` table there, and every shortcut label goes through `formatShortcut`. Choices the code does not explain: (1) *Mica is a toggle, not a guess:* without `backdrop: 'mica'` from the shell the window paints the solid Mica base colour, so a window that could not get Mica still looks finished; the sidebar and toolbar are transparent in both cases. (2) *Selection is a tint with an accent pill* rather than the macOS solid accent, because Windows list selection is quiet; the solid-accent rules in `Table.css` and `Review.css` are overridden, not edited. (3) *The popover's "Best deal" reuses Today's `bestDeals`*, so an estimate below zero is never a best deal in either place; with only losing bundles it says "No deal above zero yet". (4) *The Exit row has no key hint* on Windows: the app does not bind one. *For v2:* the app icon is `desktop/icon/app-icon.svg` (tauri icon regenerates `src-tauri/icons`); the menu bar template icon `tray-template.png` is its own artwork and was not touched.
- **BOGO is automatic, from Home Depot's own pair listings** (PO: the background agent finds deals and messages him; owner: "if anything can be automated somehow, do it"; Nov 23). This replaces "BOGO is the owner's entry" above except as a fallback. *What the recording shows* (`fixtures/homedepot-pages/bogo-2026-11-23.json`, read free in the owner's Chrome): a starter's page carries the promotion in `pricing(...).conditionalPromotions[]` (`experienceTag` BOGO, `subExperienceTag` buyOneGetOne, a reward tier at `rewardPercent` 100, `dates.end` as MM/DD/YYYY, `description.shortDesc`), but the free tools it may be paired with are not on the page (`omsSkus` null). Home Depot does list every starter + free tool pair as an item of its own at exactly the starter's price (RYOBI `PCL1201K2-PCL515B` $179, was $268 = `PCL1201K2` $179; DEWALT `DCK200D1P1W921B` $299, was $538 = `DCK200D1P1` $299). So the pairs carry the list the page lacks. *The rule* (`core/src/tracking/bogoPairs.ts` `findBogoPairs`): within one brand's Featured BOGO listing, a starter is an item with exactly one such promotion and a price; a pair is an item with no promotion of its own whose model is `<starter><joiner><free>` for exactly one starter (joiner `-` for RYOBI, `W` for DEWALT) and whose price equals that starter's to the cent. Anything else stays a normal bundle with no BOGO claim (rule 4). The free model is the text after the joiner as Home Depot writes it (`921B`, `B606`, `PCL515B`), never a model worked out from it; what the free item is comes from the pair's own page through the normal split. *A "free" item may be a battery:* `DCK540D1WDCB606` is the 5-tool kit plus a DCB606 battery, and with a DCK540D1 starter in the listing it is a pair by the rule; that is fine, since a pair is priced like any bundle, part by part. On the recorded first page DCK540D1 itself is not listed, so its seven pairs and `DCK316ST2M1W60B` make no claim until a later listing page shows their starter. *The scan* (`agent/work.ts` `bogoPass`, only while Chrome is paired): per brand in Settings, the listing (`/b/Featured-BOGO/N-5yc1vZ1z24hvk/Ntk-elasticplus/Ntt-<the scan's query>?NCNI-5`, then `&Nao=<items read so far>` until a page brings no new item id, at most 15 pages; the recording held 12 items a page, so stepping by what was read covers every item whatever the page size), then each item's page in jobs of 25, each page cached for the owner's local day (`homedepot:bogo-item:<id>`). About 10 listing pages per brand every scan (102 RYOBI, 111 DEWALT items listed) and about 213 item pages on the first scan of a day, none on later ones; 300 item pages at most per scan. Two new worker jobs read them (`bogoListing`, whose path must be the recorded address, and `promoPage`); the listing is read as a search page is, from its `base-searchNav-<id>` entries (the recording's listing rows are summarised, so that key is the search page's, recorded in research-2026-11-20.json; a listing that gives none finds no pair, never a wrong one). A refusal ends the pass; a brand not read in full keeps the pairs found before; a pair whose promotion ended goes. *A pair is a bundle:* it is put in the scan store with its price, was-price and the page the pass read, and the pipeline splits, matches and estimates it like a listing row (Amazon lookups spend searches under the same budget as any bundle). Its deal carries `bogoPair`; pairs are grouped by promotion and starter (`bogoGroups`), ranked by each pair's own estimate, the label "BOGO: N free-tool choices — best: <free> +$X est.". *Messages:* once per promotion and local day, the digest names the best confirmed choice at or above both alert numbers ("BOGO: buy DCK200D1P1 and choose 921B (DCK200D1P1W921B) — +$X est., ends Jan 31."; the pair model is in the line because that is the item he buys); a pair behind its group's best is left out of the "worth buying now" line, the best keeps it. *The owner's entry* (`POST /api/deals/:id/bogo`, the sheet, Mark as BOGO…) stays only for a promotion with no pair listings: the agent refuses it on a pair or on a starter with pairs, and the UI says so. *For v2:* if Home Depot ever fills `omsSkus`, the eligible list could come from the starter's page instead.
- **BOGO pairs never crowd out the other bundles** (controller, Nov 23). About 180 pairs join the store, and each needs Amazon lookups. So the scan pays for them last (`core/src/scan/runner.ts` `pairLookupOrder`, given the pairs and their groups by the agent as `ScanDeps.bogoPairs`). Every part some other bundle holds keeps its place and comes first. Then come the parts only pairs hold, group by group (the group with the largest saving first), pairs by Home Depot's own saving (was - price, largest first). At most 3 *new* pairs per group a scan: a pair is new when it needs a part no earlier step looks up. A pair whose parts are matched or already planned (a free part another bundle or group holds is looked up once) costs nothing and is not counted. The parts of pairs past the cap are left out, not deferred, so later scans continue down the list. Refreshes and kit items that only pairs hold also go after the others, uncapped. The BOGO choices section says "N more choices waiting for a lookup" (`BogoGroup.waitingLookup`, the pairs whose row is `waiting`). The live DEWALT listing (read by the controller in Chrome) holds 12 `base-searchNav-<id>` entries a page in the recorded order (Nao 24/48/96 give 12 each, Nao 2400 none), and its state script goes on after the JSON object; `pageState` parses only the object, tested with a trailing `;window...`.
- **SerpApi's BOGO filter token is not applied** (Nov 23; 2 searches spent to learn it, `fixtures/recorded/hd-bogo-bundles-*-p1.json`). A Home Depot search with `hd_filter_tokens` `1z24hvk` (the "Featured BOGO" value) returned the same rows as the unfiltered bundle search, and the first row's page has no promotion. Nothing sends the token; `bogoFacetOf` stays only as a count of BOGO items in a search. The listing is read free in Chrome instead.
- **Keepa's Amazon price (`stats.current[0]`) is empty in all three recorded products, so the lowest-New fallback is needed** (Nov 23, `fixtures/keepa/product-B0BGYFTPGK-B01H9BL8EO-B09M3TL9BB-stats.json`, 3 tokens). PCL235B, DCB606 and DCF921B each have `current[0]` -1 and `current[1]` set: $44.89, $105.96, $154.99 (monthlySold 500, 1000, 600). Without the fallback every refresh of these parts would carry no price. The test now runs on this recording; the hand-built stats block is gone, as it covered no case the recording does not.
- **Windows installer: cross-compiled on the Mac, Node SEA sidecar, unsigned for now** (Nov 23; Alex uses Windows, the owner keeps the Mac DMG). Step 13 left Windows packaging out; this is it. (1) *Cross-compile, not a Windows CI runner:* `npm run desktop:build:windows` follows Tauri's "Build Windows apps on Linux and macOS" (cargo-xwin, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, LLVM's clang-cl and lld-link, NSIS's makensis), so one Mac builds both installers; only NSIS can be built this way (no MSI), and cargo-xwin fetches Microsoft's CRT and SDK on first use. (2) *The sidecar stays a Node SEA:* the official `node-v24.13.0-win-x64.zip`, from nodejs.org only, checked against the release's SHASUMS256.txt and a hash pinned in `desktop/bundle-agent.ts`; the blob is portable (no code cache, no snapshot) but must come from the same Node version, so the build refuses another. node.exe's Authenticode signature is removed before injection (`desktop/pe.ts`), because an injected file under Node's signature reads as tampered, while an unsigned one is only unknown. (3) *Unsigned:* SmartScreen shows "More info, Run anyway" (`docs/install.md`). Signing needs a code-signing certificate (Azure Trusted Signing or an OV/EV cert) and `bundle.windows.signCommand`; the sidecar would be signed too. (4) *Per-user NSIS install, WebView2 by the download bootstrapper:* no admin prompt, data in `%APPDATA%` beside it. (5) *Owner-only on Windows is an ACL:* Node ignores 0600, so `agent/winAcl.ts` runs `icacls <tmp> /inheritance:r /grant:r *<user SID>:F` on the empty temp file before the content goes in (the rename keeps the ACL); a failure is logged once and the file keeps %APPDATA%'s per-user ACL. (6) *Quit over HTTP:* a windowless child gets no SIGTERM, so the shell posts `/api/agent/quit` (app token) and kills after 3 s. (7) *No menu bar on Windows:* the Mac's application menu is not attached; Ctrl shortcuts must come from the page. (8) *Mica only where it exists:* the shell checks the build (22000+) with RtlGetVersion and only then makes the webview transparent and tells the page `backdrop: 'mica'`; Windows 10 gets an opaque window. *For v2:* sign both executables; a Windows ARM64 build would need the arm64 Node zip and `aarch64-pc-windows-msvc`; consider the OS credential store (Windows Credential Manager, macOS Keychain) for keys.
- **Nov 23 end-to-end fix: the doorway proves its own page, an unread page is never a question, Read again is free, and an old helper is told apart** (the owner's first real scan in the installed app: 3 searches found 16 bundles; the first fetch in the doorway answered 200 without `window.__APOLLO_STATE__=`, was called `refused`, every other id was skipped, and all 16 became "What is inside the ...?" questions with 0 estimates; two minutes later a normal tab read the same ids in full). (1) *Doorway:* after the tab loads and settles, `waitForDoorwayState` reads the tab's own document (its scripts, from the ISOLATED world, which cannot see page variables) every 500 ms for at most 15 s; no other page is fetched until it shows the state, and a doorway that never does is the refusal (`refused`, its title as `note`). A later 200 without the state is `unreadable` with its `<title>` (80 characters) as `note`, not a refusal: reading goes on and stops after two in a row (the run carried across batches; `skipped` after it also ends the run's kit pages and part prices). 403, 429, 5xx, a thrown fetch still stop at once; still no retry, no second doorway, no other headers. The note reaches the job result and the run's sentence ("... could not be read (it came back without its data, titled "...")."). The part price readers keep the old in-tab rule (the defaults stop at the first such page). (2) *waiting_page:* with `ScanDeps.waitForPages` (the agent sets it; the command line, which reads no page, does not), a bundle whose own page is not read and whose split is not resolved opens no question; an open one a scan opened is withdrawn (closed as `RESOLVED_BY_SCAN`, so a read page that still leaves it uncertain asks again). The owner's answer is never touched; nothing becomes resolved (rule 4). `BundleRow.waitingPage`, `DealRow.matchState: 'waiting_page'` and `matchKind`, `needsAnswer: false`; the UI lists it under Waiting for a lookup. *Migration:* at start the agent runs `withdrawUnreadSplitQuestions` once; the next scan does the same in `splitOne`. (3) *Read again* (`POST /api/scan/read-pages`): the same scan with no listing page and no Amazon brand page (`queries: []`, `catalogPagesPerScan: 0`), BOGO and the Chrome steps as usual, and a budget of what the last scan left of `searchesPerScan` (the two count as one scan in `lastScan.searchesUsed`); refused in words while busy, with Chrome closed or nothing unread. `lastScan.pagesUnread` counts stored bundles with no page; `canReadPagesAgain` is computed live. (4) *Helper build:* `wxt.config.ts` bakes `version_name` = package version + `git rev-parse --short HEAD` (`SPLIT_FINDER_BUILD_ID` overrides); the helper sends it in a heartbeat when its loop starts and with every job heartbeat; the agent reads the same field from the shipped zip's `manifest.json` (a small central-directory reader in `setup.ts`, no program run). Another build, or an answer "does not know that kind of job" (the old helper's own sentence, or `outdated: true`), makes `helper.outdated`; that job fails at once (not handed out again) with "The Chrome helper is older than this app ...", never a Home Depot failure, and one `reload` job is queued per outdated build (`chrome.runtime.reload()`; an unpacked install reloads the files in its folder, so a folder still holding the old build stays outdated and is not asked twice). `POST /api/helper/reload` queues one, never two waiting. (5) *Sentences:* a missing model key reads "No Anthropic key yet — add it in Settings › Keys." (the service named per provider/preset); the AI pass leads with "AI answers skipped: ..."; the run summary leads with the outcome ("Found 16 bundles; 0 estimated because their Home Depot pages were not read — Read again (free)."). *For v2:* the doorway check could parse the doorway's own document instead of fetching its page again; an outdated helper could be re-installed from the zip before the reload.

- **UI polish pass: one notice, a quiet row, plain words** (Nov 23; the owner: "don't let the info clutter the page ... professional, what a real principal UX dev would do"; reviewed against docs/ux-checklist.md). (1) *One notice region per screen, at most one banner* (`lib/notices.ts` holds the order; `NoticeRegion` draws it): Deals mid-scan stacked a scanning line, a lookup notice and the eBay warning, 135px above the table. The order is what he must act on first: offline, a pairing request, an outdated helper, a scan that left work undone, setup that stops a scan, a rejected key the screen uses, waiting lookups, a stale price. The rest wait behind "N more" in a popover with their actions, so nothing is hidden, only not shouting. The lookups notice is hidden while a scan runs: its button was disabled "until the scan has finished" and the scan is doing the lookups. The eBay key warning left Deals: eBay values are not counted in the estimate and the key is fixed in Settings; the sidebar's problem line, Today's Agent card (new "Keys" row) and Settings › Keys carry it. (2) *A deal row's reason line is drawn only when it adds something* (`lib/dealReason.ts`): the profit and the match state are columns, so repeating them as a third line on every row (and reserving its height blank) was the clutter. It now says what a hidden Match column would, a waiting state's full words, or the BOGO group's sentence; a row without one is the two-line 44px row. The width that hides Match is measured in the Deals screen (ResizeObserver on the list, the same 720px as the column's container query). Not built: "sells ~120 a month" on the row, because the row carries no sales figure (it is in the inspector's signals); the agent's DealRow would have to send it. (3) *Plain words for "Why it is asked"* (`lib/questionWords.ts`): each known note the split code writes (`'X' matches nothing in the catalog`, `hyphen split, but a part is not in the catalog`, `page names no models`, `no known <brand> bundle pattern`) becomes one sentence for the owner, from the bundle's brand and model; the original is under "Technical detail", collapsed. An unknown note passes through unchanged (rule 4: never guess); the unit test reads every note in `fixtures/e2e-2026-11-23/store/review.json`, so a new note the agent starts sending shows as plain agent text, not as a wrong sentence. The deal inspector's "Needs your answer" notice says the same plain reason. "Amazon's list of RYOBI tools" is the owner's wording for the catalog. (4) *Review rows: a green Verified chip only for a code-verified answer; "AI suggested" quiet text for an unchecked one; nothing otherwise.* "Not verified" on all 15 rows said the same thing 15 times. (5) *The sidebar status is four lines at most* (`lib/sidebarStatus.ts`), one of them a problem that links to where it is fixed. (6) *Targets of at least 24px by an invisible hit area*, not larger controls: AppKit's 22px push button and 20px small button keep their look and the toolbar its rhythm; the 18px accent swatches are left (WCAG 2.5.8 spacing exception: their 7px gaps keep every 24px circle clear of its neighbours). (7) *Windows*: Settings is the last navigation item, pinned to the bottom (Microsoft NavigationView), and a sheet's primary button is first (`SheetActions`; the DOM order follows what is drawn, so Tab and the eye agree). A Mac keeps its order. (8) A rejected key in Settings › Keys is amber, not the loss red: design.md keeps rust for a loss. Other error text across the app (field errors, Activity's failed result, sheet errors) is still `--loss`; changing them all is a design call left to the owner.
- **Nov 23: every doorway reader waits for the doorway's state, and "no more are made" follows only a real refusal** (the installed app's first background part-price read, on a cold tab: "Home Depot refused the search (HTTP 200); no more are made for this question." The morning's fix gave only the bundle page reader the wait for the doorway's own state; the search and part readers still fetched at once and called an empty 200 a refusal, which also stopped the rest of the question). One shared path in `extension/lib/hdReader.ts`: `withDoorway(env, startUrl, {settleMs, doorwayStateMs, signal?}, run)` closes a doorway a stopped worker left, opens and remembers the tab, waits for the load, settles, runs `waitForDoorwayState` (the tab's own document holds `window.__APOLLO_STATE__=`, polled every 500 ms for at most 15 s) and only then calls `run`; a doorway that never shows its state returns `{shown: false, title}` and `run` is never called. `createUnreadableRun()` counts 200 answers without the state (two in a row stop; a page read resets it). Used by the bundle and promo page readers, the part price reader (search then product page), the item price reader, the BOGO listing reader and the research tools; none keeps its own copy of the open/settle/close code. Search pages need no other test: recorded search pages (`fixtures/homedepot-pages/research-2026-11-20.json`, `part-search-2026-11-18.json`) and `parseSearchText` use the same marker as product pages, so "has state" is the same test everywhere. The in-tab fetches (`fetchPagesInTab`, `fetchSearchesInTab`, `fetchPathInTab`, which replaces `fetchListingInTab` and the research tools' own search fetch) must be self-contained and so each carries its own small copy of the marker and title code. *Per reader:* the part price reader treats a search or product page that is 200 without the state as that part `not_read` (never `not_found`: before, a search page with no product link was "no result", and a cold empty page looked the same) and goes on; two in a row (searches and pages counted in the order read) stop it, and the parts after are `not_read`; a doorway that never shows its state leaves every part `not_read`. The item price reader says `not_read` for a doorway that never shows its state and for an empty 200. The BOGO listing says "Home Depot refused the BOGO listing (its own page never showed its data, titled "...")" for the doorway never showing and "The BOGO listing came back without its data, titled "..."" for an empty 200. The research tools say, for an empty 200, "The page for item N came back without its data, titled "..."" (or "The search came back ...") and make the next call; two in a row end the question's Home Depot calls with their own sentence ("answered two requests in a row without their data"); only a 403, 429, 5xx, a failed fetch, or a doorway that never shows its state set the refusal that answers "no more are made for this question". Pacing, concurrency and the no-retry rule are unchanged. *For v2:* the part reader's `PartPriceReading` has no note field, so the page title of an empty answer is not kept for parts; the helper could read the doorway's own document in place of the first fetch.

- **Undo for the owner's own answers keeps the records it replaced, not a re-split** (Nov 23; ux-checklist item 3, HAX G9). Autopilot's Undo (`reopenReviewAnswer`) re-splits the bundle and handles split questions only. The owner's Undo has to put back "exactly as it was" for both kinds, so `agent/ownerAnswers.ts` snapshots, before `applyReviewAnswer`, the records the answer writes (the question, a match question's twin questions, the split question's bundle split, the match records) and again right after. Undo compares the records now with the second snapshot; if any differs (a later scan rewrote the bundle, a newer answer) it refuses in one sentence and marks the entry not undoable, so Activity stops offering it. Otherwise it writes the first back (only the bundle's split, never its price), clears the used AI suggestion's outcome so Review shows it again, and rebuilds the report. A match record the answer created where none existed cannot be removed (no delete in `ScanStore`), so that entry is kept without Undo. The newest 50 are kept in the store collection `answers` (`by: 'owner'`), apart from `autopilot` so Autopilot counts and alerts do not change. A question he took back is left alone by Autopilot. `POST /api/review/:id/undo` takes the answer entry id (`AnswerResponse.answerId`), not the question id. In Review the row "Saved · Undo" is inline at the top of the list for 8 seconds (held while hovered, focused, busy or showing an error), Cmd/Ctrl+Z does the same while Review is open and focus is not in a text field, and only for the answer in that row. eBay yes/no answers have no entry.

- **Review's answer pane is a fixed decision card, written once and shown the same way every time** (the owner, Nov 23, on a screenshot of the pane for PCL406424SB: "too much information ... I don't know what I am supposed to be looking at ... use AI to summarize ... a schema that makes it easy for Alex to understand, and extra details in a drop down"; Alex is the PO, a reseller). *What was wrong:* the pane showed everything the agent knew in the order it was stored: an intro block, the AI's state, its reason, up to a dozen quotes, why the question was asked, a technical note and more quotes: over 1,000px of text above and below a three-row choice (the screenshot is 1,064px high at 2x and still scrolls). *Decision:* one schema, `ReviewBrief` (ask, suggestion, state, at most three reasons, one check, writtenBy), drawn the same way for every question (`screens/ReviewCard.tsx`): header, card, choices, one Details disclosure, footer. The agent fills `brief` (AI-written when it can, rules otherwise); the UI takes it only in the promised shape (`readBrief` in `api/map.ts`) and otherwise derives its own with `briefFromQuestion` (`lib/reviewBrief.ts`), so the pane never has an empty card and an older agent works. *Rule 4 in the derivation:* the suggestion is the AI's own reading and nothing else; what a part is (kit, tool, battery, charger) is said only when a catalog quote says "(kit)" or the AI's own reason says it right after the model, else the part is its model number alone; a line is the AI's sentence cut at a claim boundary (`;`, an em dash, ", and", ", but", ", so", ", which") or at a word with an ellipsis, never at a bare comma (a comma splits a list: "the 65-piece drill and impact drive kit" must not become "the 65-piece drill"), parentheticals dropped first, never reworded; for "could not tell" the line is the AI's last clause ("..., so the exact tool-only model numbers inside it could not be confirmed" says the missing thing). Check is the first unmet thing in the agent's own plain "Not verified" sentence, with the Home Depot or Amazon page. *Nothing was deleted:* the evidence quotes, the AI's full reason, what the code checked, why it was asked with the technical note, the page's own lines and the cost moved into Details, grouped by source with the source link above its lines (every claim keeps a labelled link); the disclosure's open state is kept for the session (sessionStorage). *Also:* the first-use AI explanation is a one-line dismissible footnote under the card, not a block above it (ux-checklist: say before the first suggestion what the AI does and that it can be wrong; the shorter line keeps both halves); the suggested choice is marked "Suggested" in words as well as being selected; list rows are a model number and two to four words ("Suggested: 2 parts", "AI could not tell", "Needs a listing"), never a long title; the inspector minimum is 420px, not 440px. *Measured* (Playwright, throwaway, light and dark, Mac and Windows user agents, 1280x900): the default pane of all ten real questions is 459 to 648px high of 847 to 848 available at 420px wide, so none scrolls; with Details open it scrolls, which is what a disclosure is. `?mock=real-ai` is `?mock=real` with the AI answers the second scan recorded for the same questions plus the owner's real "could not tell" on PCL406424SB. *Not done, on purpose:* no attempt to word the reasons better than the AI did (that is the agent-side brief's job, with the whole question in hand); the card shows no confidence figure (a category tied to an action, not a score); the Windows selected row's state text was white on a pale selection and now takes the secondary label.
- **The Review brief: a three-line summary above each question, written by rules or by a cheap model, and every sentence of a model's checked by code** (step 16; the owner reads three plain lines before a wall of page quotes). (1) *It explains a suggestion and never changes it* (rule 4): `ReviewQuestion.brief` (`core/src/brief`) has the question, the suggestion in plain words, a `state` (verified / not_verified / cannot_tell / none), at most three reasons each with its quote and link, one thing to check, and `writtenBy`. The state, the proposed answer, every quote text and every link are copied from records the code holds; the model writes only sentences. The answer, its status and what Autopilot may apply are never read from a brief. (2) *Rules brief always*: `rulesBrief` is a pure function of the question's note, the suggestion's status and chosen reading, the verifier's plain sentences (`assist/plain.ts`, the ones that already say "Not verified: ...") and the quotes found; no model, no cost; it is what GET /api/review returns until a model brief exists, and for an ebay question or a question with no suggestion. (3) *A separate pipeline, prompt and settings, not the research or assist ones*: their accuracy is measured and Autopilot's trust is tied to them (`MEASURED_MODELS`), so the brief has its own `ai.brief` setting (default `claude-haiku-5-5`, the judge's cheap model; `trusted` is always false), its own prompt version (`review-brief-2026-11-23.1`) and goes through `ModelProvider`, so DeepSeek works by changing the setting. A store older than it reads as Haiku on the Anthropic key. (4) *The model sees only what the code holds*: the question, the proposed answer, the verifier's plain sentences and up to six numbered quotes **the code found word for word in a stored source**. For a verified, cannot-tell or "none fits" suggestion those are the verifier's quotes. For an unverified one they are the AI's claimed quotes (assist `claimed.evidence`, research `researchClaim` quotes) searched in the question's stored lines (the named source first): a quote on no stored line (a search result, a catalog entry, whose text the stored suggestion no longer keeps) is not carried, and the AI's own `reason` is not shown to the model, because it is a claim and not something the code checked. A suggestion with no found quote gets no call. (5) *Grounding* (`core/src/brief/ground.ts`): a reason whose `quoteIndex` is not a quote is dropped; a sentence over its length (110 characters for a reason, 140 for the check, 120 for the suggestion), one with a word of the app's insides ("quote 2", "verified", "JSON", "evidence", "[3]") or an overclaim ("definitely", "proves"), or one with a model number (`[A-Z]{1,5}\d[A-Z0-9-]*` on upper-cased text, hyphenated ones also by their parts) or a number (price, count; number words two to twelve count) that is in neither the cited quote, the question (brand, model, title, the tool's note, the readings or listings), the proposed answer nor the code's own verdict sentences, is dropped. The suggestion in words must name every model of the proposed answer and bring none of its own, otherwise the proposed answer's own words stand; a check that fails is the rules' check. A brief with no surviving reason is the rules brief. (6) *When, and what it costs*: after a suggestion is made (the scan's AI pass, Ask AI and Ask about all all end in `AgentWork.aiPass`, so one hook there covers them) and, for stored live suggestions with no brief, a batch of at most 20 when the agent starts; one call each, no retries. The cost (the model's published rates; null for an unpriced model, which is then not asked, as the AI pass does) is kept on the suggestion as `briefMeta.costUsd` and counted in `aiSpentThisMonth` (the month it was written), so the monthly cap holds it; a call is skipped when the cap would be passed, with no key (silently: the rules brief stands) or with no found quote. A model brief that fails the checks still cost something, is kept as `briefMeta.fellBack`, and is not asked again for a day; a call that gave no answer leaves no mark and is tried at the next start (three in a row end the pass). `scripts/brief-eval.ts` (`npm run brief:eval`, `--dry-run` first) prints each real suggestion's brief with the verdict on each sentence and the total cost. *For v2:* if briefs ever need to be stored apart from suggestions (a brief for an answered question), key them by the question's `reviewSignature` and the suggestion's key; a number in a sentence is checked as a digit string, so "a dozen" would pass as no number, which the tests do not need yet.

- **The brief's model is Opus, its suggestion is one line per part, and a "what it is" word must agree with the title** (step 16 round 2; the owner's 15 real questions, `npm run brief:eval -- --data scan2`, same prompt, no retries). *Measured:* `claude-haiku-5-5` $0.00518 in all, 12 of 15 briefs passed the checks, 17 of 57 sentences dropped, suggestions were wordy ("PCL1201K2 kit + P20015BTL part, as the two parts that make up this bundle") and one check line invented a fact ("confirm P553 is the battery or charger" on a title that says Miter Saw); `claude-opus-5-5` $0.1768 (about 1.2 cents a question), 15 of 15 passed, 3 of 70 sentences dropped, suggestions named each part ("PCL1600K2 kit + P553 miter saw"), checks specific and correct. The card is what the owner reads, so `DEFAULT_BRIEF_MODEL` is `claude-opus-5-5`; Settings (`ai.brief`) can point it at a cheaper one. A store still on the Haiku default (provider anthropic, no address, model `claude-haiku-5-5`) moves to Opus once (`brief-model-opus` in `runtime.migrations`): the owner never chose it, and one who picks Haiku afterwards keeps it. (2) *Tighter prompt and checks, so a cheaper model does better later* (`review-brief-2026-11-23.2`): the suggestion is at most 70 characters, one `<MODEL> <what it is>` entry per part joined by " + ", each opening with a model of the proposed answer, naming what a part is only in the title's, the question's or a quote's words (else the model alone); the check must cite a quote (`quoteIndex` is required; none or an unknown one drops it to the rules' check). `core/src/brief/kinds.ts` keeps a closed vocabulary of tool and accessory words in families (saw, drill/driver, battery, charger, trimmer, light, ...): a family claimed for a model that no source names is refused, and the *last* proposed part is held to the title's last words (Home Depot writes a hyphenated bundle's title in the order of its model, "KIT-EXTRA"): called a battery or charger when the title ends "and Miter Saw", or called a tool the title's last words do not name, it is refused. The check is deliberately strict (a legitimate "multi-tool" on a title that ends "Charger" is not refused, but a title that ends "Charger" refuses "battery"); a refused sentence only falls back to the proposed answer's own words, never to a guess. (3) *The rules brief speaks as the app does*: the "Why it is asked: ..." prefix is gone; the question's note is worded by `core/src/review/words.ts` (moved from desktop-ui, which re-exports it, so the card and the agent share one mapping; a note it does not know is not shown), and the verifier's checker-talk gets owner sentences (`core/src/brief/ownerWords.ts`: "The page's list mixes two items in one line, so the code could not match it.", "The page also lists \"Bag\", which none of these parts accounts for."). Rules sentences may be 140 characters, a model's reasons stay at 110. (4) *The brief is written as each suggestion lands*: `runAiPass` calls `afterKeep` right after it keeps a suggestion for a question still as asked, so the card fills in during a long Ask about all; the brief's cost counts in the pass and in the month's cap; a brief problem (the cap, an unpriced model) is the pass's note and never ends it. The one hook in `agent/work.ts` is `afterKeep: briefAfterKeep(...)`.

### Step 16, lever 4 measured and rejected: a second model's agreement is not evidence (Nov 23 2026)
`npm run crosscheck:eval -- --run` on the recorded runs' 24 unverified answers (19 right per the answer key, 5 not),
claude-sonnet-5-5 deriving the parts from the code-verified quotes alone, cost $0.0611
(fixtures/research/crosscheck-claude-sonnet-5-5.json). Two models agreeing would release 10 right answers and let 3
wrong ones through; it disagreed on 9 right and 2 wrong. The bar is zero wrong, so agreement is used nowhere: not in
Autopilot, not as a "two models agree" label on the card. Why it fails is as predicted in the harness's note: the second
model reads the first model's chosen quotes, and a hyphen model number spells its own split, so the two are not
independent. Answers are released only by code rules measured at zero wrong (step 15 P2, step 16 levers 1 and 3).

### The card never tells him to do what the app can do (Nov 23 2026, desktop-ui only)
The owner, on "Next: On the Home Depot page, see whether the Includes list names the accessories sold as A21701 and
A25RS51": "if I'm told to do this, why can't an AI pipeline do it for me". The card is built against an agent contract that
is being written in parallel (all fields optional; `api/types.ts`): `brief.check.who` ('app' | 'owner'), `followUp`
(queued, running, done, gave_up, with `doing`, `tried`, `roundsLeft`), `counts.reviewWorking`, and `POST
/api/review/:id/ask` answering `{ queued, reason? }` with `force`. Decisions the code does not explain:
- **Only `who: 'owner'` is drawn as Check/Next.** An older agent sends no `who`; then `checkWho` reads the words (read, look
  up, see whether, the Includes list, on the Home Depot page) and calls it the app's. The rules brief tags its own checks
  `who: 'app'`; its "could not tell" Next is now only "Pick the parts below." (it no longer says to open the page).
- **A question the app is still on is not his.** It is out of `counts.review`, out of J/K and the default selection, and in
  a collapsed "The app is still working on N" section under Needs you; a link to one opens the section. Its card is
  neutral ("Working on it", `doing` with a spinner, `tried`), and the choices wait under "or answer it yourself" (a number
  key opens it), so he can still answer early. Done or gave-up, it is his again with "What the app checked" (three lines,
  the rest in Details) above Why; for gave_up the sentence is what is still missing, so he is asked only that.
- **Ask AI is never silent.** The press answers at once: "Checking again…" with the spinner on the row and card, or the
  agent's `reason` on the card. "Ask anyway · about $0.07" (force) is offered only for a reason of the "nothing new" kind
  (`askAi.ts` NOTHING_NEW): the contract does not say which reasons force can change, so any other reason is said without an
  action. The agent side should word that reason so it matches, or add a field. The A key answers too (no key, nothing to
  read, already working) instead of doing nothing. Ask AI is also offered again after "could not tell".
- `?mock=real-ai` shows all four follow-up states on copies of the real PCL406424SB question; what the app "tried" there is
  invented (marked in `api/mockFollowUp.ts`).
### Step 16: the follow-up loop, the app does the reads it used to tell the owner to do (Nov 23 2026)
The owner, on the card for PCL406424SB-A21701-A25RS51 ("Next: On the Home Depot page, see whether the Includes list names
the accessories sold as A21701 and A25RS51"): "if I'm told to do this… why can't an AI pipeline do it for me". Two facts
from the live app: the first ask said cannot tell because its Home Depot search failed on a cold tab, and a second press
of Ask AI returned `{queued: true}` and did nothing (askQueueOf skips a question that has a suggestion).
- *Tasks come from structured records only, never from a sentence* (`core/src/research/followUp.ts` deriveTasks). The
  verifier now returns, beside each refusal a free read could change, a `VerifyGap` (`model_unconfirmed`, `kind_unknown`,
  `kit_contents`, `untied`, `leftover`, `bundle_unread`) on the unverified result's `gaps`; the research trace keeps each
  failed client call with its input (`failedCalls`). Mapping: `model_unconfirmed` → `catalog_lookup` (the stored catalog,
  local) and `confirm_model(brand, model)` (Home Depot search for "<brand> <model>", the one row whose own model field is
  the model, exactly or by sameModel, then its page by item id: the part-price reader's rule, as Chrome jobs);
  `kind_unknown` → `confirm_model`; `kit_contents` (a kit part whose own page is not among the sources) → `read_kit_page`
  (by the catalog's item id, else found as confirm_model finds a page); `bundle_unread`, a `missing:` lack, or no bundle
  page with lines → `read_bundle_page`; a failed call → `tool_failed` (that same call once more; a failed search for
  "<brand> <model>" of a model the question names is left to confirm_model); a `kit:` lack → `read_kit_page`; a model the
  question's readings or the answer name that no ledger product or catalog entry shows → `confirm_model`. `untied` and
  `leftover` have no read that settles them: they are told to the follow-up ask. Each task runs once per question.
  Keepa is not used here: the catalog check already reads Amazon's record for every match question (`catalogCheck.ts`).
- *A round*: the tasks (Chrome reads first, at most six; the catalog is local), each result kept as a ledger entry with its
  link (`FollowUpState.entries`) and a plain line for the card (`tried`: "Searched Home Depot for A21701 — found: …", the
  page's link); (i) the answer checked again by code with no model call, exactly as lever 1 (a model's page or row is a
  `reading`, products only; a kit part's page keeps its lines; the bundle's page joins only when the ask had none); (ii) only
  when still not verified and the evidence key (`followUpKey`: every entry read, an empty search included, a failed read not)
  is one no follow-up ask saw, ONE research ask (`RESEARCH_PROMPT_VERSION + '+followup.1'`): the same system prompt, the
  stored evidence, the sources the first ask read with its own tools, the loop's sources (ids "fu-…"), and a `<follow_up>`
  block written by code (`followUpNotes`: what the first ask came to, each new source by id and model, which models Home
  Depot shows no product for, and the gaps in fixed sentences; never a page's words, which stay in the evidence as data).
  Its answer goes through verifyResearch: no new acceptance path. At most `MAX_FOLLOW_UP_ROUNDS` = 2 follow-up asks per
  question, each on new evidence, each under the monthly cap (`estimatedQuestionCost`; an unpriced model is not asked).
  Measured free on the real case's shape (`core/test/research-followup.test.ts`, constructed rows: no recording of those
  three models exists): the loop reads the three models, asks once, the answer "PCL406424SB + A21701 + A25RS51" is refused
  (a 2-tool combo the catalog does not know as a kit, the PO's rule), round 2 has nothing new, and the question comes back
  `gave_up` with its four `tried` lines.
- *Where it runs* (`agent/followUp.ts`, two hooks in `agent/work.ts`): after every AI pass (scan, Ask about all), for the
  open split questions whose live answer is unverified or cannot tell and whose loop has not run for that answer, with
  Chrome connected: the free reads and the code's check always; follow-up asks only when `ai.reaskOnNewEvidence` is true.
  That setting is lever 2's, now this loop (lever 2's `reaskDue` is no longer called; its stamps `evidenceKey`/`lacks` feed
  the tasks). **Default OFF**: whether follow-up asks keep zero wrong needs paid calls; the harness is ready (below). An
  owner's press of Ask AI runs the loop with asks allowed whatever the setting: the press is his yes to an ask's cost.
  A follow-up ask's answer is its own suggestion (`followUpOf` names the record it asked again; `followUp` travels with it).
  Autopilot leaves a follow-up ask's verified answer to the owner while `FOLLOW_UP_ASKS_MEASURED` is false; a code check
  that verifies with what was read is lever 1's and Autopilot's rules apply as to any.
- *Ask AI is never a silent no-op* (`POST /api/review/:id/ask`, body `{ force?: boolean }`, `AskResponse { queued, reason?,
  canForce?, costUsd? }`, `askPlan`): no suggestion yet → an ask; the loop already queued or running → "The app is already
  working on this question."; `force` → a fresh ask under the cap (`reaskOf` names the record); an unsettled split answer
  with a task left or reads no ask saw → the loop; otherwise "Nothing new to read since the last answer; asking again would
  cost about $0.08." with `canForce: true` and the estimate. No key, Chrome closed (split), the cap or a scan running: the
  reason, no `canForce`.
- *The card's check line is only for a person* (`core/src/brief/who.ts`, `view.ts cardCheck`). `BriefCheck.who` is set by
  code: a check whose words are a page or Includes read, a model-number lookup (a model token) or a listing/catalog lookup is
  `app`; anything else, and every eBay check (no tool reads an eBay listing for him), is `owner`. An `app` check is never
  shown as an instruction: GET /api/review puts in its place what the app is doing (`followUp.doing`), did ("The app already
  read what it could for this (4 steps); …"), or did to verify; its link stays. A brief stored before this has no `who` and
  is classed at view time. The rule is deliberately wide (most checks are reads): an owner check is what remains. While a
  follow-up is queued or running the question is left out of "Needs you" (`counts.review`) and counted in
  `counts.reviewWorking`; it comes back when the loop is done or gave up, with `tried` filled.
- *Measurement* (`npm run research:eval -- --model claude-opus-5-5 --follow-up --dry-run | --run --max-cost <usd> | --replay
  [--fresh-tools]`, `scripts/research-followup-eval.ts`): the first asks are the recorded run replayed free; "before" and
  "after" are scored against the key, with what each question's loop read. Dry run today (free, recorded reads only):
  before 7 right / 0 wrong / 8 unverified / 4 cannot tell; after the free part 8 / 0 / 7 / 4 (328690510 PCL500B + PCL525B,
  by the code's check once PCL525B's row is read, the key agrees); 2 questions would be asked again, about $0.15, at most
  $0.29. With `--fresh-tools` the five reads no recording holds (searches for DEWALT DCB346-3, RYOBI PCL525B, PSK12B,
  PSK00B; page 326415387) are made in the owner's Chrome through the running app's worker jobs and recorded on first use in
  `fixtures/research/tools/followup-chrome.json`; up to 4 questions may then be asked, about $0.29, at most $0.58. The bar
  for turning the default on, and `FOLLOW_UP_ASKS_MEASURED`: zero verified wrong after the loop. *Caveat:* the loop's
  value on the 19 is small because most unverified answers there are refused for `untied` or `leftover`, which no read
  settles; its real target is the live app's failed-tool and unread-model cases, which the key set holds few of.

### Step 16, the follow-up loop measured (Nov 23 2026)
`SPLIT_FINDER_DATA_DIR=<the installed app's data folder> npm run research:eval -- --model claude-opus-5-5 --follow-up
--fresh-tools --run --max-cost 0.60` on the 19-question key (fixtures/research/followup-claude-opus-5-5.json; 8 Home Depot
reads made free in the owner's Chrome, recorded in fixtures/research/tools/followup-chrome.json): the free reads and the
code's re-check verified one more answer (328690510: Home Depot's own page for PCL525B, "ONE+ 18V Cordless Jig Saw (Tool
Only)"), 0 wrong. The three paid follow-up asks ($0.1388 in all) each stayed "cannot tell". So the free reads and the
re-check stay on, and the paid follow-up ask stays OFF (`FOLLOW_UP_ASKS_MEASURED` false): no measured gain for its cost.
The script reads the agent token from `agentDataDir()`, which is `./output` unless SPLIT_FINDER_DATA_DIR is set; the first
run failed every read for that reason and spent nothing.

The owner's own example, PCL406424SB-A21701-A25RS51 (fixtures/homedepot-pages/followup-PCL406424SB-2026-11-23.json, read
free through the app the same day): Home Depot sells PCL406424SB as its own product (item 328690521, the 2-tool kit) and
A21701 (item 316546864, a 7-piece sandpaper set); it has no product for A25RS51. The app held none of these readings
when it asked him: a three-part hyphen split went straight to a question instead of waiting for Home Depot's part pages.

### Step 16, round 2: every part is read before a question, and a question names only what Home Depot did not confirm (Nov 23 2026)
- *Why PCL406424SB-A21701-A25RS51 went straight to a question:* not its three parts (rule 2 already took any number of hyphen
  pieces) but its special buy: `spelledOut` returned no reading when a piece ended in SB, so rule 2 did not apply, nothing
  was waited for, and the question opened with no reading. Now a special buy is read like any part, and a split with a
  part still to read waits (`waitingFor`) even when another part was already refused: no question opens while a free read
  could change it (`homeDepotSplit`). *Measured* (`npx tsx scripts/measure-splits.ts`): validation 24 right / 0 wrong / 7
  open before and after; of the 7, 2 now wait for a reading instead of asking with nothing read (PCL500525SB-PBP003,
  PSK1212SB-PCL265B). The real scan-3 store: the owner's example waits for all three parts.
- *Rejected, measured: Home Depot's own page for exactly a special buy or combo as proof it is one kit.* Asked for on the
  owner's example (PCL406424SB's own page, item 328690521, lists "18V Compact Router, 18V 5" Random Orbit Sander, …" and no
  other model). The same rule takes PCL500525SB's own page (328690510: "Includes: 18V ONE+ 5-1/2 in. Circular Saw and 18V
  ONE+ Jig Saw", no other model) as one kit, and the answer key splits it into PCL500B + PCL525B. In the settle rule that is
  a wrong split of the validation's PCL500525SB-PBP003 once its parts are read ([PCL500525SB, PBP003] for the key's
  PCL500B + PCL525B + PBP003). In the verifier, replayed on the four recorded runs: 0 newly right, 0 wrong, and the three
  runs that answered 341644987 with [PCL500525SB, PBP003] are then refused only for "PBP003 accounts for nothing", one
  technicality from a verified wrong answer. Not adopted: a special buy is never confirmed as one part (the step 12 rule
  stands), and HD's search shows the same set as `PCL424B-PCL406B` with the same title, which is what the key's convention
  would split it into. Keeping PCL406424SB whole is a preference call for the owner, so it is asked, not decided.
- *A narrower question* (`Decomposition`/`ReviewItem.settledParts` and `unsettledParts`; `ReviewQuestion.settledParts`
  `{model, what, url}` and `unsettledParts: string[]`). From the scan's own part readings (rule 2, once every part is read
  and some are refused), else from the follow-up loop's reads (`narrowOf`: a Home Depot page or search row of exactly that
  brand and model settles a part; a search with no such row makes it `not_sold`; a special buy or a combo the catalog does
  not know as a kit stays unsettled with its page). The brief then asks "What are PCL406424SB and A25RS51 in this bundle?",
  gives the settled parts as a reason with their page, and its check is the owner's (`who: 'owner'`), written by code
  (`narrowCheck`): one part not sold on its own: "Home Depot does not sell A25RS51 on its own. If it is in this box, approve
  the 3 parts; you can mark A25RS51 not sold separately after."; a special buy: "… Keep it as one part, or enter the tools
  inside it?"; several: "Home Depot: PCL406424SB is a special buy; A25RS51 is not sold on its own. Approve the parts as
  listed, or enter them?". *No "A25RS51 not sold separately" split choice:* the PO's rule is that everything with a model
  number counts (everything is a leftover except manuals), and "not sold separately" is already a part's state, set on the
  part after the split (step 15 rule 4, `NO_AMAZON_LISTING_TEXT`); a split without A25RS51 would hide a part the page names.
- *The owner's example now, step by step* (real recordings; `core/test/settle-special-buy.test.ts`,
  `core/test/research-followup.test.ts`, `agent/test/followUp.test.ts`): (1) the scan finds no reading for the three parts and
  waits (`waiting_parts`), no question; (2) the part-price read finds PCL406424SB (item 328690521) and A21701 (item 316546864)
  sold under exactly those models and no A25RS51; (3) the split stays a question: A21701 settled by its page, PCL406424SB a
  special buy, A25RS51 not sold on Home Depot. On the path the app took on Nov 23 (a question already open), the follow-up
  loop's free reads give the same three `tried` lines with their links and the same narrowing, with or without an AI answer.
- *No question reaches "Needs you" with an app check and nothing queued:* the loop now also makes the free reads of split
  questions the AI has not answered (`not_asked`; state in the agent's `review.followUps`, kept across reports), after the AI
  pass. With Chrome closed every such question is queued ("Waiting for Chrome to read the Home Depot pages this needs") and
  counted in `reviewWorking`, not `review`; the next pass with Chrome open runs it, and an owner's press then runs it at once.
  A loop that found nothing to read hands the question to the owner with an owner check ("The app has nothing more it can
  read for this; the answer is your call."). Tested on all of the owner's scan-3 split questions.
- *Fixed on the way:* the follow-up eval's Chrome recordings (`fixtures/research/tools/followup-*.json`) were read by every
  research eval and broke its index (the base eval must not see reads made by the true parts' numbers); they now answer only
  `--follow-up`. `--fresh-tools` finds the token of the agent that is running: SPLIT_FINDER_DATA_DIR, else ./output when it has
  one, else the installed app's data folder (`~/Library/Application Support/com.bundlesplitfinder.desktop`, `%APPDATA%\…`).
  The follow-up eval's replay of the main session's paid run still scores 8 right, 0 wrong ($0.1388 recorded).

### A Windows smoke workflow exists and has not been run (Nov 23 2026)

- `.github/workflows/windows-smoke.yml` (manual `workflow_dispatch` and tags `v*`, `windows-latest`, Node 24.13.0 because the sidecar's SEA blob must match) builds the sidecar and the NSIS installer natively, uploads the installer, and runs `scripts/windows-smoke.ps1`. **It has never run: nothing was pushed. The repository owner must push the branch and start it.** Expect first-run failures in the script itself (it was written without a Windows machine or PowerShell to test on); treat those as script bugs before treating them as app bugs.
- The script checks: silent per-user install (`/S /D=`), start, the agent listening on 127.0.0.1:8790 within 60 s, the `/api/hello` HMAC, the shape of a fresh `/api/state`, 401 with no token, 403 with a foreign Origin (and 200 with the app's own, as the control), owner-only ACLs from parsed `icacls` output, `POST /api/agent/quit` leaving no `agent-dashboard` process within 10 s, and a silent uninstall removing the folder. That covers "install and first launch", "icacls on a real profile" and "quit leaving no agent" from the unverified list in docs/desktop-app.md. Still unverified, because a runner has no one to look: Mica, the flyout's position and hide-on-blur, launch at login, `chrome://extensions` from the Helper menu, real Chrome pairing.
- Choices: the store folder is checked for "no group-wide entry" rather than owner-only, because only files are narrowed (`agent/winAcl.ts`); the folder keeps %APPDATA%'s per-user ACL. `desktop/windows-smoke.test.ts` runs in `npm test` on the Mac and pins what the script relies on (the HMAC, the fresh-state shape, the status codes, the key names, the Node version), so a change to the agent cannot silently break the script. *For v2:* sign the installer and the sidecar, and run this on a tag before every release.

## Nov 23: the audit file and Reset scan data (Settings, Data)

- The app could not export an audit, so a problem report was a screenshot. `GET /api/export/audit` (ui role) is one JSON file of the
  scan store, deals, Review, suggestions, Activity and settings (agent/dataPane.ts). Its no-key guarantee is two layers: every string and
  property name goes through work.ts `scrub`, then the finished text is searched for each stored key value (plain and JSON-escaped);
  a hit fails the whole export with a plain 503 and nothing is sent. A scrubber alone could miss a key sitting in a number or split by
  escaping, so the second check is the one that is trusted.
- `POST /api/data/reset` (body `{ confirm: 'RESET' }`) clears scan data and nothing else. Autopilot history and saved answers go with it
  because their Undo points at bundles that no longer exist; the saved search responses (`cache/`) stay so the next scan does not pay
  again; the helper's last snapshot in Activity stays because it is the connection, not a scan. A backup folder
  (`backup-before-reset-<time>`, never the keys) is written first and kept. It refuses while `AgentWork.writing()` (a scan, ask, owner answer or drip round).
- The app version in the audit file comes from `SPLIT_FINDER_APP_VERSION`, which the shell sets from the Cargo version.
## Nov 23: the app runs by itself out of the box (Welcome step 4, keep awake, Telegram test)

- **The schedule is recommended from the real budget, never guessed** (`core/src/schedule/recommend.ts`). Options, most frequent first: twice a day (12 h), daily at 7:00, every 2 days, twice a week (84 h), once a week, manual. The pick is the first that fits both the steady month and what is left of this one: scans × searches a scan ≤ 85% (HEADROOM) of the plan minus the reserve, and the same for the account's searches left now minus the reserve across the days left (each interval counted up). Not even a weekly scan fitting gives manual, with the arithmetic. A scan's searches are the median of its last five finished or limited scans that spent something (never above the per-scan cap); with none, a documented default: 4 with a Keepa key (3 to 5 recorded: listing pages and catalog pages), 10 without (4 plus about 6 Amazon lookups, an assumption the first runs replace). On 250 a month, reserve 100, cap 18: usable 150, with Keepa 30 × 4 = 120 a month, daily at 7:00; without Keepa and no history 9 × 10 = 90, twice a week. The budget (`budgetPolicyFor`) still stops a scheduled scan at the reserve whatever is chosen; the recommendation only keeps the plan from asking it to. A choice that does not fit is shown but disabled; Settings can still set anything.
- **Welcome has a fourth step, "Let it run by itself", outside `setupProgress`** ("Finish setup · n of 3" keeps its meaning; a schedule is not required to be set up). It preselects the recommendation (or the schedule already running), shows the arithmetic, and Turn on saves the schedule, "Open at login" (on by default in this step) and "Keep the computer awake while a scan runs" in one PATCH. Switching from manual starts nothing at once: the first scan is the next slot (`settingsChanged`). When the three steps are done the sheet opens at this one; `RecommendedScheduleLink` (a shared component) opens it from Settings.
- **Keep awake is the agent's, for the scan only** (`agent/keepAwake.ts`, setting `shell.keepAwakeDuringScan`, default true, a store without it reads true). Held in `claim('scan')` (Scan now, scheduled, Look up all, Read again) and released when the work ends or the agent closes. macOS: `caffeinate -i -w <agent pid>` (no idle sleep; ends with the agent). Windows: hidden `powershell.exe -EncodedCommand` that calls `SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS | ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED)` and `Wait-Process` on the agent's pid; no native dependency. The Windows command is checked by a unit test as text only; it has not been run on a Windows machine. The display is not held awake. A helper that cannot start is logged once and the scan goes on. *For v2:* the Windows path should be run once on Alex's PC; a Tauri-side power request would replace the child process.
- **Telegram can be tested** (`POST /api/notify/test-telegram`, ui role): one send of "Split Finder can reach you here." through the digest's `sendTelegram`; `{ok, problem?}` with HTTP 200 either way; `problem` is Telegram's refusal in plain words plus its own description, with the token (and its secret half) scrubbed. It touches neither the digest queue nor `lastSentAt`.
- **First-run defaults were already right and are now pinned by a test**: deals worth buying and open questions notify, one digest after each run, the schedule starts manual (nothing spends searches until Welcome or Settings says so). A stored choice is never changed.
- **One constant names the helper's pairing button** (`core/src/api/labels.ts`): the app's setup text said "Connect to the app" while the button reads "Connect to the Split Finder app".
