- **Security review, Nov 23** (`docs/security-review-2026-11-23.md`). The agent strips keys from eBay's error text before
  cutting it to length, and strips the Basic credential too. A CSV cell starting with a tab or carriage return is made
  inert. `POST /api/worker/jobs` refuses what the helper would refuse: item ids are digits, and a search is page 1. Every
  link the app shows or opens goes through `desktop-ui/src/lib/webUrl.ts` (http or https, no user name). No page gets
  shell spawn, kill or execute. The CSP loads images only from the app. *Left for v2:* pairing still trusts an Origin that
  a local program can forge (the code shown in both places is the check), worker results are kept whole, and Install
  helper replaces Documents/split-finder whatever it holds.
### Signals round 2: the agent says when a bundle is worth buying, between scans too (Nov 23 2026)
The owner's app on Nov 23: the best deal, DCK225D2WCS369B at $269, est. +$69.15 (26% ROI, both parts on catalog-verified
listings), had `signals: null` and `fbaEstimate: null`: its estimate came from the drip after the last scan, and signals were
judged only at a scan's end. Another deal's verdict was "Keepa has no monthly-sold figure stored for DCK540D1 and DCS382B",
because the drip's price pulls and the catalog check's pulls kept no Keepa figures. And every estimate left out Amazon's
fulfillment fee (flat fee $0, the default).
- **Signals are judged whenever an estimate or completeness changes** (`agent/signalsPass.ts`, hooked into `work.ts`'s
  `refresh`): after the drip's lookups, price pulls, catalog check and sales pulls, after an owner's Review answer, Undo,
  confirmed price or Look up, and at start (the deals rebuilt from the store when it held deals; nothing fetched). A scan
  still judges its own at its end (`trackReport`, unchanged); `POST /api/lookups/run` runs as a scan and goes that way. Same
  rules (`bundleSignals`), same once-per-bundle+signal+day bookkeeping (`freshSignals`, now limited to the bundles judged),
  same `NotifyPolicy` (kinds, quiet hours, morning digest). The drip's "N new estimates" line and the buy line go in one
  message. Confirmed and discounted bundles become watched between scans too.
- **What "judged again" means between scans** (`core/src/tracking/state.ts retrackReport`): a bundle whose figures (section,
  price, each way's estimate, open Review) moved since its last stored point is judged against its history and gets a point,
  as at a scan. One whose figures did not move is judged against the history its last point was judged by, with the Keepa
  figures stored now, so a sales figure that arrived can turn the verdict and nothing else does; a verdict that comes out the
  same keeps its stored signals and is not sent again. A buy signal still needs a change, so a deal that stays good is not
  re-announced every day; the scan after a between-scan point sees no change (the owner was told when it happened).
- **A change is judged once sales data exists** (`judgedBy`): when the point has every part's monthlySold and the points
  before it did not, those points are left out of the baseline. Measured on the test case: PCL235B first seen at +$29.16
  (no sales figure: not a buy), the sales pull brought monthlySold 500 and Keepa's $8.02 FBA fee (+$21.14); without this
  rule the fee's drop made it "no better than the last check" and the deal the owner should buy was never named.
- **The verdict always names the failing condition** (`BundleSignals.verdict`, `OptionSignals.notBuyNow`, `DealRow.signalNote`):
  one sentence, money first, then leftovers, then sales: "Not worth buying now: est. profit $6.63 is below your $20 and 4% ROI
  below your 20%", "…: DCS382B sells about 12 a month, below your 50", "…: no sales data for DCS369B yet — Keepa is asked on
  the next lookup", "…: no sales data for X — Keepa leaves it out for slow sellers" (pulled, no monthlySold), "…: no sales data
  for X — no Amazon listing of its own to read" (a confirmed price). A buy: "Worth buying now: est. profit $21.14 (235% ROI),
  sells about 500 a month, first seen with an estimate". The owner's own numbers print without cents ("$20").
- **Every Keepa product pull keeps its trend** (`core/src/keepa/trends.ts keepingTrends`): the lookup's price pull, the catalog
  check's pull and the sales pull write the same `keepa:trend:<asin>` a scan's refresh writes: monthlySold and when it was read
  (`pulledAt`), `fbaFees.pickAndPackFee`, `referralFeePercentage` (new: `referralPercent`), the sales rank (`stats.current[3]`)
  and the summed `buyBoxEligibleOfferCounts`. Checked on the recordings: the product pull with and without stats
  (`product-…-stats.json`, `product-….json`) and the search rows all carry monthlySold, fbaFees, referralFeePercentage and the
  offer counts; only the stats pull carries a price and a rank. **The price pull's request needed no change**: it already
  sends `history=0&stats=180`, monthlySold is a product field, and stats cost nothing extra (keepa.com/api-docs/product.html,
  read Nov 23). Token arithmetic: a drip lookup stays 10 (search) + 1 (price pull) = 11; the catalog check stays 1 a listing;
  keeping the trend costs 0 tokens (one more local file).
- **The sales pull** (`salesPullsWanted`, `pullSales`): a watched deal's part whose listing Keepa was never asked about (no
  trend) is pulled once by the drip, 1 token above the reserve, after the lookups and after the catalog check (whose pulls keep
  trends too, so a listing it pulled is skipped). A listing pulled with no monthlySold is not pulled again: Keepa leaves it out
  for slow sellers and a second pull would say the same. The pull saves the price as a refresh does.
- **Realistic fees** (`fees.fulfillmentSource`, `fees.referralSource`, `core/src/scan/report.ts feesOf`): with 'keepa' (default
  for new installs), a part's fulfillment fee in the main estimate is Keepa's pick-and-pack fee for its own listing when
  stored, else the flat fee; a part priced by a confirmed price takes the flat settings. The referral rate stays the owner's
  unless he picks `referralSource: 'keepa'` (default 'flat': his rate is never changed silently). Migration
  (`agent/store.ts FULFILLMENT_KEEPA_MIGRATION`, once, logged in `runtime.migrationLog`): a stored fees object with no source
  takes 'keepa' when its flat fee is $0 and 'flat' when the owner set one. The estimate's basis says exactly what was used:
  "15% referral (at least $0.30); Amazon's FBA fee from Keepa for 1 of 2 parts ($15.01), your flat $0.00 for PCL424B; 0% tax;
  $0.00 inbound. An estimate, not your real Amazon fees." With 'flat' it is the settings in words, as before. The separate
  "FBA estimate (Keepa)" line now replaces each part's fee in the estimate (not the flat setting) and shows only when it
  differs. Preferences › Scanning: "Fulfillment fee: Amazon's FBA fee from Keepa (recommended) / A flat amount per unit"; the
  $0 warning stays on the flat choice; with Keepa the flat amount is labelled as the fallback.
- **Measured on after-scan3** (`core/test/report-keepa-fees.test.ts`), with the three FBA fees the owner's app had stored from
  Keepa (PCL1201K2 $15.01, PCL406B $7.60, PBT01B $41.53; every other part has none yet): 13 complete estimates either way;
  above zero 3 before, 0 after. Top 5 before: PCL1201K2-PCL424B +$6.63, PCL1201K2-P755 +$3.26, PCL1201K2-PCL445B +$1.32,
  PCL1201K2-PCL515B -$0.14, DCK540D1WCS382B -$0.94. After: DCK540D1WCS382B -$0.94, DCK540D1WCS565B -$7.11, DCK540D1WCB1404
  -$7.70, PCL1201K2-PCL424B -$8.38, PCL1201K2-P755 -$11.75. The five PCL1201K2 BOGO pairs fall by $15.01 each, PCL424B-PCL406B
  by $7.60. Every part with no stored fee still counts $0, so the rest still overstate; the sales pulls fill those in.

### Signals round 3: no silent mixing of fees, and fees for every part that counts (Nov 23 2026)
An estimate that counted Amazon's FBA fee for one part and $0 for another ranked bundles wrongly (the Keepa-fee'd bundle
looked worse than one whose fee was simply missing), and the PO would act on it.
- **An estimate counts a fee for every part, or says which it does not** (`core/src/scan/report.ts feesOf`, `PartFees`
  `fulfillmentFrom: 'keepa' | 'flat' | 'none'` with `fulfillmentWhy`). With `fees.fulfillmentSource: 'keepa'`: Keepa's FBA
  fee for the part's own listing; a listing Keepa was asked about with no FBA fee (it happens), or a part priced by a
  confirmed price, takes the owner's flat fee when it is above $0 and says so ("flat $5.00 for PCL235B: Keepa has no FBA fee
  for it"); at $0, or for a listing Keepa was not asked about yet, no fee is counted ('none') and the estimate is "before
  fulfillment for M of N parts". *Not asked yet counts $0, not the flat fee:* the label then says exactly what the figure is
  (no fee in it), and the drip asks Keepa next; a flat fee would make it a third, unlabelled kind.
- **What "before fulfillment" does:** never `buyNow`, never a buy line in a digest, never in "Worth buying now" (the verdict
  names it; a profit already below the owner's number is named first, since a fee can only lower it); `DealRow.beforeFulfillment
  {parts, of}`; the basis sentence opens with "before fulfillment for 1 of 2 parts: Amazon's FBA fee from Keepa for PCL1201K2
  ($15.01); none yet for PCL424B: Keepa has not been asked about its listing". Deals order (`core/src/deals/sort.ts`): by profit
  or ROI, such a row sorts after every full estimate of the same sign (above zero, or not); by discount, unchanged. The UI's own
  re-sorts (Today, table heads) are not changed here.
- **Fees for every part that counts** (`agent/signalsPass.ts feePullsWanted`): with 'keepa', every matched part of every bundle
  with a complete estimate, or one part short of one, whose listing Keepa has not been asked about within the freshness window
  (3 days): bundles by estimate, highest first, then the one-short ones by smallest break-even; 1 token each, through the drip,
  after the lookups and the catalog check; the watched deals' sales pulls follow. A part pulled for any reason has a trend
  (`pulledAt`) and is not pulled again within the window; a re-pull past it passes the window as the cache's max age. With no
  lookup waiting, the drip's next look waits only for one token, not a lookup's 11. `AgentState.agent.drip.feesWaiting`.
- **The verdict on every row with an estimate, watched or not** (`signalNote`): signals are now judged for every bundle; only a
  watched bundle's buy signal goes in a digest or marks the row `buyNow`. Wording, binding condition first: "est. $6.63 is below
  your $20", "before fulfillment for 1 of 2 parts — Keepa is asked next", "DCS382B sells about 12 a month, below your 50",
  "Worth buying now: +$21.14 (234.9% ROI), sells about 500 a month, …". A part before fulfillment because Keepa was not asked has
  no sales figure for the same reason: one pull answers both, so the verdict says it once.
- **Replayed on after-scan3** (`npx tsx scripts/replay-fees.ts`, with the 3 FBA fees the owner's app had stored): 13 complete
  estimates, 0 with a fee for every part, 13 before fulfillment for some part; the fee pulls would take 24 listings, 24 tokens.
  Deals top 8 by profit: DCK540D1WCS382B -$0.94, DCK540D1WCS565B -$7.11, DCK540D1WCB1404 -$7.70 (2 of 2 before fulfillment),
  PCL1201K2-PCL424B -$8.38, -P755 -$11.75, -PCL445B -$13.69 (1 of 2), DCK540D1WDCB606 -$14.83 (2 of 2), PCL1201K2-PCL515B -$15.15
  (1 of 2); none worth buying.

## Buy safety and eBay questions out of Review (Nov 23)

The first real scan with eBay working (Nov 23, 80 bundles, 27 estimated) announced two deals as Buy now,
PSK1212SB-PCL515B (+$43.19, 43.6% ROI) and PSK1212SB-PCL633B (+$38.79), whose estimates each rested on two title matches the
catalog check had not read yet; and Review went from 4 to 26 questions, 20 of them eBay identity questions about reference
listings that count in no estimate.

- **A buy needs confirmed listings** (`core/src/tracking/signals.ts isConfirmedListing, unconfirmedListings`). A counted part's
  Amazon listing (qty above 0, priced by the listing's own non-reference New price) is confirmed when its grade is `exact`
  (barcode) or `strong` (manufacturer part number; a catalog confirmation sets strong too), or `verifiedBy` is `keepa_catalog`,
  `serpapi_product` or `owner`. A title match (`probable`, `verifiedBy: 'title'`) is not. A part priced by the owner's confirmed
  price has no listing to confirm.
- **Held back, not announced**: when every other condition holds but a counted listing is unconfirmed, the way to sell is not
  `buyNow`; `BundleSignals.buyPending {optionId, listings, asins}`, `OptionSignals.buyPending`, and the verdict "Looks worth
  buying: +$43.19 (43.6% ROI), sells about 200 a month — 2 listings are being confirmed first". No digest line, no
  notification, not in "Worth buying now"; `DealRow.buyPending {listings}`. When the estimate meets the owner's numbers but
  something else fails too, "N listings not confirmed yet" is said first; below the numbers the threshold binds first.
- **Confirmation is the change**: a held-back buy needs no other change to stay held back from scan to scan (its "no better
  than the last check" is not said). `OptionPoint.unconfirmed` is stored in the history, and `sameFigures` compares it, so when
  the last listing is confirmed (by the drip's catalog check, a scan's, or the owner) the bundle is judged again at once and the
  reason is "Its Amazon listings are confirmed now: …"; it goes out once a day like every buy signal. A listing the check
  contradicts is removed as before and the estimate recomputed (with no other listing, no estimate and no buy).
- **The listings a buy waits on go first** (`catalogChecksWanted(store, parts, first)`): the scan's catalog checks take them
  before any other (`ScanDeps.catalogFirst`, from the stored signals); the drip checks them before its lookups, then the rest
  of the catalog checks and the fee pulls as before. A deal first held back in a scan is checked first in the next drip.
- **eBay identity questions leave Review by default** (`ebaySettings.askAboutListings`, default off; a stored install without
  the field reads off, so it is migrated by its default). A listing the judge is not sure of was already left out of the
  part's eBay range (`core/src/ebay/value.ts` counts only `match`); now its question is not in Review, `counts.review`, the
  activity's `reviewOpen` or the digest's "questions need you" either. The questions stay stored and answerable by id through
  the same answer path. The PO's rule stands: eBay values are a reference and count only when he chooses a listing or types
  a price.
- **Not done in this round**: the "N eBay listings left out: not sure they are the same product" line with a "Review them"
  disclosure under the part's reference prices; the Activity sentence "eBay: 73 calls, 20 listings left out as uncertain";
  the shared comparator rule that an estimate on an unconfirmed listing never ranks above confirmed ones of the same sign; the
  replay on after-scan3. `agent/app.ts`'s SSE review count calls `openReviewCount` without the setting (another change owns
  that file), so with the setting on its pushed count leaves eBay questions out until the next state poll.

- **A pasted link is read for its price; he still confirms it** (owner, Nov 23: "when I paste a link of where I find the item being sold it auto gets the price"). Confirm a price now takes the link first. `POST /api/price-from-link` reads eBay by the Browse item call (free; price, stated shipping, condition; an ended listing, an auction with no Buy It Now and a non-USD price are `found: false`), Amazon by one Keepa product pull through the token budget and reserve (stats.current[0], else [1], said in the note; Amazon is never browsed; a pull under an hour old is reused and its age is shown), and Home Depot by the existing free page job in his Chrome (`promoPage`, which carries the page price). Any other site is not read and the typed-price path is unchanged; no host permission and no scraping was added. The answer only fills the price field. It is saved as a `typed` price with the pasted link when he presses Save price, so the PO's Nov 20 rule (it counts when he confirms) is unchanged, and a price is never copied without being shown. The eBay shipping shown is informational: the price counted is the item price. 15 s timeouts. Not done: Facebook Marketplace and shops other than these three, and an eBay sold price for an ended listing (Browse does not return one).

## Confirming listings by themselves: the listing ladder (Nov 23)

Owner, Nov 23, looking at deals that said "1 listing not confirmed yet": "if I can do this manually with just clicking then
it should be fully automated". Before this, the catalog check (step 16) answered "unknown" whenever Amazon's record had no
exact part-number field, remembered that answer, and nothing else ran: the owner's "Confirm listing" was a paid SerpApi
`amazon_product` read whose page was graded by `pick()` (strong only on a part-number or barcode match, else no change). The
verdict "N listings are being confirmed first" had nothing behind it. Now a ladder runs by itself, cheapest first; each rung
is measured on the recorded rows with the answer key and adopted only at zero wrong (`npm run measure:autoconfirm`,
`npm run listing-judge:eval`).

- **Rung 0, the catalog check** (unchanged): 65 right, 0 wrong, 237 unknown of 302 rows with a known answer.
- **Rung 1, the listing's own fields** (`core/src/keepa/listingFields.ts`, no new call), on the 237 rows rung 0 left unknown,
  one sub-rule at a time: model as a whole token in the **title** with the brand field the part's brand: 22 right, 0 wrong
  (41 rows the key says are another product reached the gate; every one was stopped: 33 with no brand field, the rest by a
  kit-with-extras, battery, OEM/replacement, pack or other-part-number contradiction). **Adopted.** In the part number or
  model field as a token (`id_field`), in a **feature bullet**, or a **barcode** equal to Home Depot's UPC: 0 right, 0 wrong
  each: no recorded row exercises them (no scan stores a Home Depot UPC: after-scan3's catalog has none). **Not adopted**: an
  unmeasured rule does not confirm. The gate for every sub-rule: brand field = brand, manufacturer = brand when given, no
  part number that is another identifier, verify.ts's contradictions, and no feature bullet selling a bare tool with a
  battery ("Comes with a 4Ah battery", B0FD1B8BG5). A rung-1 confirmation is grade strong, `verifiedBy: 'listing_fields'`, and
  counts as confirmed for a buy (`CONFIRMING_SOURCES`).
- **Old unknowns are read again once.** A stored catalog check carries the ladder's version (`CatalogCheckRecord.ladder`);
  an unknown from before rung 1 is queued once more, the pull read from the cache when it is there (`RECHECK_MAX_AGE_MS`, 90
  days), so the drip has work for the deals that sat waiting.
- **The owner's click reads more.** The paid Verify of a page that grades only probable now runs rungs 0 and 1 on the page
  (brand, part number, model, the title rule): a page with RYOBI's brand field and a title naming PBLRC25B confirms with no
  part-number field.
- **Rung 2, the owner's click done by the agent** (`core/src/scan/autoConfirm.ts`, `agent/autoConfirm.ts`): after a scan and
  after each drip round, for a listing still a title match once the catalog check has read it (or Keepa is off), the paid
  Verify runs through `SerpApiClient` under a `SearchBudget` from a fresh free account check, scheduled policy (never the
  reserve), the per-scan cap less what the scan spent, and today's allowance. Only where it can change a decision: a buy
  waiting on its listings (`buyPending`) first, then ways to sell within 75% of both of the owner's numbers (`REACH`), the
  largest estimated profit first; each listing once (`AgentRuntime.autoConfirm.tried`). Allowance:
  `lookups.verifySearchesPerDay` when set, else what the account above the reserve leaves per day until renewal, at most 5.
  `lookups.autoVerify: false` turns it off. In the drip it looks at most every 10 minutes and records its own Activity run
  ("Confirmed 1 of 2 Amazon listings automatically (2 searches: 1 for a buy waiting on it, 1 within reach of your
  numbers)."); in a scan the scan's run counts the searches and says the sentence. Offline it settles nothing on the
  recordings (it is a live read).
- **Rung 3, the AI listing judge** (`core/src/listingJudge`), provider-neutral, Haiku 5.5 first: same / different / cannot
  tell, quoting the listing. Code finds each quote word for word in the field it names, needs a model-number quote naming the
  part, the brand field (or a title that begins with the brand: "… Screw for New Ryobi P553" is not), no other model of the
  brand in the title, and rung 1's contradictions; then `verifiedBy: 'ai_checked'`. Offline evidence: against a judge that
  answers "same" on every row, the code's check lets **0 wrong** through (1 right of the 215 rows rungs 0 and 1 left unknown;
  33 right of all 302). That bounds a careless model, but it does not prove a real one helps: of the 215 open rows only 11
  are the part. **Shipped off** (`ai.listingJudge` absent); the paid measurement is the main session's:
  `npm run listing-judge:eval -- --model claude-haiku-5-5 --run --max-cost 0.25` (dry run: 215 rows, about $0.05; Sonnet 5.5
  about $0.93, Opus 5.5 about $1.85). **Rule:** `ai_checked` shows as "confirmed by AI check" and never confirms a listing for
  a buy by itself (`CONFIRMING_SOURCES` leaves it out); a buy still needs rung 0, 1 or 2 or the owner. Changing that needs a
  run at zero wrong on the full set and the owner's yes. Rung 3 is not wired into the agent yet.
- **The words say what is really happening.** The stored verdict says only "N listings not confirmed yet". The deal row
  (`agent/views.ts confirmingNote`) replaces it from the agent's queue (`AgentRuntime.autoConfirm.queue` and `nextAt`, written
  by `noteConfirmQueue` at start, after each drip round and after a scan): "N listings are being confirmed — next check in
  about 4 min" only for listings the catalog check or rung 2 will really read at a known time (rung 2's wait for the next day
  when today's allowance is spent); otherwise "N listings could not be confirmed from Amazon's data — Confirm them (1 search
  each)". A queue more than 15 minutes past its time is stale and says nothing. `AgentState.agent.drip.confirmWaiting` counts
  them, and `nextAt` is set whenever any drip work waits.
- **Replay on the owner's third scan** (after-scan3, 30 title matches): 3 settled offline by the catalog check (PCL235,
  DCF921B, DCB606, from the recorded pulls); 27 need a live read. Of those, rung 1 on the stored search row (title and
  brand only, the most a Keepa pull could settle) passes 23 and stops 4 (A967001 and A10FB1G: no RYOBI P-pattern model to read;
  DCK540D1: title says 5Ah where D is 2.0Ah; DCK225D2: the title also names its kit tools DCD794 and DCF809, which the title rule does not read as this model): those go to rung 2 or the owner.

### The AI listing judge measured: it adds nothing, so it stays off (Nov 23 2026)
`npm run listing-judge:eval -- --model claude-haiku-5-5 --run --max-cost 0.25` on the 215 recorded rows the code rungs
leave unknown (11 of them are the part, per the answer key): after the code's check of its quotes, 0 right, 0 wrong,
215 unknown; it answered "different" on 198 rows, 2 of them wrongly. Cost $0.0446
(fixtures/listing-judge/judgements-claude-haiku-5-5.json). No confirmation was gained, so `ai.listingJudge` stays off and
is not wired into the agent. Listings are confirmed by Amazon's catalog record, by the title-plus-brand rule (22 right,
0 wrong), by the automatic product-page read within its allowance, or by the owner.

## A scan's discovery is free: Home Depot's bundle lists read in Chrome (Nov 23)

The owner's automation was limited by searches: every scan paid SerpApi for Home Depot's listing pages and the Amazon
brand pages (3 searches a scan in the owner's three scans), with 250 a month, 100 held in reserve and 2 left above it,
although the paired helper already read Home Depot's pages free (the BOGO pass, the bundle and kit pages, the part prices).

- **The scan reads each brand's Home Depot Bundle list in Chrome** (`core/src/scan/listingSource.ts readBundleListings`;
  the runner's `ScanDeps.chromeListing`, which plans no SerpApi listing page). *Checked live in the owner's Chrome (free):*
  the address is the search query's with Home Depot's own filter tokens in its N- value (base `5yc1v`, then `Z` and each
  token: Bundle `bwo64`, RYOBI `m5d`, DEWALT `4j2`), `/b/N-5yc1vZbwo64Zm5d/Ntk-elasticplus/Ntt-ryobi%2B18v%2Bcombo%2Bkit?NCNI-5`;
  12 `base-searchNav-<id>` entries a page, `&Nao=12` the next 12, `&Nao=24` none: 24 RYOBI bundles. SerpApi's own pages
  report hundreds for the query because it applies no filter token reliably (`hd_filter_tokens` accepted and ignored,
  as recorded before), so its listing is replaced, not filtered. The queries are today's (`BRAND_QUERIES`).
- **Paging, as the BOGO pass pages:** pages until one adds no new item id, the next offset being this list's own items so
  far, at most 10 pages a brand (`BUNDLE_LISTING_PAGES_MAX`); an item two lists show is one row. A refusal (a listing or
  item page) ends the pass: what was read is kept and saved, the problem is said on the scan, and no other Home Depot page
  is read in that scan (bundle, kit and part-price passes skip with "Home Depot refused"). No retry; the doorway rule.
- **Prices come from each bundle's own page, read in the same pass.** The recorded listing entries hold identifiers only
  (`research-2026-11-20.json`: brandName, productLabel, modelNumber, canonicalUrl); the price was not looked for in the
  listing state because no recording shows one. The item page (`parsePromoPage`, the BOGO reader) gives price, was-price
  and the Includes bullets, kept in the BOGO pass's item cache (`homedepot:bogo-item:<id>`): either pass reuses a reading
  for 6 hours (`LISTING_ITEM_MAX_AGE_MS`; the BOGO pass's own rule stays "read today"). The reading is also the bundle's
  page when none is stored, so a new bundle costs one page read, not two. Item pages count toward `pageReadsPerScan` (60).
  A bundle whose page was not read this time keeps its stored price; a Chrome row never writes null over it.
- **The same pipeline:** a Chrome row is saved by the listing page's own code (`saveListingRow`, factored out of
  `applyListing`): a bundle by its model's shape, else a catalog entry. One change for every row: a stored bundle's
  `bogo` facts are kept when a listing row rewrites it (before, a SerpApi row of a BOGO pair dropped them until the next
  BOGO pass).
- **Not added: the brand + Bundle list without a query.** The brief allowed it if a recording showed it lists more
  bundles; there is no recording of it, so it is not read (no guessed totals). *Live check:* open the RYOBI
  Bundle filter in Chrome with no search words (its address form is not recorded either) and compare its item ids with the query's 24.
- **Where the lists come from: Settings `scan.listingSource`** `auto` (default: Chrome when the helper is connected; SerpApi
  only when it is not, the owner started the scan, and a search can pay), `chrome` (never a search for them), `serpapi`
  (as before). **A scheduled scan with Chrome closed waits for Chrome** (`agent/scanSource.ts scheduledScanWaits`; the tick
  starts it once the helper is back), unless `scan.serpWhenChromeClosed` (default off) lets it search. A scan that can
  neither read nor search finds no new bundles and says so; its other steps run.
- **The Amazon brand pages are not read when Keepa looks parts up** (`discoveryFor`: `catalogPagesPerScan` 0). Measured
  (`npx tsx scripts/measure-catalog-step.ts`, the owner's after-scan3 store): of 421 listings, 92 were saved only by the
  3 brand pages (20 + 35 + 37); without them **0 of 59 splits change** (38 models are known only from them, none decides a
  split), and **5 parts** (6 with a B twin: PCL515B/PCL515, DCW600B, P755, DCB606, DCB1404, all `probable`) were matched to
  a listing only a brand page saved. Each of those is one Keepa lookup (11 tokens) away, against 3 searches for the pages.
  No Keepa brand search stands in: none is recorded, and the per-part lookup already does the job. Without Keepa the brand
  pages stay within the fifth of the scan's allowance they always had, which is 0 when the budget is short.
- **What a scan spends now.** Chrome + Keepa: 0 searches to find bundles and 0 for brand pages; a lookup Keepa cannot pay
  for still goes to SerpApi within the per-scan cap and never the reserve (step 15, decision 1, unchanged). Chrome without
  Keepa: 0 to find bundles, up to a fifth of the allowance for brand pages, each new part's lookup a search. Chrome
  closed: scheduled waits (or searches if allowed); a manual scan searches as before.
- **The schedule recommendation** (`core/src/schedule/recommend.ts`, `discovery: 'chrome'`): with Keepa a scan plans 0
  searches, so every choice fits and the first is **"Every 3 hours while Chrome is open"**, bounded by
  `MIN_SCAN_INTERVAL_HOURS` = 3, Home Depot politeness, not the budget: a scan reads 2 to 3 list pages a brand, the BOGO
  lists, and at most 60 item pages, each new or older than 6 hours, paced as a tab; 8 such passes a day. The month's
  searches go to the automatic listing confirmation, the owner's lookups, and lookups Keepa cannot pay for. Without Keepa
  a free scan plans 6 searches (the lookups), and the budget decides as before. Recent runs are not used in Chrome mode:
  they paid for listing pages. Settings, Scanning: "Find bundles" (the source, and the Chrome-closed switch), and the
  forecast says "Finding bundles spends no searches".
- **The step list** names the step "Home Depot bundle lists" and reads "read in Chrome · 24 bundles · 0 searches" (the
  searches are the brand pages'); the run's summary adds "Bundle lists read in Chrome: 24 bundles." and its "0 searches.".
- **A new worker job, `bundleListing`**, rather than a wider `bogoListing`: an older helper answers it "does not know that
  kind of job", which the agent reads as outdated and reloads it, never as Home Depot failing. Its path is checked by
  `isBundleListingPath` in the agent and in the helper (security review M2: only the filter tokens above, a query and an
  offset).
- *Live checks:* the helper must be rebuilt and reloaded for `bundleListing`; one real scan should show the two lists'
  page counts and bundle totals (24 RYOBI expected), whether DEWALT's `4j2` list matches its query as RYOBI's did, and
  whether the 22 bundles the owner's SerpApi listing pages found (after-scan3) are in the Bundle lists: the replay
  (`core/test/scan-chrome-listing.test.ts`) shows the Chrome rows build the same records as SerpApi's rows, not that Home
  Depot lists the same products.
- *For v2:* if Home Depot's listing state carries prices (unrecorded), the item page reads for price could go; record a
  full listing state once to find out.
## The listing ladder, round 2: no endless "being confirmed" (Nov 23)

Live, the two pending deals (PSK1212SB-PCL515B and -PCL633B) said their listings were being confirmed for 25 minutes and
nothing happened: with 102 searches left and a reserve of 100, rung 2's allowance was 0, and the queue still counted its
listings for "the next day", every day. PBP004 is on B09HDZ5MPV ("TTI Ryobi PBP004 …", brand TTI) and PCG002 on
B0B1QRQYK2 ("ONEAND 18V Lithium-ION Charger PCG002", brand ONEAND). The validation answer key labels **both as the right
listing** (TTI makes RYOBI; "ONEAND" is RYOBI's "ONE+" as Amazon prints it), so the hold was right to wait for a
confirmation, but they are not another brand's parts.

- **Queued only when a rung can run** (`agent/autoConfirm.ts`): rung 2 only when it is on, has a key, today's allowance is
  not spent and the last known account (`AgentRuntime.serp`, kept fresh by rung 2's own free check) leaves searches above
  the reserve (`rung2CanRun`); the catalog check only when Keepa can pull (`keepaCanPull`). Otherwise the listing is
  `unconfirmable` with its reason from what was read (`verify.ts unconfirmedReason`: "listed under the brand TTI", "no part
  number in Amazon's record", "title only"), no free account check is made, and the drip does not count it. When the
  allowance is positive again, the listing is queued again once (it was never read, so it is not in `tried`).
- **The words**: "Looks worth buying: … — 2 listings could not be confirmed: PBP004 is listed under the brand TTI; PCG002
  under the brand ONEAND. Confirm or replace them." `DealRow.buyPending.blocked` is true, and each part's
  `PartRow.confirmNote` says its reason on the deal's page. A stored verdict from before ("being confirmed first") is
  replaced the same way.
- **Re-pick before giving up** (`agent/verifyMatches.ts repickUnconfirmed`): the part's other stored search rows (free),
  then one Keepa search for the part (`RYOBI PCG002`, 11 tokens, only above the Keepa reserve, at most once a week a part).
  When exactly one other candidate passes rung 0 or rung 1, the part moves to it, grade strong, with the record "switched
  from B0B1QRQYK2 (…) to …: Amazon's catalog lists brand RYOBI, part number PCG002", its price pulled (1 token) and the
  estimate and signals rebuilt; the old listing stays an alternative. None, or more than one: nothing changes.
- **The narrow brand contradiction is not adopted** (`verify.ts narrowBrandContradiction`, `npm run measure:autoconfirm`):
  "brand field another brand, manufacturer (when given) not the brand either, title's first word that brand" fires on no
  recorded row (0 right, 0 wrong, 302 unknown) and on both live listings, which the answer key says are right (2 wrong).
  So no listing is downgraded to "needs a listing" on its brand field: it stays counted and `unconfirmable`, and the owner
  confirms or replaces it.

## Brand aliases for rungs 0 and 1 (Nov 23)

A listing's brand field counts as the part's brand when it is a recorded alias of it (`core/src/keepa/brandAliases.ts`,
each alias with its listings); every other condition of the rung is unchanged. Derived from the brand fields the rows the
answer key marks right carry: RYOBI ← **TTI** (B09HDZ5MPV, PBP004, right; B096WHWDNB, PCG002, uncertain) and **ONEAND**
(B0B1QRQYK2, PCG002, right). Left out: FIRE ROSE (B0BL8BC8MF) is a seller's brand already read through its manufacturer
field; "ONE+" as a brand field is in no recorded or live row; DEWALT has none. Measured (`npm run measure:autoconfirm`):
recorded rows 87 right, 0 wrong, 215 unknown before and after (no recorded right row carries an alias); the two live
listings 2 wrong before (the catalog check reads them as another brand's) and 2 right after. **Adopted.** The evidence
reads "Amazon lists it under TTI, the maker of RYOBI; its title names PBP004" and "Amazon lists it under ONEAND, Amazon's
spelling of RYOBI ONE+; its title names PCG002". `LADDER_VERSION` is 2, so a stored unknown is read again once (from the
cached pull) and the deals are judged again. B096WHWDNB (TTI, part number PCG002, bulk packaged) would now confirm for
PCG002; the key holds it uncertain, so it counts neither way.

## The manufacturer field for rungs 0 and 1 and the listing judge (Nov 23)

The fifth real scan (`fixtures/e2e-2026-11-23/after-scan5`) found the three best deals so far, RYOBI $99 kits
PSK1212SB-PCL515B (+$43.19), -PCL633B (+$38.79) and -PCL031B (+$26.47), and held each at "Looks worth buying": the catalog
check, rung 1 and the judge's check held any listing whose manufacturer field was not the brand itself, and Amazon's record
for PBP004 (B09HDZ5MPV) and PCL031B (B0CRFS4F6G) says manufacturer **TTI**, for PCG002 (B0B1QRQYK2) **RYOBI Tools**.

**Rule** (`core/src/keepa/brandAliases.ts makerAgrees`, one function for `verify.ts checkIdentity`,
`listingFields.ts listingProblem` and `listingJudge checkListingJudge`): given the brand field is the part's brand (or a
recorded brand alias) and every other condition of the rung holds, a manufacturer field that is (a) a recorded
manufacturer alias of the brand (RYOBI ← TTI, with its listings), or (b) the brand's name followed by the one word
"Tools" is not a reason to hold the listing. Only "Tools" because it is the only company word in any recorded listing
("RYOBI Tools" is also RYOBI's own site's name); "Power Tools", "Inc", "Co", "Industrial" stay held. Anything else stays
unknown as before, and a manufacturer alias never makes another brand in the brand field the part's (a brand field Tolxh
with manufacturer TTI is still contradicted). A confirmation says so: "…; manufacturer TTI: TTI (Techtronic Industries)
makes RYOBI". `LADDER_VERSION` is 3, so a stored unknown is read again once from the cached pull.

**Every manufacturer value recorded with brand field RYOBI or DEWALT** (distinct listings; the Keepa search and pulls, the
two SerpApi product pages, the catalog-check reasons of after-scan3 and after-scan5):

| Brand | Manufacturer | Listings | Reading | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RYOBI | RYOBI | 16 | the brand | already accepted |
| RYOBI | Ryobi | 2 | the brand | already accepted |
| DEWALT | DEWALT | 2 | the brand | already accepted |
| RYOBI | TTI | 2 | **adopted alias** | B09HDZ5MPV (PBP004, key right); B0CRFS4F6G (PCL031B, no key) |
| RYOBI | RYOBI Tools | 2 | **adopted form** | B0B1QRQYK2 (PCG002, key right); B0CM7TMB22 (PCL615B, no key) |
| RYOBI | Partyreal | 1 | not adopted | B08L38QFFW (PSBDD01, key right), but a seller's name nothing ties to RYOBI; stays held |

No DEWALT listing carries another manufacturer, so the "DEWALT Tools" form is written but unmeasured on DEWALT.

**Measured** (`npm run measure:autoconfirm`, `measureManufacturerRule`): rungs 0 and 1 with the rule off and on over 332
rows (every row of `measureIdentity`, the two live listings, and the four after-scan5 listings whose check quotes a
manufacturer, keyed by the validation labels, `fixtures/keepa/identity-labels.json` and `fixtures/audit/corpus.json`):
**4 change from unknown to confirmed: 2 right (PBP004 B09HDZ5MPV, PCG002 B0B1QRQYK2), 0 wrong, 2 with no key (PCL031B
B0CRFS4F6G, PCL615B B0CM7TMB22).** Adopted at zero wrong; each value rests on one right listing. The after-scan5 rows hold
only what the fixture has word for word (stored title and brand, the manufacturer the reason quotes): no part number and
no counts, so they confirm through rung 1's title rule; the real record also had a part number that is the part's (the
check reaches its manufacturer test only after one), and its counts are read again when the listing is.

**Replay** (`replayScan5Deals`: the stored deal rows, history and Keepa sales figures, the app's default numbers $20, 20%,
50 a month): all three become "Worth buying now … its listings confirmed now" (PCL515B and PCL633B: PBP004 and PCG002;
PCL031B: those two and PCL031B). None still waits on the recorded fields; in the app each waits only for the re-read of
its cached pull, which can still hold it if a count field (pack or item count) disagrees.

## Nov 23: suggested prices and "answer once"
Two places the owner's manual work was cut, inside the PO's rules ("A price counts when he confirms it", Nov 20; rule 4;
rule 5). Replayed with `npx tsx scripts/replay-suggestions.ts` (free, no network; `core/test/replay-suggestions.test.ts`).

### Suggested prices: prepared by the agent, counted only when he confirms (core/src/scan/suggest.ts)
- **At most one suggestion per part with no counted price** (`PartRow.priceSuggestion`: price, kind, label, evidence with
  links, fulfillment), only where the price form is open (looked up, not in Review, no Amazon New or confirmed price counting).
  (a) *Amazon listing not for sale now:* Keepa's average New price for that ASIN over 90 days, else 30 ("Amazon listing not for
  sale now; its 90-day average was $146.37 (Keepa)"). The average is Keepa's NEW type (index 1, the lowest New offer), Amazon's
  own (index 0) only when NEW has none: the owner sells as a third party, and the recording shows the gap (DCF921B, B09M3TL9BB:
  $235.14 Amazon, $146.37 lowest New over 90 days). Read from the trend a pull stored (`PartTrend.newAvg90/newAvg30`; every pull
  already asks `stats=180`, which carries avg30/avg90 at no token cost). (b) *No Amazon listing:* the middle of the stored eBay
  listings (the eBay pass searches New, Buy It Now) the judge or the owner marked the same product, with the count and range
  ("6 eBay listings, $220–$390.15, middle $274.48"); fewer than 3: none. An inactive listing never takes the eBay figure, and no
  listing never takes a Keepa one: one source per suggestion. (c) *Home Depot's price for the part sold alone* is his cost side:
  never a suggestion, at most a "Context:" line under one.
- **One click confirms it, as any confirmed price.** `POST /api/deals/:id/parts/:part/price {kind: 'suggested', suggestion}`:
  the agent compares kind and price to the cent with the suggestion the part carries now (409 if a scan changed it), and stores
  an ordinary `ConfirmedPrice` whose link is the suggestion's (the Amazon listing; the eBay listing nearest the middle) and whose
  `source` keeps the label and evidence. So the Nov 20 ruling holds: an eBay figure counts only because he chose it; nothing
  counts by itself. It counts until the Amazon listing is for sale again, as before.
- **The "if accepted" line** (`IncompleteEstimate.ifAccepted`, `DealIncompleteEstimate.ifAccepted`): what the bundle would make
  if he accepted every unpriced part's suggestion, evaluated exactly as a confirmed price would count (fee settings, rule 5).
  Only when every unpriced part has one; a second line in the box, never the headline, never a buy or a signal.
- **The drip pulls the missing averages** (`agent/suggestPrices.ts`, a hook after the fee and sales pulls): each inactive listing
  of a bundle missing a price whose trend has no average field, smallest break-even first, 1 token each through the drip's
  Keepa budget and above the reserve. A trend that says Keepa has none (null) is not asked again.
- **`prices.autoAcceptSuggestions` exists and is always false.** Not in the UI, not patchable, forced off on load: the PO said
  his approval is the answer.
- *Replay (after-scan3):* 39 bundles missing a price; from what is stored, 0 suggestions (the copy holds no Keepa trend and no
  eBay record). The drip would pull 5 averages (P321, DCK225D2, PCL1600K2, PCL206, A10FB1G: 5 tokens), reaching 7 bundles if
  Keepa has an average for each. With the eBay listings recorded on Nov 19 standing in for the eBay pass, 13 bundles get an eBay
  suggestion (DCK200D1P1, 6 of 6 the same, middle $274.48), 9 of them with the "if accepted" line (+$33.72 to +$74.56 est.);
  P553 (2 of 5 the same) gets none, and PCL416B, PCL1400K2 and A72601 have no recording.

### Answer once, apply everywhere (core/src/facts)
- **What is stored, per brand and model** (`partFacts`, an optional collection of the scan store; the agent's
  `scan/partFacts.json`; cleared by Reset scan data with his other answers): `isPart` (a part Home Depot does not sell alone, or
  sells as another model, that he approved as a part), `sellAs` (a special buy or combo sold whole, or as the tools he typed in
  its place), `notSoldSeparately` (in the box, not sold separately), each with the date and the question it came from. *Not
  copied:* a chosen Amazon listing is already the part's user-decided match record and a confirmed price is already keyed by
  brand and model; both already apply in every bundle, so a second copy could only drift.
- **Applied through settle rule 2.** A part Home Depot did not confirm that he already settled is settled with an `owner_fact`
  evidence line ("A986501 is a part of the bundles that name it: your answer on Nov 23 (PCL1600K2-A986501)"), in every current
  bundle (open questions are read again at once, as `withdrawUnreadSplitQuestions` reads them) and every future one. Only the
  question Home Depot left is answered: the page's own contradictions and the tool count are still checked. A part only his fact
  settled has no Home Depot title, so it is not counted as a tool in that check (the count still checks every part Home Depot's
  pages read); without this the owner's own example (PCL406424SB-A21701-A25RS51, "2-Tool") could never settle.
- **What an answer remembers** (Review's "…and remember this for A986501", checked by default, `ReviewAnswerBody.remember`):
  only what it says about a part the question asked about (`unsettledParts`). His parts hold it: `isPart` (or `sellAs` whole for
  a special buy). His parts replace a special buy with tools, keeping the reading's other parts: `sellAs` those tools. He leaves
  a part out, or answers None: nothing, since leaving a part out of one bundle says nothing about another (rule 4).
- **"In the box, not sold separately"** (the inspector's link on a no-listing part, `POST /api/facts`): the part is not counted,
  as an item with no model number, and is no reason for an incomplete estimate. **Newer evidence wins:** when Home Depot's reading
  finds it sold alone, or its Amazon listing is seen for sale, after the answer, the fact is not applied and the row says what
  changed ("Home Depot sells A10FB1G on its own since Nov 25 ($12.97)."), with Keep my answer (a new date) and Change. It asks
  on the part row, once, not as a Review question.
- **Change** (`DELETE /api/facts/:key?field=`) forgets the answer and reads again every split it settled, so those bundles are
  asked about again. Undo of the Review answer undoes that bundle only; the fact stays until Change.
- *Replay (after-scan3):* 9 open questions (6 split, 3 match). Two split questions ask about a named part (A986501, A10FB1G),
  one question each: one answer per distinct part settles 0 others in this copy. The other 4 have no single part (no reading,
  or the page names PBP003/PBP006 that no part explains). Match questions were already per model (DCF860B is held by 2 bundles).
  The test of "one answer settles the others" (`core/test/part-facts.test.ts`, `agent/test/partFacts.test.ts`) uses the real
  PCL1600K2-A986501 record under a second item id as the stand-in for the owner's live data, where parts repeat.

### Free scan, fix round: the first live scan read 0 bundles (Nov 23)
The installed app's first free scan said "Home Depot bundle lists · read in Chrome · 0 bundles · 0 searches" and every
later step "none found": both `bundleListing` jobs answered `{"products": []}`, while the same two paths fetched by hand
from a product-page tab in the owner's Chrome held 12 (RYOBI) and 3 (DEWALT) `base-searchNav-<id>` entries. The path was
neither rewritten nor refused (agent and helper pass it as is); the helper parsed a state that held no product.
- *What differed from the working fetch, and is changed:* (1) the reader parsed only the first `window.__APOLLO_STATE__=`
  block of the answer (`fetchPathInTab` cut at its first `</script>`), so a page whose first block is not the listing's
  reads as empty; every block is now kept and read (`hdPage.ts parseListingText`, items merged in page order). (2) the
  doorway was the list's own address; the owner's working fetch was made from another page, so the Bundle reader's doorway
  is now the brand's search page (`/s/ryobi`, `/s/dewalt`). Which of the two was the cause is not known offline: no
  recording of the empty answer exists. *Live check:* the next scan's step list and its run line.
- **An empty list is never "0 bundles"** (`LIST_EMPTY`): a list whose first page comes back with its state but no product
  is a problem said in words, with what the state held ("its state had N entries, none a product; kinds: …", from the
  helper), and the other lists are still read. When every list is empty, the step fails "bundle list empty", the bundles
  found before stay as they were, and SerpApi's listing pages run instead only where the owner allowed a search
  (`scan.listingSource` auto, and a scan he started or `serpWhenChromeClosed`; `ScanDeps.chromeListing.serpFallback`).
- **Prices come from the list when its entries carry them.** The live entries hold a
  `pricing({"isBrandPricingPolicyCompliant":false,"storeId":"121"})` entry with `value`, `original` and
  `conditionalPromotions` (the old recordings were trimmed to identifiers). `parseSearchState` reads it as a product page's
  pricing is read (two entries that disagree give no price) and its BOGO promotions as `bogoPromotionsOf` does; such an
  item's page is not read for its price (`pricedByList`), and the bundle pass still reads its page for the Includes list.
  A row carries `bogo` (the list or page showed a BOGO promotion); the BOGO pass still decides the pairs.
- **Each brand's Bundle list is read twice: with the search words and without them** (`/b/N-5yc1vZbwo64Z4j2`, `?Nao=12` for
  later pages, the form given by the controller; `isBundleListingPath` accepts exactly these), and the two are united.
  DEWALT's list for "dewalt 20v combo kit" held 3 entries live. The job keeps each list's pages, items and how many it
  added (`ScanJob.chromeListing.lists`), and the run line says "DEWALT: 3 with the search words, 37 more without them" so a
  live run shows which lists more. Neither list is dropped until a run shows that.

- **Windows owner-only files, second pass** (Nov 23, after the first windows-latest smoke run). The run showed `agent-token`, `keys.json` and `pairing.json` with the folder's three inherited entries (SYSTEM, Administrators, the user) although `winAcl.ts` narrows the temp file before the content goes in. The cause is not proven from here: Microsoft documents that `MoveFileEx` keeps the moved file's security descriptor on a same-volume move and only a cross-volume move assigns the destination's default (learn.microsoft.com, MoveFileExW, Remarks), so the rename is not the established culprit; the only silent path was an `icacls` failure whose single `console.warn` went to a windowless sidecar nobody reads. Now: (1) the narrowing is applied again on the FINAL path after the rename (idempotent) and read back with `icacls <file>`, parsed (one explicit entry for the current user, by SID or name, no `(I)`); (2) when that does not hold, one sentence goes to Activity errors, "Windows did not let Split Finder restrict keys.json to your account: <reason>. It is readable by this PC's administrators.", and the write is never failed; (3) only the three secret files are narrowed. The other store files hold no keys and keep %APPDATA%'s inherited per-user permissions (user, SYSTEM, Administrators), which is the normal Windows state and which a standard other user cannot read; the smoke script checks the three files (SID comparison, no `(I)`) and that the store folder grants nothing to Users, Authenticated Users or Everyone, printing the entries on failure. The next real run settles the cause: if the Activity sentence appears, its reason names it. *For v2:* the OS credential store instead of files.
### Free scan, fix round 2: the list is fetched in the page's own world, and paged on Home Depot's final address (Nov 23)
The second live scan read 6 bundles. Hand fetches in the owner's Chrome (from the `/s/ryobi` doorway, in the console:
the page's MAIN world) got 12 `base-searchNav` entries a page and `"totalProducts":24` (with the search words) or `101`
(without them), while the helper's own job got 6 a page, or for the query list a state of other kinds (36 entries:
numeric ids, ROOT_QUERY, "BundleI…"). The helper fetched with `chrome.scripting.executeScript` in the **ISOLATED**
world (`ReaderEnv.runInTab`, as every reader does), `credentials: 'include'`, no headers of its own.
- **The Bundle list reader now fetches in the page's own world** (`ReaderEnv.runInPage`, `world: 'MAIN'`), as the
  owner's working fetch was made; the doorway, settle, pause and refusal rules are unchanged. Every other reader stays in
  the ISOLATED world (their pages read right there). *Live check:* whether the page world answers 12 a page.
- **Paging follows the redirect.** `/b/N-5yc1vZbwo64Zm5d` answers from `/b/RYOBI/Bundle/N-5yc1vZm5dZbwo64`, and a Nao on the
  first address is dropped by that redirect, so the reader returns the answer's same-origin final address
  (`fetchPathInTab` `finalPath`, from `res.url`) and the scan pages on it (`withNao`), each offset by the entries the
  pages so far returned, until the list's own `totalProducts` (the first `"totalProducts":<n>` in the state) is reached, a
  page adds nothing, or 10 pages. Both path checks accept that canonical shape: `/b/`, up to three name segments of
  letters, digits and `-`, the N- value with exactly the base, the Bundle token and one brand token in either order, an
  optional `/Ntk-elasticplus/Ntt-<query>`, and only `NCNI-5` and `Nao=<n>` as parameters, each once.
- **A state with entries and no product is sampled, not guessed.** The reader returns `sample` (hdPage.ts `sampleOf`): each
  key's kind (runs of 5+ digits written `#`) with its count, and the first entry of the 3 largest kinds as JSON cut to
  1,500 characters, any property named like a credential (token, cookie, session, auth, secret, password, csrf) dropped.
  The agent checks its shape, keeps it on the list, and the run's Activity details carry every list
  (`RunRecord.bundleLists`: total, pages, products found, the address paged on, the sample).
  `npx tsx scripts/record-listing-sample.ts <app data folder>` writes the newest sample to
  `fixtures/homedepot-pages/listing-sample-<date>.json`; the other shape is parsed only once that fixture exists.
- **Per list counts** in the run line: "RYOBI: 24 of 24, 2 pages with the search words, 77 more without them (101 of 101,
  9 pages)".

- **Windows secret files: the OpenSSH ACL, not a user-only ACL** (Nov 23, after run 4 on windows-latest; supersedes the user-only target in the entry above). Run 4 showed the narrowing did run: the three secret files carried explicit, non-inherited `SYSTEM:(F)`, `Administrators:(F)`, `<user>:(F)`. The verifier then wrongly recorded 4 errors, because it demanded one entry. The target is the state Microsoft's OpenSSH for Windows uses for key files: `icacls /inheritance:r /grant` for administrators and SYSTEM only, with `*S-1-5-32-544` by SID for other languages (learn.microsoft.com, OpenSSH key management, "Host key generation" and "Administrative user"); ours adds the owner. SYSTEM and administrators can take any file anyway, and removing them breaks backup and repair tools. `restrictToOwner` runs `/inheritance:r /grant:r *<user SID>:F *S-1-5-18:F *S-1-5-32-544:F`; the verifier accepts exactly that set (the user present, SYSTEM and Administrators optional, no inherited entry, no other account, no deny) and records an error only when the final state differs. Names are matched as icacls prints them (English); a localized Windows reports and is never accepted by guess. Only `agent-token`, `keys.json` and `pairing.json` take this path: `store.lock.<pid>.<hex>.tmp` went through it because `takeLock` writes 0600, and a lock holds no secret. The smoke script checks the same set by SID and fails on any recorded agent error; the store test no longer reads the first Activity error by position. Guide and security review say: your keys are readable by your Windows account and by this PC's administrators, the same as SSH keys.
### Free scan, fix round 3: Home Depot's Bundle filter lists "sets", and the keyword search is read too (Nov 23)
The reader's sample on the first live run of fix round 2 showed the cause (recorded:
`fixtures/homedepot-pages/bundle-list-ryobi-2026-11-23.json`, `bundle-listing-sample-2026-11-23.json`): the Bundle filter
(`bwo64`) lists products of type `CONFIGURABLE_BUNDLE` with `modelNumber` null (brandName null on 4 of the 12), canonical
address `/p/sets/<slug>/<itemId>`, `bundleFlag` true and `bundleItems` referring to `BundleItems:<id>` entries
`{id, quantity}`: Home Depot's own list of the set's items (2 to 5 in the recording). The parser required a model number
and dropped all 12. The fetch was never the problem.
- **The listing parser keeps every entry** (`hdPage.ts parseListingState`), keyed by item id, with its model or null, its
  productType, bundleFlag, its items resolved (a reference that does not resolve to `{id, quantity}` gives no item list),
  its pricing `value`/`original` and `promotion.dollarOff`/`percentageOff`. `listingSource.ts listedOf` hands a set on with
  model '' and `set`. An entry of a Bundle list with no brand name is that list's brand (the list is filtered by it).
- **A set is a bundle kind** (`BundleRecord.set`, model ''): key its item id as every bundle's, price its `value`, was-price
  its `original` (the saving is original − value), and the step after the lists reads each set item's own page free in
  Chrome (`runner.ts readSetItems`, the item page reader the lists' prices use; one read per distinct item, within
  `pageReadsPerScan`) for its brand, model, title and price sold alone. The reading is kept on the set and saved as the
  item's Home Depot part price, so the normal part pipeline takes it from there. The split (`split.ts setSplit`) is the
  items' models, each as many times as its quantity, `resolved`, with the evidence "Home Depot lists this set's N items"
  and the set's link (`hd_set`): settled by Home Depot's own data, so no Review question and no AI. Until every item's page
  is read the set waits (no question; the row says it waits for its pages); an item whose page names another brand leaves
  it unsettled (rule 4). A set's own page is not read (it says nothing the split needs).
- **No model number, everywhere a model was assumed:** deals and the alerts name a set "Set <item id>" (`views.ts setModel`,
  signals, Keepa snapshots), the CSV's model cell and file name say the same, and the rows show "Set · N items" in place
  of the mono model line (`DealRow.setItems`).
- **Three lists per brand, unioned by item id:** the Bundle list with the query, the Bundle list without it (paged on the
  redirected address to its `totalProducts`), and the plain keyword search page `/s/<query>?NCNI-5` (the recording's
  `searchReport.searchUrl`), which lists the classic model-numbered combos SerpApi's rows were (SerpApi ignored the filter
  tokens and returned this search). The search is read to `SEARCH_PAGES_MAX` (2 pages, about SerpApi's 24 rows); every
  list at most 10 pages, all together at most `LISTING_PAGES_PER_SCAN` (30). The run line names each list with what it
  says it has, what was read, in how many pages, "page limit" when a limit cut it ("read 120 of 400"), and how many it
  added to the union.
- *Replay on the fixtures* (`core/test/scan-sets.test.ts`): the RYOBI list for "ryobi 18v combo kit" gives 12 sets
  (12 of its 24 on the recorded page), 35 item places, 22 distinct items to read; with readings every set settles with
  no question. The item pages are not recorded, so the test's readings are made up.
- *Live checks:* the set items' own pages (does `/p/<item id>` answer with the product state parsePromoPage reads, for
  every item, including accessories with no model number, which today keep their set waiting); the keyword search's
  answer in the page world and whether its Nao pages; the DEWALT lists; and whether a set and a classic combo can be the
  same product under two item ids.

## Outcomes: what he bought, what became of it, and a scorecard (Nov 23)

The two numbers that decide the two-week trial were not measurable: profit actually realised on deals the app flagged, and how
far the estimate was from what was really made. This is the smallest honest way to record them. (`core/src/outcomes/outcomes.ts`
is the pure logic, `agent/outcomes.ts` the store side, `desktop-ui/src/lib/scorecard.ts` the scorecard's words.)

- **A purchase freezes the estimate** (`snapshotOf`). "I bought this" copies, once, the deal's parts (model, quantity, Amazon
  listing and link, grade), each part's estimated sell price, referral and fulfillment fee and after-fees amount, the bundle's
  estimated profit, ROI, cost, parts total and net revenue, the fee settings of that moment, the basis sentence and whether the
  last scan had flagged it Buy now. The purchase holds that copy (`structuredClone`); no later scan, price change or fee setting
  reaches it, and the only edit route (`patchPurchase`) names date, price paid and quantity and refuses any other key, so the
  snapshot cannot be rewritten even by a hand-made request. Accuracy compares what he made with this, never with today's estimate.
  A deal with no complete estimate can be bought (the snapshot's `estimate` is null; it is left out of accuracy); a deal with no
  split cannot (no parts to follow).
- **Sale results are worked out from the snapshot, never from the current settings.** A part sold with only a price takes
  `amazonNet` with the snapshot's own rate and fulfillment fee for that part and is labelled `fees_estimated`; a net payout he
  gives wins (`payout`) and the label goes. Returned or not sold settles the part with what came back (0 by default). Still
  holding settles nothing. One status per part per purchase, the sale price is per unit and applies to all the part's units
  (quantity bought times its quantity in the bundle); selling a part in several lots is not supported in version 1 (enter an
  average or the payout). Rewrite: split a part into lots.
- **Cost is shared across parts by their estimated net** (`costShare`), since Home Depot sells the bundle, not the parts. This is
  an allocation, said so in the guide; it only matters for a partly sold deal's realised figure. A complete deal's actual profit is
  net received less cost, with no allocation. The cost is his price paid with the snapshot's tax and inbound settings applied the
  way the estimate counts a bundle's cost, so with nothing changed the cost equals the estimate's and the difference is the sales.
- **Realised this month counts parts settled in the month** (by their date); "N of M parts" counts every part of the deals that
  had one. Accuracy is over completed deals only (every part sold or returned) that had an estimate, in dollars and as a percent
  of the estimate's size (null against an estimate of zero), per deal and summed.
- **Flagged against bought** is backed by records only: the count flagged now (current rows) and, of the purchases, how many were
  flagged when bought (kept in the snapshot). There is no history of every deal ever flagged, so no "flagged ever" is shown.
- **The collection is his records, not scan data.** `outcomes.json` goes through the store (single-writer lock, atomic writes,
  set aside if unreadable) and is in the audit file and in Reset scan data's backup, but `RESET_COLLECTIONS` does not list it, so
  Reset never empties it. Each change keeps one entry (`before` and `after` of the purchase) for Undo, the newest 100, shown as
  sentences in Activity and GET /api/outcomes; Undo works while the purchase is still what that change left it, so changes are
  taken back newest first (409 with a sentence otherwise). Removing a record is such a change and undoes to the whole snapshot.
- **Routes** (`/api/outcomes`, all `ui` auth: the extension has no use for them): GET, GET export.csv, POST (record), PATCH and
  DELETE `/:id`, POST `/:id/parts/:part`, POST `/entries/:id/undo`. Validation lives in core and answers one sentence (400);
  dates are real calendar days not later than tomorrow (time zones), sale dates not before the purchase.
- **UI:** Today's Scorecard (three rows, one disclosure, empty state with no $0), **Bought** beside the Deals views (his records:
  a bought deal may have left the list), I bought this in the inspector and the row menu, a Bought tag on rows, a sentence per
  change in Activity. Money typed with a comma only reads as thousands ("1,179.50"); "12,5" is refused, never read as 125.
- *Not done, for the rewrite:* a purchase of a deal whose price was seen long ago has no stale warning; the estimate at purchase
  is the agent's current one, not one re-checked against Home Depot; there is no multi-lot sale of a part and no per-sale fee
  detail; accuracy has no breakdown by cause (price, fees, sale price).
## Honest spend and honest progress (Nov 23, after the owner's scan 5)

### The reserve is spent only on the owner's explicit say-so, also on a manual scan

The owner started a scan expecting 0 searches (Home Depot's lists are read free in Chrome). Keepa had 7 tokens, so the
Amazon lookups fell back to SerpApi and the manual scan spent 17 searches, from 97 to 80, under his reserve of 100,
without a word. A manual scan used reserve 0 (`budgetPolicyFor(.., 'manual')`): "he pressed the button" was taken as
leave to spend the reserve, but he pressed a button that said nothing about searches, and Home Depot's part of the scan
is free now. A reserve is money he told the app to keep, so it is spent only when he says so about that spend.
- **Scan now, Read again and Look up now keep the reserve**, as a scheduled scan does (`work.ts reserveTrigger`); they
  spend only what the account holds above it, under the per-scan limit. At or below it a scan spends no search: lookups
  go to Keepa or wait, and the summary says how many wait and what the one action may spend ("Searches are at your reserve
  (97 searches left, you keep 100), so this scan spent none. To look these up, use searches for them: up to 18 of your 97
  left."). Above the reserve a scan stops at the reserve rather than crossing it (with 105 left and a limit of 18 it
  spends 5); the rest waits and the same action offers it. This is stricter than "nothing changes above the reserve" for
  that one case, and it is the case the rule is about: a scan that quietly crosses the line is the bug.
- **The explicit yes is `POST /api/lookups/run { useReserve: true }`**, sent only by the button "Use searches for these (up
  to N of your M left)" on Deals when the balance is at or below the reserve. N is the least of the parts waiting, the
  per-scan limit and what is left; `useReserve` uses the manual policy (reserve 0), the limit still binds. Without it the
  route keeps the reserve whoever sends it.
- **Every control that starts a scan says what it may spend before the press** (`desktop-ui/src/lib/scanSpend.ts`): "May
  use up to 18 searches for Amazon prices; reading Home Depot is free." (the least of the limit and what is above the
  reserve), or "Uses no searches: 97 searches left, at your reserve of 100." Scan now is no longer disabled at the reserve:
  it is free to read Home Depot, so it can run.
- Not changed: Look up and Verify on one bundle (the owner's click, the cost on the button) still use the manual policy.

### The balance shown is the last account figure less the ledger rows after it

`serpLeft` came only from the account check at a scan's start, so it was 17 too high after the scan. Now it is
`searchesLeftNow(check, ledgerRows)` (`core/src/serpapi/balance.ts`, pure): the check's figure less the rows of
`serpapi-ledger.jsonl` written after the check's time. The agent keeps the rows in memory (read once from the file, so a
restart gets the same number) and takes the rows a new check already counts out of it. The free account check is made
again when a scan ends, through the same `SerpApiClient.account` path; a failed check leaves the corrected figure.
Rung 2's gate reads the corrected figure; every spend is still held to a fresh check at its own start.

### The scan line names the long work and shows no time from a guess

The line said "Step 8 of 8, finishing up, less than a minute left" for about ten minutes: the runner's own steps were all
done, the AI pass on the Review cards (and the follow-ups, and the listings rung 2 reads) came after it, and the view was
the runner's last. The desktop app now adds two steps after the runner, `review` ("Review cards 3 of 9") and `confirm`
("Listing confirmation"), each counted per item, and "Finishing up" is only the digest and the messages. eBay and the
listing check use the words "eBay prices 34 of 80" and "Listing check" in the app (the extension keeps the spec's).
The time left is shown only from a measured rate (this scan's own after 3 timed items, else the median of earlier scans):
a seed is a guess and is not shown, so the answer is "no time", not "less than a minute". The AI's per-card time is kept
for the next scan (`askAi`). The extension is unchanged.
### Home Depot's pace: read less, slower, in a better order (Nov 23)
What was refused (`fixtures/e2e-2026-11-23/after-scan5`, scans.json and activity.json): scan 5 read 22 list pages, then
25 bundle pages (three at a time, a second apart: the helper's page reader ran `concurrency 3`, `pauseMs 1000`) and 15 set
item pages, some 62 pages in about five minutes, plus the BOGO listings before them. Home Depot then answered "Error Page"
("Home Depot refused a set item page; the rest are read next scan"); the part-price step was skipped and 139 sets were
left waiting. Reading on at that rate would never clear them, and a refusal is never retried or worked around (no loop,
no header trick, no new tab, no SerpApi search in its place). The only fixes are to read less, slower, in a better order.

- **One gate for every Home Depot read of the agent** (`core/src/scan/hdPace.ts HdPace`, the agent's `AgentWork.pace`,
  `remoteReaders(..., gate)`): lists, bundle pages, set item pages, kit pages, part prices (a search and a page: two),
  BOGO pages and an AI question's research pages, in a scan and between scans. One read at a time; a page job asks for one
  page (`onePageAtATime`), so the gap holds between pages too. State in `runtime.hdPace` (survives a restart).
  - `HD_GAP_MS` 10 s + `HD_GAP_JITTER_MS` 0 to 5 s from the end of one read to the start of the next, whatever its kind: a
    person reads a product page every 10 to 60 s; scan 5 read three a second.
  - `HD_PAGES_PER_SCAN` 40, every kind together (scan 5 was refused at about 62). A scan, Read again and an AI ask each
    start their count at 0 (`beginRun` at claim). `pageReadsPerScan` (60) and the passes' own limits still hold beneath it.
  - Cool-down: the first refusal pauses every read for `HD_COOLDOWN_MS` 30 min, the rest of the scan included; a refusal
    right after a cool-down doubles it (1 h, 2 h, 4 h), never past `HD_COOLDOWN_MAX_MS` 8 h; a page read resets it. A
    refusal is a page outcome `refused`, a list or research answer that says it refused, or a part price `not_read` (that
    status also covers a page the helper could not parse; it counts as a refusal, the careful side). Chrome not answering
    is not a refusal. During the cool-down a scan reads no Home Depot page, searches no list on SerpApi in its place
    (`discoveryFor hdPaused`), runs its Amazon steps as usual and says "Home Depot paused reading until 6:40 PM; the rest
    continue then."; Read again is refused with that sentence; a pasted Home Depot link (mode `owner`: the gap and the
    cool-down, no cap) says it too.
  - Between scans: `HD_DRIP_GAP_MS` 90 s, so at most 40 pages an hour, and `HD_DRIP_PAGES_PER_DAY` 240 (six hours of it).
  - Settings do not expose the cap: no obvious place, and a number the owner raises is the one that gets him refused.
- **The order** (`core/src/scan/hdOrder.ts`): bundles and sets by their Home Depot saving: any saving before none, the
  biggest percent off first (the listing's `percentOff`, the set's own `percentageOff`, or was − now), then dollars off,
  then newest. A set's unread items are queued together, so the first pages read finish whole sets; an item several sets
  list is one page (it already was; `pagesWanted` and `readSetItemsFor` keep it so, tested). Bundle pages and set item
  pages share one plan (`pagePlan`) cut at what the scan may still read. A part price that completes an estimate (a waiting
  split of a bundle whose other part already has an Amazon price) is read before every other part, and a scan keeps its
  two pages each (`priceReserveOf`) before the page passes take theirs. *Changed for every caller:* the command line and
  the tests read bundle pages in this order now, not newest first.
- **Lists at most once a day** (`listingSource.ts`, a cursor per list in the scan cache, `homedepot:list:<brand>:<kind>:<query>`):
  a list read through (its end, or its own page limit) is not read again for `HD_LIST_REREAD_MS` 22 h (not 24, so a scan
  at the same hour each day still reads it); one the scan's pages (or a refusal, a stop) cut continues at its next page the
  next scan. The BOGO listings the same way (`runtime.bogoReadAt`, set only when every brand was read through). Prices a
  list carries are therefore refreshed daily, not every scan.
- **The slow read between scans** (`AgentWork.hdDripOne`, run by the tick's `drip()`): while the helper is connected, the
  agent is not paused, nothing else runs and Home Depot is not paused, one read each time its gap has passed, in the scan's
  order (completing part price, then the pages bundles wait for, then a kit page, then the other part prices). What it
  settles goes through the scan's own code: `readSetItemsFor` saves each item on every set that lists it and as the item's
  part price, `splitOne` splits the sets it finished, `settleWaitingSplits` the waiting splits, then the deals are rebuilt
  and their signals judged (`refresh`); the Keepa drip looks the new parts up as before. No SerpApi, no Keepa, no AI in it.
  One Activity run an hour: "Read 2 Home Depot pages between scans (free); 1 bundle settled. 138 sets wait for 218 …". The
  Keepa drip no longer reads Home Depot itself (its part-price and kit-page reads moved here).
- **Honest words, from the constants** (`backlogSentence`): "139 sets wait for 220 Home Depot pages; between scans at most
  40 are read an hour (240 a day), and each scan reads up to 40.", the pause first while paused. In the scan's summary in
  place of "— Read again (free)", in `AgentState.agent.homeDepot` (figures and sentence), and on each row waiting for its
  page (`DealRow.pageWait`, the waiting tag's hover). No finishing time is promised: the rate is a ceiling.
- **Replayed on after-scan5** (`core/test/hd-order.test.ts`): 139 sets wait for **220 distinct item pages** (347 item
  places; 74 items are listed by more than one set). The 71 sets with a saving need 106 of them, all read first. The drip
  alone reads the 220 in **5.5 hours** at 40 an hour (106 in 2.65 h), within its 240 a day; each scan adds up to 40 (less
  its lists, once a day 22 pages, and its part prices). 11 parts wait for a part price, 1 of them completing an estimate
  (PSBCK102K2), read first.
- *Not changed:* the unpaired extension's own scan (side panel) still reads with its own reader's `concurrency 3`; every
  agent job now asks it for one page. Each page job still loads its doorway first (the doorway rule), so Home Depot sees
  up to two loads a counted page. The UI's raw `POST /api/worker/jobs` is not paced.
- *Live checks:* that 40 pages a scan at 10 to 15 s apart and 40 an hour between scans are not refused (the first refusal
  shows in Activity with its time and what was read); that a scan's length stays reasonable (40 pages at about 20 s each
  is some 13 minutes of reading); the helper still answers one-id page jobs as before.

## Nov 23: Settings, Scanning was blank; the accuracy percent near zero
- **Settings, Scanning showed a blank window** in the build first prepared for the PM. Cause: the "Recommended schedule…" button (added Nov 23) called `useUi()`, which exists only in the main window; the Preferences window is a page of its own (main.tsx). Found while recording a walkthrough, not by a test. Fix: the button reads the context if there is one, and from Preferences asks the main window through a localStorage `storage` event (lib/runStep.ts), the way the look is carried across windows. If the main window is closed to the menu bar the sheet opens there unseen; accepted for now. **Guard:** a test walks every Preferences pane and every component a pane imports and fails on any `useUi()` call, and a script (scratch, to be moved into the repo's checks when the UI gets a browser test) opens all eight tabs.
- **No accuracy percent against an estimate within $5 of zero.** The first recorded sample made +$9.70 against −$0.99 estimated and read "+1079.8%". The dollar difference always shows; the percent shows only when the estimate is at least $5 either way (`MIN_ESTIMATE_FOR_PERCENT`).


## Nov 24: a wrong "Confirmed listing": a title that names another model; a price far above Home Depot's

**The example** (`fixtures/keepa/catalog-wrong-part-number-B0DGLWKS9S.json`). The set "Adaptor Plate for TOUGHSYSTEM 2.0
and 10-Compartment Shallow Pro Small Parts Organizer" ($54.87) holds DEWALT DWST08017, $39.99 at Home Depot sold alone.
Amazon's own record for B0DGLWKS9S says brand DEWALT, part number DWST08017, model DWST08017, but the listing is "DWST60122-1
Dewalt Pro Backpack x McLaren F1 Team, Limited Edition" at $389. The catalog rule (rung 0) confirmed it, round 2's re-pick
switched the part to it from B0C3TN1Z7P, and the set read +$259.45, ROI 473%, "Confirmed listing". The owner caught it by
eye. An audit of his 69 matched listings found this one wrong and no other.

**Why the measured rules missed it.** The title guard (`titleOrCountProblem`'s "title names X, not MODEL") reads model
numbers with `MODEL_PATTERNS`, written and measured on tools: DEWALT's is DC… only, so "DWST60122-1" was no model to it.
Every "zero wrong" measured so far was measured on tools, batteries, chargers and kits. Home Depot's sets (Nov 23) brought
a new population, DEWALT storage, hand tools and accessories (DWST…, DWMT…, DWHT…, DW…) and RYOBI accessories (A98401,
RHCM04 …), which no measured rule had seen. **A "zero wrong" holds only for the populations it was measured on**; a new
source of parts needs the measurements run again on its own listings.

**Rule 1: a title that names another model of the brand's family holds the confirmation** (`match.ts FAMILY_PATTERNS`,
`otherModelNamed`). `MODEL_PATTERNS` is not widened: it also finds and chooses listings (title matching, search), and a
wider pattern there was not measured. `FAMILY_PATTERNS` is used for one thing only, to stop a confirmation by structured
fields: rungs 0 and 1 and the judge's check (`titleOrCountProblem`, through `listingProblem`), the re-pick switch (it
confirms through rungs 0 and 1), and the grader's part number match (`grade`: strong becomes review, a question, when
the title names another model of the family). The forms are those the recorded titles hold (`familyForms`): DEWALT DW,
up to three letters, three to six digits, then letters or digits and an optional "-digit" (100 forms recorded, e.g.
DWST08017, DWST60122-1, DWMT74195, DW0100, DWAMF1235RL), plus the DC form; RYOBI A and five or six digits, A with two
digits, one or two letters and one to three digits (A98401, A32HF01, A10FB1G, A90FS01A), RH…, FV…, STM… (15 forms), plus
the P form. The part's own model named in the title clears it as before. The reason reads "its title names DWST60122-1,
not DWST08017".

| Measured (`npx tsx scripts/measure-wrong-confirm.ts`) | Rows | Changed | Right | Right lost | Wrong after |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rungs 0 and 1: every `measureIdentity` row, the two live listings, after-scan5's quoted checks, the audit corpus (279), the example | 612 | 1 | 1 (B0DGLWKS9S) | 0 | 0 |
