| The part number grade: recorded Keepa products whose part number is the part's | 16 | 3 | 1 (B0DGLWKS9S) | 0 | — |
| The listing judge's check: 215 recorded judgements, and a judge that says "same" to everything | 215 | 0 | — | 0 | 0 |
| after-scan5's 99 automatic matches, on their stored titles | 99 | 0 | — | 0 | — |

The part number grade's two other changes are listings whose key is uncertain, each a title that names another written
model (B0DY31QJ4Q for PCL001K names PCL001B; B09SN6Y6TS for P215 names P215BN): a question is the right answer to both.

**Rule 2: the price guard** (`core/src/scan/priceGuard.ts`). An automatic match whose counted Amazon price (the
featured price while active, as the report counts it) is more than **4×** Home Depot's own price for the part sold alone
(its own page, `matchedModel` the part) is not counted, whatever confirmed it: the part goes to Review's "Which listing"
(`hold.ts holdForOwner`) with "Amazon's price is 9.7× Home Depot's price for this part: check the listing", the held
listing first and the part's other listings after it, Home Depot's page of the part sold alone among the question's
sources and both links in the match's record. A lower grade, never a rejection (no `rejectedAsins`): the owner may choose
the same listing, and his answer (decidedBy user) stands. With the part in review the bundle has no estimate ("1 of 2
priced: DWST08017 review") and no buy. The guard reads only the store, so it runs free at a scan's end (`postScanWork`)
and before the agent publishes a report (`rebuild`, never beside a scan).

| Amazon ÷ Home Depot, 265 listings with both prices | Listings | Key: the part | Key: not | Uncertain | No key (stored, counted) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| below 0.25× | 82 | 0 | 82 | 0 | 0 |
| 0.25–0.5× | 58 | 4 | 52 | 0 | 2 |
| 0.5–0.8× | 51 | 12 | 30 | 6 | 3 |
| 0.8–1.25× | 52 | 9 | 21 | 4 | 18 |
| 1.25–2× | 16 | 1 | 11 | 2 | 2 |
| 2–3× | 3 | 0 | 2 | 0 | 1 (A98401, 2.80×) |
| 3–6× | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| above 6× | 3 | 0 | 3 (B0F3N79KTM for PCL406/PCL406B 6.72×; B0DGLWKS9S 9.73×) | 0 | 0 |

Sources: every matched listing of the three stored scans (`prices.json` over `partPrices.json`), every audit corpus pair
whose part Home Depot priced (`fixtures/validation/hd-part-prices-2026-11-18.json`), and the example. **The threshold**:
listings keyed as the part reach 1.63×; listings the owner's scans counted reach 2.80× (A98401 on B09V1PG5R1, $27.93
against $9.97; the owner's live data, every right listing at or below 2.8×); above that only wrong listings, the lowest at
6.72×. 4× sits in the gap (its geometric middle is 4.3×, rounded down toward catching), with room on both sides.
Measured: 3 rows above it, all wrong; 0 right held; 0 of after-scan5's matches changed. **The mirror (far below Home
Depot's) is not adopted**: below 0.25× all 82 rows are wrong, but they are search rows the grader already rejects
(`match-corpus.test.ts`), no listing any rung confirmed or counted was below 0.41×, and right listings reach 0.41× (DCB205,
$68.95 against $169), too thin a margin for a rule with no case to answer.

**Stored data is read again** (`catalogCheck.ts`, `LADDER_VERSION` 4, `CONFIRMATION_LADDER`). Every stored automatic
confirmation by fields (grade strong, verifiedBy keepa_catalog, serpapi_product or listing_fields) with no confirmed check
at ladder 4 is queued once, from its cached pull (the pull that confirmed it), under the current rules: confirmed, the
check is recorded and nothing else changes; contradicted, removed as before (a re-pick falls back to the listing it switched
from, as a title match); unknown, held for the owner as rule 2 holds (`change: 'held'`). The price guard needs no version:
it reads the stored prices each time. The owner's B0DGLWKS9S is withdrawn by both without a scan, a search or AI.

**Replay** (`replayScan5`, `core/test/price-guard.test.ts`): after-scan5 itself changes nowhere under either rule. The
set with the example's match as the owner's app stored it (Home Depot's $39.99, B0DGLWKS9S at $389, DWST14925 as stored,
B0799RHJ7W $14.88, the app's default fees): before, "confirmed", +$288.43 (+$259.45 with the owner's own fee settings);
after, no estimate, "1 of 2 priced: DWST08017 review", the question "Which listing" offering B0DGLWKS9S, B0C3TN1Z7P,
B0HMV5V9L7 and B0HQQZWYTX.

*Known gaps:* the judge's own quote check reads model numbers with `MODEL_PATTERNS` and so never confirms a DW… or A…
model (a question, the safe side); the family forms are the recorded ones, and a third brand or a new line of models needs
its own; the price guard needs Home Depot's price for the part sold alone, so a part with none (a set item never sold
alone) is guarded by rule 1 only.
## Checking the app's own work (Nov 24, after a wrong "Confirmed listing" reached the owner)

What happened (`fixtures/keepa/catalog-wrong-part-number-B0DGLWKS9S.json`): Amazon's catalog record of B0DGLWKS9S, a $389
DWST60122-1 backpack, carries part number DWST08017, a $39.99 TOUGHSYSTEM adaptor plate. The catalog rule confirmed it, and
the set "Adaptor Plate … and 10-Compartment … Organizer" ($54.87) read +$259.45, ROI 473%. The owner caught it by eye. The
rule itself is being fixed apart (keepa/verify.ts, a price guard at confirmation). This is a second line that does not care
which rule erred: it reads only what the app decided and stored (the deals, `BundleRow`), so it catches this class of mistake
whatever produced it.

- **Where** (`core/src/audit/sanity.ts`, pure; `agent/signalsPass.ts checkOwnWork`): every time the deals are written
  (`work.ts publish`: after a scan, after every rebuild between scans, after an owner's answer), the checks run and their
  findings are stored beside the deals (`deals.sanity`). A `hold` is passed to the signals (`SignalOpts.held`, a scan's and
  the between-scans pass alike): the deal is never Buy now and never "looks worth buying", its verdict starts "Needs a look:".
  A held buy keeps its `buyPending` bookkeeping, so the catalog check still reads its listings first (the contradicted case
  is still removed by the rule). A `note` is shown, holds nothing. Nothing changes a match or an estimate (rule 4).
- **The owner decides** (POST `/api/deals/:id/needs-look`): "This is right" remembers the finding's `clearKey`
  (`tracking.sanityCleared`): `listing|BRAND:MODEL|ASIN` for a listing (on every deal), `bundle|<id>|too_good` for an
  estimate. "Wrong listing" withdraws the match the way the catalog check withdraws a contradicted listing (status none, the
  ASIN in `rejectedAsins`, so no later scan chooses it for the part; `decidedBy` stays auto so the part can be matched again
  to another listing). Reset scan data forgets both with the rest of tracking.
- **UI**: an attention tag "Needs a look" and the reason line "Needs a look: <sentence>" on the row; in the inspector, a
  "Needs a look" section above the verdict: each finding's sentence, its links (Home Depot's bundle, Home Depot's page of the
  part sold alone, the Amazon listing), This is right, Wrong listing (only for a finding about one listing).
- **Red-teaming a data set**: `npm run audit:sanity -- <data folder | desktop audit export>` (a table, exit 1 on any hold;
  what the owner marked right is left out unless `--all`). The extension's export holds no deals and is refused.

**Checks adopted**, each measured; "right" and "wrong" from the answer keys (the validation labels of Nov 18 with Home
Depot's sold-alone prices, the audit corpus, `docs/decisions.md` on B0FD253MKS) and the Nov 24 case:

| check | severity | threshold | flags on right answers | flags on wrong ones |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title_other_model`: a counted listing (not one the owner chose) whose title names another model and not the part's | hold | a model-like token: 1 to 6 letters, then at least 3 digits in all, optional "-1" | validation candidates: 0 | validation: 841; the Nov 24 case |
| `title_extra_model`: the title names the part and another model too; not for a kit (DEWALT DCK, RYOBI …K, …K2, PSK, any kit a way splits) | hold | as above | validation: 1 (P119, a charger listing that names what it charges); corpus: 1 (the same) | validation: 137; corpus: 192; after-scan5: B0FD253MKS "PCL700B & PCL702B" (two tools) |
| `price_vs_home_depot` high: Amazon's counted price over Home Depot's price for the part sold alone | hold | more than 3 times | highest right ratio 2.80 (A98401, Home Depot at $9.97) in after-scan5, 1.00 in the validation set (24) | validation: 2 (a two-tool "P326-PCL406B" listing); PCL702B 4.04; the Nov 24 case 9.73 |
| `price_vs_home_depot` low | note | under 0.3 | lowest right ratio 0.39 (validation), 0.41 (after-scan5 DCB205) | validation: 82 (accessories, spare parts). A note only: too low lowers an estimate, a deal missed, never a bad buy |
| `too_good`: a way to sell's estimated ROI, or its priced parts' Amazon total against the bundle's regular price (its price when no regular) | hold | ROI above 100%; total above 1.5 times | highest right ROI 44%, total 1.27 (after-scan5); after-scan3 0.92 | PCL702B deal 107% and 1.79; the Nov 24 case 473% (526% at default fees) and 5.53 |
| `listing_two_models`: one ASIN counted for two models that are not the same model (RYOBI's PCL515 and PCL515B are one) | hold | any | after-scan5: 0 (B0B88R7HWB for PCL515/PCL515B is the twin) | none recorded; caught in the mutation test |
| `pack_counted_as_one`: the title says a pack of N ("2-Pack", "pack of 3", "Double Pack") and the part's model has no "-N" | hold | N of 2 to 50 | corpus: 1 (PBP2003, itself a 2-pack: "This is right" clears it once) | corpus: 42 |

On the stored deals: **after-scan5** (258 deals, 46 confirmed listings, every one's title names its model) holds one deal,
PCL1201K2-PCL702B, on three checks (two-tool listing, 4.04 times Home Depot, ROI 107%); **after-scan3** flags nothing;
after-scan5 rebuilt by today's report flags PCL702B's listing once (`title_extra_model`). The Nov 24 case, rebuilt from
after-scan5 with the owner's later records, is held by three independent checks: title, part price, estimate.

**Checks dropped**:
- A confirmed listing whose title names no model at all: after-scan5 has none, but the corpus's 16 such rows are all
  `uncertain` and Amazon's own listings often omit the model (B0B88R7HWB "ONE+ 18V Cordless Reciprocating Saw …" is PCL515B):
  noise. Only a title that names *another* model is held.
- The parts' Home Depot sold-alone prices against the bundle's regular price: right answers run from 0.53 (a set whose "was"
  price is Home Depot's) to 2.16 (PSK1212SB, a $99 starter kit whose battery alone is $129); no wrong case in the data. Any
  threshold flags right answers or nothing.
- A set item of quantity 2 priced as 1: every set item with a quantity above 1 in after-scan5 waits for its page, so nothing to
  measure; and it would lower an estimate (a deal missed), not raise it.
- A separate "part matched to the whole bundle's listing" check: folded into `title_extra_model`, whose sentence says when the
  other model is a part of this bundle.

**Mutation tests** (`core/test/sanity-mutation.test.ts`): after-scan5's 41 confirmed records that a deal counts, each
corrupted one way at a time in the scan store, the report rebuilt by the app's code, the checks run. Another product's
listing confirmed by part number: 41 of 41 held. Title swapped for another product's: 41 of 41. Quantity doubled (a 2-pack
title at twice the price): 41 of 41. Price times ten: 39 of 41 (38 before the estimate check was changed to count the parts
priced so far, not only complete ways to sell). **What gets through**: a price times ten on PBP002 and A10FB1G, parts with nothing to
compare a price with (no Home Depot sold-alone price, no list price, no eBay price), in kits whose other parts have no price
yet, so no deal has an estimate a buy could rest on. Once one does, only `too_good` stands between such a price and Buy now;
no check the data supports would do better (a battery at $280 is 0.62 of a $448 kit).

**The model's second opinion, a harness only** (`scripts/redteam-listings-cli.ts`, `npm run redteam:listings`): per
confirmed listing, the listing judge's prompt and code check (core/src/listingJudge) over the recorded title and brand, the
disagreements listed for a person. Without `--spend` it reads no key and calls nothing; with it, the estimate is printed
first (after-scan5: 46 listings, about $0.01 on claude-haiku-5-5), `--max-cost` stops it. Not run; the main session decides.

*For the rewrite*: the thresholds rest on 2 wrong cases in the owner's data and the labelled search rows; re-measure them on
every new audit export (`npm run audit:sanity`), and add each real wrong case the owner catches as a fixture and a test.

## Nov 24: fewer questions, each settled only by evidence the app holds or reads free

The owner's Review after a day of slow reading held 24 open questions (fixtures/e2e-2026-11-23/after-scan6). He asked that
the app settle more of them itself. Each group was traced to the line that opened it, and settled only where Home Depot's
or Amazon's own records make the answer certain (CLAUDE.md rule 4). Five rules, each measured before it was adopted
(`npx tsx scripts/measure-fewer-questions.ts`, free, no network: (a) the validation set against its answer key with Nov 18's
readings, each rule alone on top of the adopted ones, plus a probe running the rule on every settled bundle as if open; (b)
the owner's stores after-scan3/5/6, every bundle the rule changes, a resolved change scored against the owner's own answer
or checked by hand against its pages; (c) rule C's kit count against each kit's own title; (d) rule D on every saved listing
of a RYOBI model with a Home Depot pack size, against the validation labels; (e) rule E on every set). The step 15 figures
(`scripts/measure-splits.ts`) are unchanged: validation 24 right, 0 wrong, 7 open; 47 probes, 0 different.

| Rule | Validation: changed / right / wrong / open | Probes (resolved, wrong) | Real stores: what changed | Wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Page names the two halves | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 6, 0; real 84, 0 | DCF860BWCB2460C (scans 5, 6): question → waits for DCB2460C's reading | 0 |
| B. A part Home Depot does not sell alone | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 6, 0; real 92, 0 | PCL1600K2-A10FB1G settled (by hand: Amazon B00P8HJF5G, brand RYOBI, part number A10FB1G; the title says "and 11 in. Flexible Shaft Bit Holder"); PSBCK102K2-A10FB1G and PCL406424SB-A21701-A25RS51 narrower (the page's Includes line names A10FB1G, A25RS51) | 0 |
| C. A part's own page, before the question | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 6, 0; real 84, 0 | PSBCK102K2-A98401: question → waits for PSBCK102K2's and A98401's pages; with PSBCK102K2's page as read on Nov 23 (326415387: "(1) PSBDD02 …, (1) PSBID02 …"), it and PSBCK102K2-A10FB1G settle, each right by its pages | 0 |
| C, its count (kitPageTools) | — | — | 17 kit pages: page and title agree 3, differ 0; items whose words do not say 10 (no count); title states none 4 | 0 |
| D. Home Depot's pack size for a RYOBI model | 1 listing changed, right (labels: B0BG5SPWVL is PBP2003) | — | PBP2003's listing B0BG5SPWVL: review → title match (scans 5, 6; the labelled listing). Validation replay: title matches 79 of 79 right (78 of 78 before) | 0 |
| E. A set holding other brands' items | — | — | 6 sets of after-scan6: question → not estimated | none possible: no estimate is made |

- **Rule A** (`settle.ts pageHalves`, `pageHalvesSplit`): a DEWALT model in no known bundle form that is one whole model, W,
  and a second whole model without its leading D (DCF860B W CB2460C), whose page's leading models are exactly those two and
  whose bullets name no other DEWALT model anywhere. The W form is Home Depot's own for a kit and its bonus item. The reading
  then goes through rule 2's checks unchanged (each part sold under exactly its model, or waiting for its reading).
- **Rule B** (`accessoryOf`): a part rule 2 found "not sold by itself", in a reading the model number spells out, is a part
  when the bundle's own page lists it on an Includes line ("Includes: (1) A10FB1G 11 in. flexible shaft bit holder …"), or
  when Amazon's catalog record of exactly that brand and part number was confirmed by the catalog check on its stored match
  (`catalogConfirmed`, `SettleData.amazonCatalog`; evidence kind `amazon_catalog`). It has no Home Depot title: its
  Includes words stand in, and a part whose words give no tool count is left out of the bundle's tool count, as an owner's
  fact is. The part then has no price yet, the existing "Missing a price" state with its break-even.
- **Rule C** (`partPageDue`, `partPageItems`, `kitPageTools`; reads `partPagesWanted`, in the scan's kit-page pass and the
  drip's `readKitPagesFor`): when the bundle's page names a model no part explains, a part Home Depot confirmed whose own page
  (its reading's item id) is not read yet makes the split wait, not ask; the page is saved on its part-price record
  (`PartPriceRecord.page`), and an item on its Includes list explains the named model as a kit page's does. A page refused or
  unreadable `PART_PAGE_REFUSALS_MAX` (3) times is waited for no longer (`pageRefusals`). Rule 3's kit-half count may come
  from that page when the kit's title states none: each item's tools by its own words, any unknown item making it unknown.
- **Rule D** (`classify.ts hdPackSize`, `wantedFor`; `knownModels` puts `packQty` on the model's existing catalog entry; no
  entry is made, since an entry also splits a hyphen bundle): Home Depot's own page of exactly a RYOBI model says "(2-Pack)",
  so a listing of that pack is graded as the part, and one of another pack size is not. At the agent's start the open "Which
  listing" questions asked only for that reason are judged again on the saved listings (`rejudgePackQuestions`, the scan's
  own re-judge; the price guard's question and the owner's answers are never touched). This is the one place a Home Depot
  part-price reading changes a match: core/test/validation-prices.test.ts says so; the validation results were replayed
  (`npm run validate -- --replay`), PBP2003's lookup rows left them, so the sanity counts on those rows read 804 and 136
  (were 841 and 137), no right listing lost.
- **Rule E** (`split.ts setSplit`, `Decomposition.otherBrands`, section `other_brands`, `BundleRow.otherBrands`,
  `DealDetail.setContents`): a set whose item pages name a brand the app does not price (not DEWALT or RYOBI) is settled as
  Home Depot lists it, every item with its own brand, model and page, and not estimated: "Not estimated: holds other brands'
  items (Husky H52MWC10MOD)", in its own group, never a question, no leftover silently dropped, no pricing for other brands.
  An item with no brand, or of DEWALT or RYOBI in the other's set, still asks.
- **Waiting says what it waits for**: `BundleRow.waitingFor`, "Waiting for Home Depot's pages of PSBCK102K2 and A98401" (the
  row's line, and the desktop row's tag). A part-price read that is refused stays waiting as before (no cap was added there: a
  read that came back not found is a reading, and the question then opens with its reason).

**Replayed on after-scan6** (core/test/fewer-questions.test.ts, at the start with no scan and nothing spent): 24 open → 13.
Settled: PCL1600K2-A10FB1G (PCL1600K2 + A10FB1G); PBP2003 matched to B0BG5SPWVL (a title match, then the catalog check).
Waiting: DCF860BWCB2460C for DCB2460C's reading; PSBCK102K2-A10FB1G for PSBCK102K2's page; PSBCK102K2-A98401 for
PSBCK102K2's and A98401's pages. Not estimated: the six sets (Husky H52MWC10MOD; Everbilt 802464, 800934; Graco 26D362;
Everbilt 802474; STP, FIRM GRIP, HDX, Slime; Husky HD60012-TH, ECOMAX).

**Left as they are, and why:**
- *DCK225D2WCS380B / WCS391B / WCS356B / WCS354B.* Their pages name DCD708B and DCF809B ("Includes 1 DEWALT DCD708B …, two
  20-Volt MAX lithium-ion 1.3 Ah battery packs"); the kit's own page (catalog, item 322138483) names DCD794 and DCF809 with
  2 Ah batteries. DCD708B is not in DCK225D2: the pages contradict each other, and the "kit's own page explains it" rule
  changes none of the four (measured: 0). The siblings WCS369B and WCF850B settled only because their pages write the models
  without the "MODEL:" lead that `pageCatalog` reads ("DCS369B a compact …", "DCD794 - …"), so their page named no model.
- *PCL1600K2-A986501 and PCL1600K2-A965201.* No Includes line names the accessory, and no catalog record of it is stored;
  nothing free would settle them. A Keepa catalog check of a listing naming them would (rule B), but it costs a token and is
  not planned for a part of an unsettled split.
- *The special buys* PCL406416SB, PCL406424SB and the two hyphen bundles holding them (4 questions): the special-buy-as-one-
  kit rule was measured and rejected on PCL500525SB (step 16, round 2). A25RS51 is now settled within its bundle (rule B);
  the question is about the special buy alone.
- *DCKTS681D1P1W82 and W21*: a two-digit suffix no catalog entry fits; W21's page names five models none of which is a kit.
- *DWST08017*: held by the price guard (9.7×) on purpose.
